41. DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.2 — TARGET SELECTION THROUGH A MOD-OWNED TARGET LOCK — 2026-10-15
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
SYMPTOM (same battle, log 18:38-18:40): the SmartTargeting change counter
grew by about 13 per second (1200 changes in 90 s). The game re-selects the
target of every gun group without a manual target every few hundred
milliseconds and overrode the mod's pick; the mod set it back. Every change
reset the aiming progress, so in 1.11.0/1.11.1 SmartTargeting hurt accuracy
instead of improving it.
CHANGE: the mod sets its pick as a "manual" target (Ship.Aim.manual = true),
which the game does not override, and remembers it per gun group. Rules:
- a manual target the mod did not set (the player's order) is never touched;
  when the player changes the target of a group locked by the mod, the mod
  hands that group back to the player;
- when the mod's pick stops making sense (no target in range, no range), the
  lock is released and the group returns to the game's automatic targeting;
- re-evaluation every 2 s, target changed only when the pick differs (aiming
  progress reset only then).
Side effect: on player ships the gun groups driven by the mod look like
groups with a manually assigned target in the UI. Switch off with
SmartTargeting=false (player) / SmartTargetingAi=false (AI).
From the same battle: British battleships (gun range 7.9 km) closed in on the
German ones (14.4 km) and stopped closing at about 7.3 km, as the "outranged"
rule intends; the penetration comparison was still in the wrong units (fixed
in 1.11.1, to be confirmed).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
42. DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.3 — PANIC FROM CREW LOSS, BELT PENETRATION IN THE RIGHT UNITS, PRIZES TAKEN OFF THE SUNK LISTS — 2026-10-15
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
OBSERVATIONS (campaign battle Germany (player) vs Britain, 1.11.2, log
18:44-19:18, 0 exceptions): 52 charges (start 3.1-3.8 km with 850 m
torpedoes), 68 "close in" decisions (British BBs 10.4 km and CAs 7.5-7.9 km
against German 14.4 km, stopping at 0.7x own range), a player scout charge
(CL Undine), 72 torpedo holds / 71 releases, 6 surrenders.
DEFECTS AND CHANGES:
1. PANIC TOO EASY: 17 cases, including torpedo boats with damage 0.03-0.17
   and crew 0.64-0.70. Many AI ships start a battle at 70% crew (the game's
   minimum), i.e. exactly at the PanicCrewRatio threshold, and the formula
   had a base chance even at zero excess. Now: the crew LOSS relative to the
   first battle tick is used (PanicCrewLoss, default 25%), no base chance:
   p = PanicChancePerCheck x excess x (0.25 + 0.75 x "greenness"). The
   PanicCrewRatio key is replaced by PanicCrewLoss. The PANIC entry now shows
   the starting crew and the loss.
2. PENETRATION UNITS: Ship.GetPenetration returns cm, armour is in mm (UI:
   29.1 vs 330 for an 11" gun at 10.7 km). Moreover the bool=false variant
   GREW with distance (25 at 7.6 km, 52 at 16.8 km), i.e. plunging/deck
   fire. Now: bool=true (belt), x10, compared with GetEffectiveArmor(A.Belt).
   The "close in" decisions so far were right mainly thanks to the outranged
   rule, not the penetration rule.
3. TARGET SELECTION: entries "target X instead of X" - the game dropped the
   manual flag and the mod, re-asserting it, reset the aiming progress even
   for the same target. Now the same target only renews the lock, without a
   reset and without counting a change. Counter: 2000 changes in 34 min over
   about 40 ships (1.11.0: 13 per second).
4. PRIZES: the transfer of 6 British TBs to Germany went through (log: new
   owner germany, status Repairing), but the autosave after the battle holds
   no German ship of British origin (player=germany, playerOriginal=britain:
   0 hits). Hypothesis: the ships stayed on the battle's sunk lists
   (DefenderShipsSink / sunkShipsCached), which the loss accounting erases
   regardless of owner, and had a crew near zero (LowCrew status). Now before
   the transfer: removal from both sunk lists and the cache, prize crew raised
   to min_crew_threshold (0.7), training green as before. Added logs "after
   DamageShip" and "after loss accounting" with status, isSunk, HP, owner and
   port of the captured ships. STATUS: to be confirmed in the next battle
   with a surrender; if the ships still vanish, the cause is in
   Ui.DeleteSunkedShips or BattleCleanupTempShips (next step).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
43. LONG-RANGE ACCURACY IN THE RADAR RANGEFINDERS (technologies.csv, accuracy_long) — 2026-10-16
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT: Long-range accuracy in the fire-control technologies
(technologies.csv, accuracy_long): aim_rangefinder_10 (stereo
rangefinder V, 1925) 27.5 -> 45; aim_rangefinder_11/12/13 (radar
rangefinders I/II/III, 1935/1940/1945) 15/20/30 -> 90/160/260.

WHY: W.J. Jurens (navweaps.com, "The Evolution of Battleship Gunnery in
the U.S. Navy, 1920-1945") documents a ~66% drop in range dispersion
(from ~1.1% to ~0.4% of range). For a target smaller than the pattern the
hit probability scales inversely, i.e. x2.5-2.9. Recomputing the whole
mod tree (rangefinders as one chosen component, aim_control and shells
cumulative, assuming percentages add up and multiply the guns.csv base)
gave only x1.08 on accuracy_long and x1.10 on general accuracy between
1920 and 1945; the whole 1920-1945 leap sat in aim (599 -> 1244), i.e.
aiming speed, not the accuracy ceiling. After the change the 1945
accuracy_long sum is +290% against +47.5% in 1920 (x2.6). The change goes
through the rangefinder branch because that is where the progress
actually happened (fire-control radar, stabilisation, fire-control
computers), not through guns.csv - the section 3 attempt (0.21-1.62 in
accuracy_1-5) was reverted because it counted the same progress twice and
broke the narrow range the hit curves are tuned for.

EFFECTS: applies to player and AI. Battles after 1935 become clearly
bloodier at 15-25 km; short range unchanged (accuracy_long works at long
range only). hitChanceCurver (DIP 1.3.5) and params.csv dispersion
unchanged. Full variant (x2.5) adopted; the half variant (x1.8) was rejected. STATUS: not yet battle-tested.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
44. DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.4 — A CONQUERED MINOR'S FLEET PASSES TO THE CONQUEROR, BALANCE-OF-POWER MESSAGE FIXED — 2026-10-16
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT: DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.4.
1. The fleet of a conquered or dissolved minor nation passes to the new
   controller of the port instead of being scrapped (MinorFleetToConqueror,
   on by default). The game calls CampaignController.ScrapShip for every
   ship of the loser from two places: TransferProvinceToNewOwner (province
   lost) and DisableMediumPlayer (nation dissolved). A prefix on ScrapShip
   recognises that context and calls TransferShipToNewOwner instead: on a
   lost province the ships stationed in its ports go to the conqueror; on
   dissolution each ship goes to the controller of the province
   (Province.Controller) where it is stationed. Ships under construction,
   commissioning, sunk or already scrapped are handled as before. Minor
   nations only (Player.isMedium) - the great powers are unchanged. Log:
   "[Conquest] <ship> <nation> -> <recipient> (...)" plus a summary when
   a nation is dissolved.
2. The "emergency naval appropriations" message (balance of power) was
   never visible in-game: Ui.ReportImportant requires a ship and the call
   passed null, which ended in a NullReferenceException inside the game
   (40 log entries on 15 Oct, caught, no effect on the grant itself). The
   nation's first ship is passed now; without a ship the message is
   skipped.

WHY: on 1 Jan 1900 Egypt, after losing Northern Egypt to Spain, scrapped
its whole fleet (2 CA, 2 CL, 3 TB, income 0). Historically the fleet of a
conquered state fell into the victor's hands (the French fleet at Toulon
1793, the Danish at Copenhagen 1807, the Russian at Port Arthur 1905);
scrapping at the moment of conquest has no counterpart. Related: a ship
ordered by a minor that ceases to exist before delivery has nobody to be
delivered to - it stays with the builder (game behaviour, not the mod's).

ASSUMPTIONS: the fourth TransferShipToNewOwner parameter (PortElement) is
taken to set the new home port - the port where the ship is stationed is
passed. Province.Controller is a player name (string); the recipient is
looked up in CampaignData.Players. STATUS: not yet tested in-game - to
be checked at the next conquest of a minor ("[Conquest]" log entries).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
45. DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.5 — FLEET TO THE CONQUEROR FOR THE GREAT POWERS TOO — 2026-10-16
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT: DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.5 - the fleet hand-over to the conqueror (section
44) extended to the great powers. Analysis of the game code
(TransferProvinceToNewOwner): after a province is lost the game fetches
the loser's remaining ports (PortsDB.GetPlayerPorts); if any exist, ships
from the lost ports are moved to a random one of them (SetPortLocation);
if none is left, ScrapShip is called. The rule "flee when there is
somewhere to flee to, fall into the conqueror's hands when there is not"
is therefore half in the game already and the section 44 prefix supplies
the other half. The minor-nation restriction is removed; the same hook is
added on DisablePlayer (dissolution of a great power): every ship goes to
the controller of the province it is stationed in. Ships under
construction unchanged.

WHY: no reason to treat a great power differently - historically it was
the great powers' fleets that passed to the victor (Port Arthur 1905,
Copenhagen 1807), while scuttling (Toulon 1942, Scapa 1919) is a crew
decision the game does not model. Player: losing the last port ends the
campaign anyway. STATUS: not yet tested in-game.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
46. DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.6 — A CAPTURED SHIP BRINGS ITS DESIGN — 2026-10-16
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT: DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.6 - a ship that changes hands (a battle prize,
sections 39/42; a fleet taken over, sections 44/45) brings its design
along. After TransferShipToNewOwner the recipient's design list
(Player.designs) is checked: if it holds no design with the same hull and
tonnage (1% or 50 t tolerance), the ship's design is cloned with
PlayerController.CloneDesign(design, recipient) and attached as
Ship.design. The captured ship can then be repaired and refitted and its
class built further. Player and AI alike, in both directions. Log:
"[Capture]/[Conquest] design of <ship> ... cloned to <nation>" or
"... already has a design".

WHY: without a design in the owner's list the game cannot show the
captured ship properly in the fleet window or plan its refit - hence the
earlier impression that prizes "vanish". Historically captured ships were
commissioned and modernised as a matter of course (Japanese Iwami/ex-Orel
and Sagami/ex-Peresvet after 1905; HMS Canada/ex-Almirante Latorre), so
access to the design follows from the capture.

ASSUMPTION: CloneDesign appends the clone to the recipient's design list
(as the game does for its own clones in the constructor); the design
count before and after is logged to confirm it. STATUS: not yet tested
in-game.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
47. DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.7 — NATIONAL RESEARCH PROFILES FOR PLAYER AND AI (techBonuses.csv) — 2026-10-16
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT: DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.7 - national research profiles for the 14 great
powers, working for the player and the AI. New file Mods/techBonuses.csv
(nation, technology category per the "type" column of technologies.csv,
percent, basis); 118 entries in the -10..+50% range. A postfix on
CampaignController.GetResearchSpeed(Player, Technology) multiplies the
research speed by 1+percent/100 for the player's nation and the
technology's category. The method is used both by the monthly research
update (UpdatePlayerTech) and by the research window (UpdateTechRes), so
the modified value is visible in the interface. Options:
NationalTechBonuses (on), NationalTechScale (1.0 - multiplier on every
percentage). Log: "[Tech] <nation> <category>: research speed a -> b"
once per pair.

WHY: the existing TechMod values in aiPersonalities.csv (sections 9 and
18) are AI research-priority weights (sole user: Player.GetTechPriorities),
not research speed, and do nothing for the player; describing them as
"research speed bonuses" in sections 9/18 was inaccurate. The profiles
follow documented specialisations: Britain - heavy guns (15in/42 Mk I),
Parsons turbines, ASDIC and convoys, signals; penalties for APC shells
(Jutland) and magazine protection. Germany - Krupp armour (KC 1894),
Zeiss stereoscopic rangefinders, survivability (Seydlitz, Derfflinger),
shells; penalties for the high-pressure boilers of the 1930s and the lack
of large hulls 1918-1935. USA - Ford Rangekeeper and Mk 37, superheavy
16in shells, industrial shipbuilding, damage control, high-pressure
steam; penalties for the Mk 14 torpedo and 1942 night tactics. France -
contre-torpilleurs (45 kn), Indret boilers, quadruple turrets; penalties
for the Richelieu 380mm and fire control. Russia - mines (Port Arthur,
Baltic), Novik, Obukhov guns and the B-37; penalties for imported
turbines and building times. Italy - speed (Capitani Romani, Ansaldo),
Zara/Condottieri cruisers; penalties for 381mm dispersion, no radar,
coordination. Austria-Hungary - Whitehead of Fiume (the torpedo), Skoda,
Tegetthoff triple turrets. Japan - Long Lance, 61cm torpedoes,
Takao/Mogami cruisers, night fighting, optics; penalties for damage
control (Taiho, Shinano), ASW, radar. Netherlands - Hazemeyer AA fire
control, Bofors 40mm, light cruisers, Werkspoor. Scandinavia - Bofors
40mm and heavy guns, Swedish steel, coastal defence ships, mines;
penalties for no dreadnoughts and no cruisers. Spain, China, Portugal -
import-dependent fleets: profile balance about 0. Greece (Byzantium) -
profile per the character adopted in section 19 (heavy armour, big
calibres, fast cruisers).

EFFECTS: percentage sums per nation range from 0 (Spain) to +230
(Germany, Japan) - industrial powers research faster in their fields
than import-dependent fleets; intended. STATUS: not yet tested in-game -
to be confirmed by "[Tech]" log entries and the speed shown in the
research window.

48. DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.8 — PENETRATION IN MILLIMETRES, CLOSE-IN HYSTERESIS — 2026-10-16
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
OBSERVATIONS (campaign battle Germany (player) 3 BB vs Scandinavia, 1.11.6,
log 14:31-14:37, 0 exceptions): CA Statsraad Erichsen (229 mm) closed in
on BB Kronprinz from 19.9 km because it was outranged (9992 vs 15836 m);
it stopped at 6.9 km (0.7 of range) and flipped "close-in ended" /
"closes in" five times in a row in the 6.9-7.1 km band. The penetration
check logged "belt pen 95.9 cm vs effective belt 428.8 mm" and treated
the penetration as certain. CL Potentilla: retreat cancelled (6.2 vs
11.1 speed units), torpedo charge from 14.9 km, charge start 3095 m with
850 m torpedoes. TB Alv: charge. 36 target assignments, no panic, no
surrender.

WHAT: DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.8.
1. Penetration units: Ship.GetPenetration(..., belt=true) returns
   millimetres, not centimetres. Evidence: a 229 mm gun of 1901 gave 95.9
   (belt) / 58.5 (deck) at 19.9 km, and penetration.csv for 9" at 20 km
   lists 182.8 / 107.1 mm - the same belt/deck ratio (1.64 vs 1.71) and
   a gun-generation multiplier of about 0.53; read as cm (x10 from
   section 42) it would be 959 mm, five times the table, and the "cannot
   penetrate" branch never fired. The x10 is removed; the log reads mm.
   Effect: cruisers and old battleships that cannot penetrate the
   target's belt will now actually close in (section 39, item 2).
2. Close-in hysteresis: once a close-in has ended (0.7 of range) it
   restarts only beyond 0.85 of own range instead of flipping every tick
   at the boundary.

STATUS: 1 - verified against data (penetration.csv), not yet
battle-tested; 2 - not yet tested.

49. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.0 — PACKAGE NUMBERING, DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.9: UPDATE CHECK — 2026-10-16
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT:
1. Package numbering. From this version every publication carries its own
   Iron Admiral X.Y.Z number (VERSION file in the repository: package
   version, DIP_GG_Fixes version, date), the zips are named
   Iron_Admiral_X.Y.Z_Nexus.zip / _Mods_only.zip, and each publication
   is a separate GitHub release vX.Y.Z marked latest. The release script
   bumps the patch number automatically and stamps it into README, the
   Steam/Nexus/feature descriptions and the installer; Mods receives
   IRON_ADMIRAL_VERSION.txt with the installed package number. First
   package numbered this way: 1.2.0 (DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.9). Release v1.1
   stays as an archive.
2. DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.9 - update check. At game start the add-on reads
   Mods/IRON_ADMIRAL_VERSION.txt and fetches the VERSION file of the
   repository's main branch in the background (one HTTPS request to
   raw.githubusercontent.com, 8 s limit, nothing is sent). When the
   remote version is newer, about 15 s after start (once the UI exists)
   it shows a Ui.ReportMessage box with the new version number and
   github.com/KlaudiuszLedwoch/Iron-Admiral/releases; the result always
   goes to the log ("[Update] installed ... latest ..."). Switch:
   CheckForUpdates in MelonPreferences. No network = one log line, no
   effect.

WHY: the package is published several times a day; a shared 1.1 number
and overwritten zips made it impossible to tell what anyone had
installed or to point at a specific release. The in-game notice replaces
checking the page by hand.

STATUS: numbering - in effect with this release; notice - to be
confirmed at the next publication (installed 1.2.0 vs a newer one on
GitHub).

51. DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.11 — IN-GAME UPDATE AFTER THE PLAYER CONFIRMS — 2026-10-16
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT: DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.11 - in-game update after the player's
confirmation. The newer-version message (section 49) now has "Download /
Pobierz" and "Later / Pozniej" buttons. By default only the message is
shown; the download starts solely after the click. On click the add-on
fetches Iron_Admiral_X.Y.Z_Mods_only.zip from the GitHub release (URL
and SHA-256 from the VERSION file in the repository, lines
"url_mods_only" and "sha256_mods_only" written by the release script),
verifies the checksum (on mismatch nothing is installed), extracts to a
temporary folder and copies the Mods\ content into the game. The loaded
DIP_GG_Fixes.dll cannot be overwritten but can be renamed: old ->
DIP_GG_Fixes.dll.old, new under the proper name; the .old is deleted at
the next start. Finally a message "X.Y.Z installed, restart the game" or
"update failed" with the manual download link. Everything in the log as
"[Update]". Options: CheckForUpdates (message), UpdateDownloadButton
(button; false = link only). The release script now writes VERSION after
building the packages (so it can carry their checksum), and
Mods\IRON_ADMIRAL_VERSION.txt (installed version) has three lines:
package version, DIP_GG_Fixes version, date.

WHY: the package is published several times a day; manual download and
extraction discourages. Rule: nothing is downloaded without the player's
click, only from the Iron Admiral repository, with checksum
verification. Writing to the game folder needs the permissions the
installer and the game itself have; on failure an error message and the
link.

STATUS: not yet tested - first test at the 1.2.3 publication with 1.2.2
installed.

63. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.14 — ZIP PACKAGES WITH STANDARD PATHS, .NET 6 CHECK IN THE INSTALLER — 2026-10-18
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
PLAYER REPORTS (Steam, 17-18 October):
- "I downloaded the mods only zip, and the file structure is wrong. All the
  files are in one place and melon loader won't load it. The nexus one is
  the same."
- "...it giving me a 6.0 .net runtime problem."
CAUSE:
- The release script packed both zips with Compress-Archive of Windows
  PowerShell 5.1, which writes entry names with backslashes
  ("Mods\accuracies.csv"). The ZIP specification (APPNOTE 4.4.17) requires
  forward slashes; several unpackers (Linux/Proton, macOS, some Windows
  tools) treat the backslash as part of the file name and extract 150 loose
  files named "Mods\..." into one folder - MelonLoader then finds no mod.
  Checked on the 1.2.13 zips: first entries "Mods\accuracies.csv",
  "Iron_Admiral_1.2.13_Nexus\payload\".
- MelonLoader 0.6.x runs on .NET 6 (requirement: .NET 6.0 Desktop Runtime,
  LavaGang release page). The installer did not check it.
WHAT:
- release.ps1 writes the zips with System.IO.Compression and forward-slash
  entry names (directories as "name/" entries). Test on the author's PC:
  "t/Mods/", "t/Mods/Sub/", "t/Mods/a.csv", "t/Mods/Sub/b.txt".
- installer\Install.ps1: checks
  %ProgramFiles%\dotnet\shared\Microsoft.NETCore.App\6.*; when missing it
  offers "winget install Microsoft.DotNet.DesktopRuntime.6" or prints the
  link https://dotnet.microsoft.com/download/dotnet/6.0.
- README_EN/PL: TROUBLESHOOTING section (.NET 6 error, Mods_only layout,
  returning to the unmodded game, where the log is); README.md and the
  Steam/Nexus descriptions: the same notes under Download.
- DIP_GG_Fixes.dll unchanged (1.11.21).
STATUS: zips rebuilt and published as Iron Admiral 1.2.14.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
64. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.15 — ONE-FILE SETUP SCRIPT, MANUAL MELONLOADER GUIDE — 2026-10-18
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: players with the Mods_only package or a failed installer did not know
how to set up MelonLoader and its .NET 6 runtime (Steam thread, 17-18
October); the author had the same trouble on a clean machine.
WHAT:
- Install-IronAdmiral.ps1 in the repository root (and in the Nexus zip), one
  file to paste into a forum post: self-elevates; finds the game in the Steam
  libraries (or takes -GamePath / asks); stops while the game runs; installs
  the .NET 6 Desktop Runtime and the VC++ Redistributable x64 with winget
  (without winget it prints the links); downloads the official MelonLoader
  0.6.6 x64 zip and checks its SHA-256 (687b8260...3c0db); reads the VERSION
  file from GitHub and downloads the current Mods_only package, checked
  against the sha256_mods_only line; moves the old Mods folder to
  Mods_backup_<date> and copies only the Mods folder from the package.
  Checked on the author's PC: script parses, VERSION read, 1.2.14 package
  downloaded with a matching checksum, Mods\TweaksAndFixes.dll in place
  after extraction.
- README_EN/PL and README.md: sections ONE-FILE SETUP SCRIPT and INSTALLING
  MELONLOADER BY HAND (links: dotnet.microsoft.com/download/dotnet/6.0,
  learn.microsoft.com VC++ redistributable page, LavaGang MelonLoader 0.6.6
  release); Steam/Nexus descriptions: the same guide under Download, a block
  with the recent highlights and a short "in testing" note.
- DIP_GG_Fixes.dll unchanged (1.11.21).
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.15.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
65. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.16 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.31) — SERIES PRODUCTION, INFLATION AND CREDIT, TREASURY PANEL, IN-GAME HELP, CAMPAIGN GUIDE — 2026-10-18
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: in the base game every ship of a class costs the same and takes the
same time, and money has no time value: loans, printed money, reparations
and unspent cash have no effect on prices. Historically the first ship of a
class was the expensive one (design changes, new tooling), follow-on ships
became cheaper and faster along a learning curve (Wright 1936; navy
programmes such as the British "Town" class and the US Fletcher-class
destroyers built dozens of hulls in parallel), idle yards bid low and full
yards charged more. Large borrowing and money printing drove inflation,
lenders priced in a country's credit and inflation eroded debt.
WHAT - series production (all nations, construction and refits):
- Prototype (first ship of a design): cost x1.15, time x1.10.
- Series law only for ships laid down after a ship of the design was
  completed; sisters ordered while the prototype is still on the slip are
  built at base cost and time. The laying-down month and whether the
  prototype was already in service are recorded when the order is placed
  (the game's own ship date is the design date, not the laying-down date);
  ships laid down before this version get the month estimated from their
  building progress. The record is kept with the economy state.
- Learning curve: cost 94% per doubling of the series (floor x0.80), time
  90% per doubling, building time never below 2 months.
- Batch: two or more ships of a design laid down within 6 months: cost -5%.
- Common dock: ships of one design laid down together that fit side by
  side into the largest dock: time x m^log2(0.8) (m = ships fitting
  together). The prototype gets neither batch nor dock bonus.
- Shipyard occupancy below 30%: cost x0.90, time x0.95; above 90%: cost
  x1.10, time x1.10.
- Sister ships in service: maintenance -3% each, at most -15%.
- 36 months without a new ship of the design: the next one is a prototype.
- Example (author's campaign, 1910-1911): destroyer V-1, base 7 months;
  29 sisters ordered together after V-1 was commissioned: time x0.19-0.29
  (all limited to 2 months), cost x0.68-0.80.
WHAT - inflation and credit (great powers):
- Inflation impulse relative to the economy (GDP = state budget): 0.4
  points per 1% of annual GDP of extraordinary inflow (loans, grants,
  reparations, loot, sales), 1.2 points for printed money; spending
  changes act symmetrically (0.4 points per 1% of GDP, monthly change).
- Inflation raises all expenses of the nation; it returns to a 1% baseline
  (6% of the distance per month); research slows inflation only down to the
  baseline (diminishing returns), so deflation comes only from large
  spending cuts; range -15%...300%.
- Loan interest = inflation + spread 2-5 points by credit rating (debt /
  GDP, worst at 50%; +0.5 deficit, +0.5 war; 15 years after a bankruptcy
  the worst spread), +0.1 point per inflation point above 15%, floor 0.5%.
  Ratings AAA...CCC. Inflation erodes the loan balance and the overdraft.
- Unspent cash earns inflation minus 1.5 points a year (never below 0).
  The first test build paid 150% of inflation; with printed money and 51%
  inflation that meant about 77% a year on the printed cash.
- Research and shipyard expansion add GDP growth (diminishing returns /
  relative to the expansion size); inflation above 10% and sharp jumps add
  unrest.
- The treasury crisis (war loan, printing, cuts) and balance-of-power
  grants feed the same model; economy state saved per save game in
  UserData\IronAdmiral_Economy (the 1.11.21 treasury fields are taken over).
WHAT - information in the game:
- Treasury panel on the campaign map (F8): inflation and causes, prices,
  credit rating and interest, loan, savings, growth, ships under
  construction with series factors, table of the great powers.
- Finances window: inflation and inflation losses (the row hidden by DIP is
  switched back on); monthly treasury report after each turn (OK / no more
  reports); warnings for important events.
- Map detection by the world-map flags (the game state reported MainMenu
  on the campaign map, which also blocked the player's treasury-crisis
  dialog).
- In-game Help: chapter "Iron Admiral - new mechanics" (help.csv + English
  and German .lng), an outline without formulas.
- GUIDE_EN.md: campaign guide with examples from the test campaign and
  player reports, updated with every version.
- All settings in UserData\MelonPreferences.cfg [DIP_GG_Fixes].
CHECKED in game (DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.24-1.11.31): series logs for AI and
player, prototype rule, occupancy penalties (Byzantine 93%, Scandinavia
95-118%), economy state saved and loaded, panel, Finances window, help.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.16.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
66. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.17 — WHAT INFLATION MEANS: SURCHARGE, NOT A PRICE LEVEL (DOCUMENTATION) — 2026-10-18
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: in the test campaign Britain's budget (GDP) rose from 30.2 to 39.3 bn
in two turns while its inflation fell from 33% to 29%. The question was
whether GDP grows with inflation. It does not: the mod treats inflation as
a surcharge at the current yearly rate on all expenses; prices do not
accumulate into a permanent price level, and the state budget is not
inflated (every figure in today's money, a "real" model). A nominal price
index (prices and budgets growing together for good) was considered and
rejected: it would push ship prices and budgets out of the scale the game
is built for after a few years of high inflation, with no gain in play.
The swing in Britain's budget comes from the game itself (war, peace,
recovery after the crisis).
WHAT: explanation added to the in-game Help (chapter "Inflation, credit and
growth", English and German .lng) and to GUIDE_EN.md; descriptions updated.
DIP_GG_Fixes.dll unchanged (1.11.31).
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.17.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
67. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.18 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.32) — AI SHIPYARD EXPANSION ONLY ON A STEADY OR LARGE SURPLUS — 2026-10-18
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: the rule from section 60 (no expansion with a deficit) checked only
the current month. In the test campaign (October 1911) Britain had one
positive month (+86M after its research budget was cut), started a
shipyard expansion at once (about 370M a month) and was 310M a month in
deficit the next turn, with cash falling by about 300M per turn. The USA
earlier paid 317M a month for an expansion on a 422M monthly budget.
WHAT: an AI great power starts a shipyard expansion only when
- it has had a surplus for 6 months in a row (AiShipyardSurplusMonths) and
  enough cash to cover the expected shortfall for the whole expansion
  (expected monthly cost minus surplus, times its length), or
- its monthly surplus covers 1.25 x the expected monthly expansion cost
  (AiShipyardCostCover).
The expected cost and length are remembered from the nation's last
expansion; before one is seen, half the monthly budget is assumed. The old
limits stay (no expansion with a deficit or with capacity above 2 x the
fleet). The surplus counter is not saved: after loading, the first 6
months allow an expansion only through the large-surplus rule. The log
shows the surplus, months in a row, expected cost and missing cash when an
expansion is refused. Player not affected.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.18.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
68. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.19 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.36) — REFITS ORDERED TOGETHER, NO SHIP PARTS ON THE MAP, SERIES GAP BY COMPLETION DATE, WAR DIAGNOSTICS — 2026-10-18
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY / WHAT (all from the author's test campaign, October-November 1911):
- Refits ordered in the same month all counted as the prototype (three
  battleships of one refit design: each x1.15 cost, x1.10 time), because
  ships with equal progress were never "earlier" than each other. Equal
  progress is now ordered as for new construction: one prototype, the rest
  at base cost and time (confirmed in game: refits of 3-5 battleships per
  design, one #1, the others #2-#5).
- After the refit screen, turrets, barrels and other ship parts appeared
  on the world map until the game was restarted. While the ship designer or
  the refit screen is open the mod no longer calls the game's building-time
  function for other ships (it uses its own laying-down record or the ship
  date instead).
- The rule "36 months without a new ship of the design: the next one is a
  prototype" measured the gap from the design date of completed sisters, so
  long-running classes restarted as prototypes (Japan: torpedo boat of the
  Tsubame class with 15 sisters in service charged x1.15; France: battleship
  of the Charles Martel class). The gap is now measured from the completion
  of the last sister.
- War diagnostics: after every turn the log lists for each great power its
  attitude towards the player, war / alliance / almost war / high tension /
  peace, nation and fleet power relative to the player, cash, income and
  the number of wars it fights - to find out why AI powers with attitude
  near -100 do not declare war.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.19.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
69. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.20 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.38) — COALITION WARS AGAINST A DOMINANT POWER, NO AI DESIGNS WITHOUT A WAY TO BUILD — 2026-10-18
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: in the test campaign (1913-1914) Germany had attitude -99 with almost
every great power and no one declared war. The war diagnostics (section 68)
showed why: each hostile power alone had at most half of the German fleet
(USA x0.42-0.51, Britain x0.33-0.52, Japan x0.34, Italy x0.25) and the game
keeps relations at -99 until a nation judges a war worth it; together they
had about 1.1-1.6x. Historically the dominant naval power faced coalitions
(Britain's balance-of-power policy, the Entente against Germany).
WHAT - coalition wars (every nation, player and AI alike):
- Hostile powers of a target: AI great powers in "almost war" with
  attitude at or below -90 (CoalitionAttitude), plus those already at war
  with it.
- The target must be dominant: its fleet power is greater than that of
  every single hostile power, and the hostile powers together reach 1.5x
  its fleet power (CoalitionPowerRatio).
- When nobody fights the target yet, the strongest eligible member
  declares war with a 25% chance per turn (CoalitionStartChance); once a
  member is at war, every other eligible member joins with 50% per turn
  (CoalitionJoinChance).
- Eligible: AI only (the player decides his own wars), cash not below zero,
  no bankruptcy mark, fewer than 3 wars (CoalitionMaxWars).
- The war is started through the game's attitude change; when the game
  keeps the relation at -99 the attitude is set to -100 directly. Confirmed
  in game: the game and TweaksAndFixes treat such wars as wars (war checks,
  peace talks).
- The first 1.11.37 build lacked the dominance test and piled AI powers on
  the weak Netherlands (coalition x140-x207 of its fleet); 1.11.38 adds it.
WHAT - AI ship designs: an AI nation skips generating new designs in a
turn when it has no ports, cash below zero, no shipbuilding capacity or
full shipyards (AiDesignOnlyWhenBuildable); designs at campaign start are
not affected. Log: "[Design] <nation>: no new ship designs - <reason>"
(confirmed: Scandinavia, 84,469 of 73,249 t under construction).
Log: "[War] coalition against <target> ready (...)" and "[War] <nation>
leads / joins ... war: True".
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.20.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
70. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.21 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.40) — REFITS OF SHIPS ABOVE TREATY LIMITS, REACHABLE TORPEDO CHARGES, NO HOPELESS CLOSE-IN, EVASIVE ZIGZAG — 2026-10-18
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY - refits: in the test campaign (Germany, 1916) battleships built before
the Washington Treaty limits were above the treaty tonnage and could not be
refitted at all: the refit design was checked against the treaty limit like
a new ship. The treaty itself (1922, Chapter II Part 3 Section I(d))
allowed existing capital ships to add up to 3,000 t for anti-aircraft,
anti-torpedo (bulges) and deck protection.
WHAT - refits: for a refit design the tonnage limit is raised to the ship's
own tonnage before the refit plus 3,000 t (RefitTreatyAllowanceTons), never
above the hull's own maximum; new ships keep the treaty limit. Log:
"[Refit] <ship>: refit tonnage limit X t -> Y t". 0 = vanilla.
WHY - battle AI (battle of February 1916, 3 German battleships against 40
US ships, log analysis):
- 20 torpedo charges, several of them impossible: CL Omaha charged from
  14.6 km with 1.6 km torpedoes at 4.3 against 12.1 speed units; DD Tolman
  from 8.7 km with 1.7 km torpedoes. A division that could not escape went
  straight into a charge without checking whether it could reach the target.
- 19 close-ins by cruisers and a battleship with 76-466 mm penetration
  against a 634 mm belt; every close-in ended "still cannot penetrate,
  still outranged" - the ships only sailed into the fire.
WHAT - battle AI:
- Charge reach: a division that cannot escape starts a torpedo charge only
  when the target is within its charge start distance x1.5
  (ChargeReachFactor) or when it is faster than the target; otherwise it
  fights with guns. Log: "charge on <target> not feasible (...)".
- No hopeless close-in: before closing in, penetration is checked at the
  closest planned range (own gun range x CloseInStopRangeFactor); when the
  belt could not be penetrated even there, the ship does not close in
  (CloseInOnlyIfPenetrates). Log: "cannot penetrate <target> even at X m".
- Evasive zigzag (EvasiveManeuvers): during a torpedo charge (until 1.3x
  torpedo launch range) and during a close-in while out of own gun range or
  without penetration, AI divisions alternate their course by +-30 degrees
  (EvasiveAngle) in legs of about 25 s, randomised +-30%
  (EvasiveLegSeconds). The game's accuracy model penalises fire at a
  turning target (params.csv acc_turning_target -0.5 at 2.5 deg/s) but
  penalises the turning ship's own fire even more (acc_turning_self -0.8),
  so the zigzag is used only while the ship's own guns are not effective.
  The final approach to torpedo launch range stays straight. Log: "evasive
  zigzag +-30 deg while charging / closing in".
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.21; in-game confirmation of the refit
limit and of the battle changes pending.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
71. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.22 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.41) — IN-GAME SETTINGS PANEL — 2026-10-19
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: players asked for the battle AI, crews and prizes without the
economic layer. Every mechanic could already be switched off, but only by
editing UserData/MelonPreferences.cfg, which many players never open.
WHAT: a settings panel inside the game, opened and closed with F10
(SettingsPanelKey) or Esc, in the main menu, on the campaign map and in
battle:
- every on/off option of the mod (38 at this version) in groups: Battle AI,
  Crews and prizes, Campaign AI, Economy, Diplomacy, Information and
  updates; each option with its ON/OFF switch and its description;
- a click changes the value at once and saves MelonPreferences.cfg; battle
  options act from the next AI decision, campaign options from the next
  turn; the update check options act after a game restart (marked);
- "All in this group: defaults" restores the defaults of the open group;
- options are read from the preferences category itself, so new options
  appear in the panel automatically (unknown ones under "Other");
- the game's own UI input is paused while the panel is open, so clicks do
  not fall through to the map or the menus; drawing uses the same plain
  IMGUI boxes and labels as the treasury panel, clicks are hit-tested by
  the mod.
Numeric fine tuning (thresholds, chances, multipliers) stays in the
configuration file. In-game Help: the settings entry now points to F10.
Log: "[Settings] panel opened / closed", "[Settings] <option> = on/off -
saved".
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.22; in-game confirmation pending.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
72. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.23 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.49) — MASSED TORPEDO ATTACK, RAMMING, HOPELESS BATTLESHIPS RETREAT, ESCAPE MISSIONS, TORPEDO TURN-AWAY, HISTORICAL GUNNERY DATA — 2026-10-19
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY - battle AI: a single destroyer or light cruiser that cannot reach
torpedo range alive is right not to charge (section 70), but a whole
flotilla attacking together is a different matter: the enemy cannot sink
every ship before some of them reach launch range - the reasoning behind
massed destroyer attacks in the First World War. The same logic was missing
for the other end of the scale: an AI battleship that could not penetrate
its opponent even at close range simply sat in the battle, and a task force
whose task was to get away fought to the last ship.
WHAT - battle AI (AI only, player ships are not affected):
- Massed torpedo attack (FleetTorpedoAttack): while the enemy has more
  battleships and battlecruisers afloat than the AI side, every AI torpedo
  division charges the enemy capital ships at once, whatever the distance;
  the charge still breaks off on heavy damage or when the torpedoes are
  spent. Log: "massed torpedo attack ON for <nation>: enemy battleships/
  battlecruisers X vs own Y", "<ship>: massed torpedo attack - charges ...".
  Seen in the battle of 19 October 1916 (4 German battleships against 9 US
  light cruisers): all US cruisers went in, two were taken as prizes.
- Ramming (Ramming, RamRange 2000 m): an AI light ship with no torpedoes
  left that cannot escape, or takes part in a massed attack, steers at the
  nearest visible enemy ship within ramming range, aiming a little ahead of
  it. Log: "<ship> rams <target> at X m".
- Hopeless battleships retreat (CapitalEscapeWhenHopeless): an AI
  battleship or battlecruiser that cannot penetrate its target even at the
  closest planned range (section 70) retreats when it can survive the
  battle - it is faster than every visible enemy, or no visible enemy can
  penetrate its own belt. A retreat on armour alone is not cancelled by the
  "cannot outrun - fight" rule. Log: "cannot hurt <target> and can survive
  the battle (...) - retreats to save the ship".
- Escape missions (EscapeMissionLogic, EscapeBattleTypes =
  escape_taskforce): in battle types where the defending side's task is to
  get away there is no massed attack; a division that is faster than its
  pursuers retreats, a division that cannot outrun them splits into single
  ships that flee on different bearings (fanned out up to 75 degrees from
  the direction away from the enemy), so that part of the force escapes -
  as a commander would scatter a force that cannot outrun its pursuers.
  This rule takes precedence over the other AI rules. Log: "escape mission
  - ... retreats instead of attacking", "... the division scatters".
  Prompted by the battle of 19 October 1916: in a "Task Force fails to
  escape" battle the whole US cruiser force charged from more than 30 km.
- Turning away from torpedoes (TorpedoTurnAway, TorpedoTurnAwayRange
  4000 m, TorpedoTurnAwaySeconds 45 s): an AI division that is not itself
  charging and sights enemy torpedo tracks that would pass within 500 m of
  one of its ships turns to run parallel to them (combing the tracks, the
  standard counter used by the Grand Fleet at Jutland). Log: "N enemy
  torpedo track(s) sighted ... division turns away".
- Settings panel (section 71): opening it no longer disables the game's
  event system; the TweaksAndFixes campaign camera reads it every frame and
  threw an error per frame while the panel was open.
- Log: a massed charge reads "massed attack" instead of a meaningless
  start distance.
WHY - data: comparison with the NavWeaps gun data and with Okun's ricochet
tables.
WHAT - data:
- SAPC shells (technologies.csv projectile_shell_ia_sapc, components.csv
  ap_6, 1925): semi-armour-piercing capped shells - more bursting charge and
  damage (ap_damage +50, ap_fire +12), less penetration (-20), ricochet
  angle modifiers 10 / 25 and a shorter fuze (-0.5); names in
  English.lng and German.lng.
- Rates of fire (guns.csv firerate by grade), after NavWeaps: 6" grade 1
  4 -> 5; 8" grades 4-5 5 / 5.5 -> 4; 11" grades 2-4 2.07 / 2.45 / 2.85 -> 3
  (German 28 cm SK L/45 and L/50 of 1907-1913: 3 rounds/minute nominal);
  12" grade 5 3.35 -> 3.0; 14" grades 4-5 2.1 / 2.5 -> 2.0; 16" grades 3-4
  1.72 / 1.95 -> 1.5.
- Ricochet (params.csv): ricochet_angle_from 30 -> 45, ricochet_angle_to
  50 -> 80, ricochet_chance 55 -> 100 - after Okun's model (M79): uncapped
  shells enter the ricochet region above 45 degrees and always ricochet
  above 80 degrees.
- Destroyer secondary guns (shipTypes.csv dd): secondary calibre range 1-2"
  (was 2-2) and ai_max_caliber_sec(2) (was 0), so AI destroyer designs carry
  light secondary guns.
WHAT - diagnostics: armour diagnostics in the log (ArmorDiagnostics, log
only): the game's penetration functions return the same value at every
target aspect (0-90 degrees), so aspect does not reduce penetration; deck
hits do occur (side-hit chance 81% at 0-15 degrees falling to 26-39% at
45-75 degrees in the first battle measured); per-battle summary "[Armor] battle summary". The hit-type
counter also counts interface calls and is not a count of real hits.
WHAT - save compatibility check: built in 1.11.42 and withdrawn in 1.11.46.
Its hooks on the loading of every ship record (Ship.FromStore,
Player.FromStore) made every ship record fail to load, even when nothing
was changed; the tool stays switched off until it is rebuilt on another
hook. Saves themselves were not affected once it was removed.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.23; massed attack confirmed in game,
ramming, battleship retreat, escape-mission logic and the torpedo turn-away
pending confirmation in battle.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
73. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.24 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.50) — AI TACTICS BY SHIP SIZE, SURRENDERED SHIPS TO THE BATTLE WINNER, SERIES PRODUCTION FIXES — 2026-10-19
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY - battle AI: the battle of 19 October 1916 (Coastal Raid, 1 German
armoured cruiser and 1 destroyer against 1 Scandinavian armoured cruiser,
4 light cruisers and a transport) showed that the massed attack, the torpedo
targets and the retreat of a ship that cannot hurt its enemy all depended on
battleships being present. Without battleships on either side the rules did
nothing, although the same reasoning applies to any fleet.
WHAT - battle AI (AI only):
- Massed torpedo attack (FleetTorpedoAttack): switches on when the enemy's
  combat tonnage in the battle (transports excluded) exceeds the AI side's
  own (MassAttackStrengthRatio 1.0), instead of counting battleships and
  battlecruisers. Log: "massed torpedo attack ON for <nation>: enemy combat
  tonnage X t vs own Y t".
- Torpedo targets: torpedo craft charge capital ships or any enemy at least
  1.5 times larger than themselves (TorpedoTargetSizeRatio) - destroyers
  attack light cruisers, light cruisers attack transports.
- Retreat of a ship that cannot hurt its enemy (CapitalEscapeWhenHopeless):
  applies to the main ships of a side - battleships and battlecruisers, and
  any other non-light ship of at least 75% of the tonnage of the largest own
  ship in the battle (MainShipSizeRatio). Without battleships the armoured
  cruisers take that role.
WHY - prizes: a surrendered ship was always given to the opposing side, even
when its own side won the battle; in the same battle a surrendered German
destroyer was lost although the Scandinavian force was destroyed.
WHAT - prizes (SurrenderedShipsToBattleWinner): surrendered ships go to the
side that won the battle (the game's battle winner, otherwise the side with
more victory points). A ship whose own side won returns to its owner and
goes to repair with the same damage as a prize and a minimum crew, crew
training kept. A ship whose side lost is captured as before. A drawn battle
keeps the previous rule. Log: "[Capture] ... surrendered but <nation> won the
battle (...) - ship returns to its owner".
WHY - series production: 13 cruisers of one class ordered in one session
were each logged as the second ship of the series with no batch or dock
effect, and 6 of them had been scrapped before completion.
WHAT - series production:
- A ship scrapped or sunk before completion no longer counts towards the
  series (learning curve, batch, common dock).
- The series log line of a ship is written again whenever its series
  number, batch, common dock or shipyard-occupancy band changes; orders
  placed during one session are counted together at the end of the turn.
- Confirmed in game: AI navies ordering in the same turn get the learning
  curve, batch discount and common-dock time (e.g. 7 destroyers in one
  dock).
CONFIRMED in game (section 72): turning away from torpedoes (Scandinavian
armoured cruiser, track passing 57 m and 231 m away); slow ships leaving
the division and fighting when they cannot outrun the enemy; charges
aborted on damage.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.24; size-based tactics, return of
surrendered ships and the series fixes pending confirmation in game.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
74. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.25 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.51) — MASSED ATTACK ONLY WITH ENOUGH TORPEDO CRAFT, WITHDRAWAL WHEN OUTMATCHED — 2026-10-19
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY - battle AI: in the battle of 19 October 1916 (Port Strike, 3 German
battleships against 1 Scandinavian armoured cruiser, 1 destroyer and 2
torpedo boats) the massed attack switched on because the German force was
far stronger, and the Scandinavian destroyer and torpedo boats charged from
25-28 km. A massed attack works because part of a large flotilla gets
through; 3 small ships against 3 battleships cannot. The armoured cruiser,
which could not penetrate the German belt, had no way to withdraw, and
nobody tried to leave a battle that was lost from the start.
WHAT - battle AI (AI only):
- Massed torpedo attack (FleetTorpedoAttack): besides an enemy stronger in
  combat tonnage, the side now needs at least 3 torpedo craft per enemy
  target (MassAttackPerTarget); targets are capital ships and ships at least
  1.5 times larger than its smallest torpedo craft (TorpedoTargetSizeRatio).
  The count of ships decides, not tonnage. Log: "massed torpedo attack ON/off
  for <nation>: ..., N torpedo craft vs M targets (needed 3 per target)".
- Withdrawal when outmatched (WithdrawWhenOutmatched): when the enemy is
  stronger and there are too few torpedo craft for a massed attack, every AI
  division that is faster than all visible enemies, or that no visible enemy
  can penetrate, withdraws. Slower divisions keep fighting (torpedo charges
  by range and speed, ramming when out of torpedoes). Log: "enemy force too
  strong and too few torpedo craft for a massed attack - withdraws (...)".
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.25; pending confirmation in battle.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
75. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.26 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.52) — SERIES PRODUCTION RIGHT AFTER LOADING A CAMPAIGN — 2026-10-19
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: the log of 19 October 2026 (16:19) showed the 7 Kormoran-class cruisers
of a running series as "prototype not finished yet - base cost and time"
for the first seconds after the campaign was loaded, and with the correct
series effects (batch, 7 in one dock) 4 seconds later. The series was
computed before the saved laying-down records of the campaign were read, so
the laying-down month was estimated from the building progress and fell
before the prototype's completion.
WHAT: series effects are computed only after the saved economy state of the
campaign (with the laying-down records) has been read; until then the game's
own cost and time apply, and nothing is cached or logged.
CONFIRMED in game (section 73): 7 cruisers of one class ordered together
after the prototype entered service - batch discount, 7 in one dock, cost
x0.79-0.89, time x0.39-0.48 depending on the place in the series; escape
mission logic: every ship of a Scandinavian task force retreated instead of
attacking.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.26; pending confirmation after the
next campaign load.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
76. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.27 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.53) — REFIT ABOVE THE TREATY LIMIT CAN BE SAVED — 2026-10-19
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: in the campaign of 19 October 2026 the refit of a 55,690 t German
battleship (Hildebrand class) could not be saved under a 35,000 t treaty
limit. Section 70 raised the ship's maximum tonnage for the refit (the log
showed 55,690 t -> 58,690 t), but the save check of the refit screen
compares the design with the player's treaty tonnage limit for the ship
type (Player.TonnageLimit), which was left at 35,000 t.
WHAT: on the refit screen the player's treaty limit for the type of the
ship being refitted is raised to the same value as the ship's maximum -
its own tonnage plus the modernisation allowance (RefitTreatyAllowanceTons,
3,000 t), never above the hull maximum. Only while a refit design is open in
the designer; new designs keep the treaty limit. Log: "[Refit] <ship>:
treaty limit for bb 35000 t -> 58690 t for this refit".
CONFIRMED in game (sections 74-75): escape mission - a Scandinavian task
force retreated while no pursuer was in range, then each ship fled alone on
a different bearing (-50, +75 and 0 degrees) when the German battleships
closed in; series production after loading a campaign - the first series
entry came after the economy state was read and was correct at once (batch,
7 in one dock).
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.27; refit save pending confirmation
in game.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
77. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.28 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.54) — REFIT ABOVE THE TREATY LIMIT: THE DESIGN OPEN IN THE DESIGNER — 2026-10-19
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: with 1.11.53 the refit of the 55,690 t battleship still could not be
saved. The log (18:14) showed the raised limit for the refit design that was
opened first ("treaty limit for bb 35000 t -> 58690 t"), then the game closed
and reopened the designer with a new refit design ("Hildebrand (Feb.
1918)"); the save check of that design ran with the 35,000 t limit ("Design
invalid: Can not build: Above displacement limit for BB: 35,000 t"). The
mod only knew the refit design it had seen before, and any other ship
checked in the background cleared it.
WHAT: the raised treaty limit is applied to the ship currently open in the
designer (PlayerController.Ship) when it is a refit design of the same ship
type: its own tonnage when first seen plus the modernisation allowance
(3,000 t), never above the hull maximum. New designs keep the treaty limit.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.28; refit save pending confirmation
in game.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
78. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.29 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.56) — REFIT ABOVE THE TREATY LIMIT SAVED (CONFIRMED) — 2026-10-19
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: 1.11.54 still did not let the refit be saved. The designer decides
whether a design can be built through PlayerController.CanBuildShipsFromDesign
(log 18:36: reason "tonnage" for the 55,690 t refit design); the save button
reads that result (TweaksAndFixes logs "Saving ship: ... | False / Design
invalid: Can not build: Above displacement limit for BB: 35,000 t").
WHAT: when CanBuildShipsFromDesign / CanBuildFromDesignOnInit refuse a refit
design and the only reason is the displacement limit, and the refit stays
within the ship's own tonnage plus the modernisation allowance (3,000 t,
never above the hull maximum), the design is allowed. The save button of the
designer is covered as well. New designs keep the treaty limit. Log:
"[Refit] <ship>: refit of N t allowed above the treaty limit (allowance up to
M t)".
CONFIRMED in game (19 October 2026, 18:58): the refit of the Hildebrand-class
battleship (55,690 t, treaty limit 35,000 t) was saved ("Saving ship: BB
Hildebrand (Mar. 1918) | True").
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.29; confirmed in game.

79. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.30 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.57) — RISINGS IN OCCUPIED NATIONAL CORE PROVINCES, A DECISION TO PUT THEM DOWN — 2026-10-19
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: a great power that conquered the heartland of another (for example
France taking Italy) kept it for good. Historically, provinces inhabited by
another great nation were hard to hold after the war, while a large nation
could hold a small neighbour (Germany could control Belgium or the
Netherlands, not the reverse).
WHAT - national cores: a historical list of national core provinces around
1890-1914 (the game's isHome flag includes colonies and lands of other
nationalities): Britain (England x4, Scotland, Wales, Northern Ireland,
Shetland), France (north, south, west, Corsica), Germany (east, west, south,
Prussia, Heligoland), Italy (all 7), Austria-Hungary (Austria, Hungary),
Russia (central, northern, southern Russia, Siberia x3, Far East, Crimea;
Ukraine and Belarus not counted), Japan (all 7), USA (6 continental
provinces), Spain (north, south, west, Balearic and Canary Islands), China
(central, eastern, northern, southern, north-eastern China, Liaodong,
Manchuria, Penghu), Portugal (Portugal, Madeira, Azores), Netherlands,
Scandinavia (Denmark x2, Sweden x2, Norway x2), Byzantine Empire (Greece x2,
Crete, Dodecanese, Bosporus, Dardanelles; Anatolia not counted).
WHAT - risings (CoreProvincesIndependence): the game's own rebellion, made
stronger. In peacetime (no war between the occupier and the province's
nation; if that nation was dissolved, the occupier at peace) the revolt
chance of an occupied core province builds up over 12 months
(CoreRevoltRampMonths) by up to 25 (CoreRevoltBonus) times r/(r+0.5), where
r is the home population of the province's nation divided by the
occupier's: about +17 at equal population, +12.5 at half, +4 at a tenth
(game's own value for home provinces about 2.5). At war the game's value
applies. A successful rising hands the province to its nation (the game's
rebellion uses the province's claim); a dissolved great power is restored
first.
WHAT - decision: when such a rising breaks out, the occupier decides. The
player gets a window on the campaign map: put the rising down (about one
month of income x0.5-1.5 by the strength of the rising, unrest at home +6
x0.5-1.5, attitude -8 with every other great power and -12 with the
neighbours of the province and its own nation; the province is then calm
for 12 months and the build-up starts again) or let the game's rebellion run
(the province can still be defended at sea, or is lost). The AI puts a
rising down if it has three times the cost in cash, its unrest stays below
40 and the rising is not too strong. Settings in MelonPreferences.cfg:
CoreSuppressIncomeMonths, CoreSuppressUnrest, CoreSuppressRelations,
CoreSuppressAiMaxUnrest, CoreSuppressCalmMonths. Log: "[Independence]".
CORRECTION: numeric settings mentioned in sections 73-74 (TorpedoTargetSizeRatio,
MassAttackStrengthRatio, MainShipSizeRatio, MassAttackPerTarget) are set in
UserData\MelonPreferences.cfg; the F10 panel shows on/off switches only.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.30; pending confirmation in game
(decision window, restoring a dissolved great power).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
80. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.31 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.58) — FOG OF WAR: STRATEGIC MAP, CONTACT REPORTS, IDENTIFICATION IN BATTLE — 2026-10-20
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: enemy fleet positions, courses and compositions were known exactly,
and every detected ship showed its class and name at once. Historically,
reconnaissance was uncertain at every level:
- dead-reckoning positions of Iron Duke and Lion differed by 11-12 nm after
  a day at sea (Jutland 1916);
- contact reports were 10-20 nm off (Tsushima 1905), and wrongly plotted
  bearings about 100 nm off (Bismarck chase 1941, contact lost for more
  than a day);
- 4 of 7 Zeppelin reports on 19 Aug 1916 were wrong (light cruisers taken
  for battleships, Scheer turned away);
- at the Coral Sea 1942 both sides struck misidentified targets;
- at Savo 1942 a report arrived after about 8 h and understated the force.
In battle, detection was not identification: the Falklands (type known only
from tripod masts), the River Plate (light cruisers taken for destroyers),
the Denmark Strait (Prinz Eugen taken for Bismarck), Samar (escort carriers
taken for fleet carriers). At night visual contact came at 100-5,500 yd
(Jutland night action); radar detected at 27,000-32,000 yd, the eye at
about 3,000 (Guadalcanal 1942). Sources: navalgazing.net (Codebreaking and
Jutland), jutland1916.com, Wikipedia (Night action at Jutland, Action of 19
August 1916, Shinano Maru, Battle off Samar, Formosa Air Battle), lrb.co.uk
(Hogben), usni.org (Proceedings 08/1992, Naval History 06/1995),
pacificeagles.net, naval-history.net (Jutland despatches), history.navy.mil
(Guadalcanal chronology, H-gram 013).
WHAT - intelligence quality Q (0-1) for every foreign, non-allied task
force on the map, recomputed monthly: era (0.15 in 1890 to 0.55 in 1945:
wireless, direction finding, aircraft, radar) x the observer's
reconnaissance technology (average Recon of its ships against the other
great powers, x0.8-1.25) x intelligence budget (against the other great
powers, x0.85-1.2), plus distance from the nearest own port (+0.35 up to
200 km, 0 from 1,500 km), plus an own or allied task force nearby (up to
+0.30; moving 500 km, stationary - patrol - 900 km; full value with
cruisers and destroyers as scouts, 35% without), plus the size of the enemy
force (up to +0.20), plus the regional weather of the month (-0.15 to
+0.05, another -0.05 in winter).
WHAT - map (FogStrategicMap): Q below 0.35 - the force is not shown; 0.35 to
0.70 - its marker is shifted by a position error (up to 1.5 times the
force's zone radius) and its route is hidden; from 0.70 - exact. The window
of a foreign task force with incomplete intelligence shows the composition
"as reported": types may be wrong (up to 60% chance, one or two steps on the
TB-DD-CL-CA-BC-BB scale, slightly more often upwards), some ships are not
reported, no tonnage, armament or route.
WHAT - battle decision window (FogBattleReports): the enemy side as a report
- counts by reported type, with possible misidentifications and unreported
ships. Report quality: era, technology, budget and light ships in your own
force (every cruiser or destroyer up to four improves it); without scouts
the misidentification chance is of the order of the historical 4 in 7, with
good scouting close to zero.
WHAT - battle (FogTacticalIdentification): a detected enemy ship stays
unidentified until your nearest ship closes to identification range = the
smaller of 60% of the game's detection range and 15 km x target size (BB/BC
1, CA 0.85, CL 0.75, DD 0.6, TB 0.5) x daylight x weather x smoke 0.5,
within 1.2-20 km. Until then the ship list, overhead label, info panel and
division tooltip show "Unidentified ship (large/medium/small)", closer
"Probable <type>?" (the type may be wrong), with no name, class icon or
details. Identification is shared by the whole side and lasts to the end of
the battle.
WHAT - AI (FogAiPerception): the AI judges enemy strength the same way -
ships never seen do not count, unidentified ones by their reported type
(massed attack, withdrawal when outmatched, torpedo targets).
WHAT - interceptions (FogInterceptions): a battle that needs a force at sea
to be found (MissionGenerator.CanParticipateInBattleByRecon) fails with a
70% chance at Q < 0.35 and 30% at Q < 0.5 of the attacker on that force -
for the player and the AI.
Settings: FogOfWar (master), FogStrategicMap, FogBattleReports,
FogTacticalIdentification, FogAiPerception, FogInterceptions - in the F10
panel ("Fog of war" group); FogStrength (1 = historical calibration, 0 = no
fog) in UserData\MelonPreferences.cfg. All rolls are deterministic (month,
ids, battle), so loading a save does not change them; nothing is written to
the save. "Fog of war" chapter in the in-game help (Iron Admiral). Log:
"[Fog]" (monthly factors, Q of the first forces, battle window,
identifications with distance and range, failed interceptions).
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.31; awaiting confirmation in game
(map marker shift, choice of the enemy list in the battle window, battle
labels, identification range calibration from the log).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
81. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.32 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.59) — MISIDENTIFICATIONS ONLY INTO TYPES THE NAVY HAS — 2026-10-20
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: first test in game (January 1919, Mediterranean, 3 BB against 16
French ships, no own scouts): the battle window showed the report as
designed (quality 0.41, misidentification chance 0.35, 14 reported, 2
missing, the right list replaced), but it listed "3 battlecruisers"
although France has no battlecruisers. A misidentification moved the type
by a step on the TB-DD-CL-CA-BC-BB scale regardless of the enemy navy.
WHAT: a reported type can only be a type the owner's navy actually has
(ships in service, not building, sunk or scrapped, plus ships of that navy
in the current battle). The one- or two-step error is counted over that
navy's types; if there is none in the rolled direction the other direction
is used; a navy with a single type is reported correctly. Applies to the
battle window, the foreign task force window and unidentified ships in
battle.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.32; the battle window of section 80
confirmed in game (enemy list chosen correctly, report with counts and the
unconfirmed-contacts line).

82. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.33 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.60) — PROBLEM REPORT AND USAGE STATISTICS (LOCAL ONLY) — 2026-10-20
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: player reports arrive without logs or with fragments, and the author
does not know which versions, nations and features players actually use.
Collecting data behind the player's back is out of the question (trust,
GDPR, Nexus moderation), and the mod sends nothing apart from a single
update check.
WHAT - problem report (F10 panel, new "Tools" tab, "Create problem report"
button): one file IronAdmiral_report_YYYYMMDD_HHMM.zip on the desktop with
MelonLoader\Latest.log, the last three logs from MelonLoader\Logs, the
[DIP_GG_Fixes] section of MelonPreferences.cfg, IRON_ADMIRAL_VERSION.txt, a
listing of the Mods folder (size, date, SHA-1 of the DLLs - shows a broken
install), system info (game and Unity version, OS, .NET, resolution, CPU,
GPU, memory), an error summary (counts of warnings and exceptions, the 25
most frequent messages) and - only with consent - the usage counters. The
Windows user name, home folder and PC name are replaced by <user>, <home>,
<pc>. Nothing is sent: the player attaches the file to a post on Nexus,
Steam or GitHub himself.
WHAT - usage statistics (UsageStatistics, off by default): at the first
start (after about 25 s, outside battle) one window asks and explains what
is counted; the answer is stored in UsageStatisticsAsked, later a switch in
F10 ("Information and updates" group and "Tools" tab). Counters in
UserData\IronAdmiral\usage.txt: sessions (total and per mod version),
first use date, last version, campaign turns, battles and battle minutes,
last nation and campaign year, number of reports created. No names, saves
or other files of the player.
Log: "[Diagnostics]".
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.33; awaiting confirmation in game
(consent window, Tools tab, zip contents).

84. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.35 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.62) — FOG OF WAR: NIGHT AND WEATHER SHORTEN IDENTIFICATION — 2026-10-20

treated them as 0-1 multipliers: clamped to the upper bound, night and bad
weather did not shorten the identification range at all (a battleship:
18 km instead of about 3 km in those conditions).
WHAT: values above 1.5 are divided by 100 before use; the log shows the
resulting light factor ("identification light factor"). The other
identification rules are unchanged.

86. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.36 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.64) — FOG OF WAR: ENEMY FLEET MARKERS NO LONGER LAND ON LAND — 2026-10-20
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: report from the game - with incomplete intelligence, markers of enemy
task forces appeared over land. The position error of section 80 was a
shift in a random direction with no check that it stayed at sea.
WHAT: the position error runs along the force's own sea route (FullPath, or
Path when there is none): the marker is shown ahead of or behind the force
on its course, at the same distance as before (up to 1.5 times the zone
radius at the poorest intelligence still shown on the map). Historically,
dead-reckoning error grew mainly along the course. A force with no route
(stationary) is shown where it is, still without a route line. Section 85
(DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.63) goes into the same package 1.2.36.

87. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.37 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.65) — FOG OF WAR: POSITION ERROR ALSO FOR STATIONARY FORCES, ALWAYS AT SEA — 2026-10-20
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: with section 86 a force with no route (stationary) was shown at its
true position under incomplete intelligence. It should be shifted like any
other, just not onto land.
WHAT: the position error has any direction (moving and stationary forces).
Each candidate is checked with the game's own land test
(CampaignMap.RaycastLand): the line from the force to the shown position
must not cross land. Up to 12 tries with other directions (golden-angle
steps), from the seventh at half the distance; when none stays at sea, the
shift runs along the force's route (section 86), and with no route the true
position is used. Check: the land test at the force's own position must
report no land, otherwise the mod does not trust it and uses the route; the
first results are logged ("position error check").
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.37; awaiting confirmation in game.

Weissenburg at 2547 m"). The game re-enables the ship panel outside

WHAT: while an unidentified enemy ship is selected, the hidden panel and the

battleship, 1.9 km for a light cruiser and 1.3 km for a torpedo boat.

89. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.39 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.67) — FOG OF WAR: VISIBILITY IN KM, CORRECTION OF SECTIONS 84 AND 88 — 2026-10-20
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: the battle described in section 88 was fought in daylight (report
from the game), and the log showed "daytime detection 34.63, weather
detection 50.00". These values are not percentages (section 84) but
visibility distances in kilometres. Section 84 read them as 35% and 50%,
so in broad daylight the identification range fell to night levels (a
battleship about 2.5 km) and the "Unidentified..." labels stayed for the
whole battle. The explanation in section 88 ("as designed") was wrong.
WHAT: visibility = the smaller of the two values (km); the identification
cap = 45% of visibility x target size (BB/BC 1, CA 0.85, CL 0.75, DD 0.6,
TB 0.5) x smoke 0.5. At 34.6 km visibility: a battleship about 15.6 km, a
torpedo boat about 7.8 km (still at most 60% of the game's detection
range). Night values have not been seen in the log yet; assumption: the
game then reports a correspondingly smaller visibility. Log: "battle
visibility ... -> visibility X km, battleship identification up to Y km".
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.39; awaiting confirmation in game
(a day and a night battle).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
90. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.40 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.68) — SHARED SHIP DESIGNS IN THE MOD PACKAGE — 2026-10-20

WHY: ship designs prepared by the author in test campaigns (50 .bindesign

the author's local folder.
WHAT: the designs are in the package in Mods\IronAdmiralDesigns. At game
start the add-on copies them into the game's shared design folder
(LocalLow\Game Labs\Ultimate Admiral Dreadnoughts\Designs) before the game
loads them. A file of the same name that is already there is never
overwritten and nothing is deleted - the player's own designs stay as they
are. The game uses them like any shared design (according to the campaign
settings). Switch: InstallModDesigns (F10 panel, "Campaign AI"). Log:
"[Designs]".
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.40; awaiting confirmation in game on a
clean installation.

91. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.41 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.69) — BATTLE REPORT IN THE BATTLE RESULT WINDOW — 2026-10-20

WHY: after a battle the result window showed only ship lists and losses;
what happened in the battle (charges, withdrawals, panic, surrenders) was
only visible in the log. The report is to be in the same window, with no

WHAT: during the battle the add-on collects key moments: the first
identification of an enemy ship (with what it had been taken for), torpedo
charges, massed attacks, ramming, scatter on escape, withdrawal when
outmatched or unable to penetrate, crew panic, surrenders (scanned every
2 s), sinkings (Ui.OnShipSinks) and, after the battle, prizes and ships
returned to their owner. When the game shows the result block of the
battle window (BattlePopup, BattleResultInfo), a "Battle report" is
appended: up to 12 most important events (priority: sinkings and prizes,
surrenders, panic and massed attacks, ramming and scatter, withdrawals,
charges, identification) in order, with the battle minute. Further events
are counted and referred to the log. Fought battles only, not auto-resolve.

92. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.42 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.70) — SMALLER POSITION ERROR ON THE MAP, REPAIR OF A CORRUPTED SETTINGS FILE — 2026-10-20
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: the game log (1.11.68) showed inexactly known forces drawn 480-2600 km
from their true position - the error was derived from the force's zone
radius and on large zones grew to ocean scale. Second problem: after an
interrupted write UserData\MelonPreferences.cfg consisted only of zero
bytes; MelonLoader reported a load error at every start and all settings
ran on their defaults.
WHAT: the position error no longer depends on the zone radius: it grows
linearly from 0 (at the threshold of exact observation) to at most 180 km
(at the threshold of detection), with random spread within that range;
still away from land (12 directions checked, route as fallback). At start
the add-on checks the settings file: if more than half of it is zero
bytes, it keeps a copy MelonPreferences.cfg.corrupt_<date> and writes the
file anew with the current values. Settings changed before the corruption
have to be set again in the F10 panel.

93. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.43 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.71) — INEXACTLY KNOWN FLEETS ARE NOT DRAWN ON LAND — 2026-10-20
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: in game some inexactly known fleets were still drawn on land. Causes:
(1) only the line from the force to the shown position was tested, not the
position itself; (2) a force in port or by the coast failed that test and
went straight to its route, and a route ends in a port, i.e. on land; the
route result was not tested again.
WHAT: the shown position itself must be sea (land test at the target
point). For a force at sea the line from the force must also not cross
land; for a force in port or by the coast only the target point is tested.
Up to 16 tries in different directions and at shorter distances, then
points on the route (ahead of and behind the force, also tested), and if
nothing passes, the true position.
Log: "[Fog] position error placement" once a month (how many shifted,
how many along the route, how many at the true position).
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.43; awaiting confirmation in game.
