94. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.44 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.72) — GREAT POWERS WITHOUT PORTS WAIT TO REGAIN THEM — 2026-10-20
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: the game (CampaignController.CheckForCampaignEnd) dissolves a great
power that no longer controls any province with a port (Player.CanDissolve
-> DisablePlayer) and ends the player's campaign as a defeat in that case
(FinishCampaign, TotalDefeat) - even when the nation still has land
provinces and a fleet. Historically, nations cut off from the sea fought on.
WHAT (switch PortlessPowersWait, F10 panel, "Campaign AI"):
- a great power without ports is not dissolved while it has at least one
  province or ship; the player's campaign goes on (in-game notice);
- ships of the lost last port go to the nearest allied port instead of the
  conqueror (without an ally - the existing rule: to the port's conqueror);
- without a port the power orders no ships (BuildNewShips);
- peace: if the power won the war (victory points), the enemy returns one
  of its national core ports (the largest); if after peace it still has no
  port and fights no other war, its fleet goes to an ally (without an ally
  - to the nation controlling the port where the ships lie);
- a core port taken by an ally is handed back to the power (on the next
  update after the capture);
- next war: when the nation holding its core ports is at war, the AI power
  joins that war against it; in wartime its army attacks core port
  provinces next to its own provinces (land offensive,
  ProvinceBattleManager.StartBattle; player and AI);
- risings in occupied core provinces (section 79) keep working and can give
  a port back.
Core ports: the national province list of section 79.
Log: "[Portless]" (monthly state of every great power without ports, ship
moves, returned ports, land offensives, joining wars).
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.44; awaiting confirmation in game.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
95. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.45 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.73) — BATTLE DISPATCHES IN THE LOG — 2026-10-21
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: the AI decisions in the log ("[Battle]") are technical (distances,
factors) - good for diagnostics, poor at telling the story of a battle.
The log should read like an account from the people who were there.
WHAT: during a fought battle the key moments are also written as short
dispatches from the people taking part - lookouts, captains, gunnery and
torpedo officers, flagships: first sightings (player side), identification
(with a correction of a wrong report), torpedo charges, massed attacks,
ramming, scatter when an escape fails, withdrawal when outmatched or
unable to penetrate, closing the range, torpedo tracks, zigzagging,
aborted attacks, panic, torpedoes held because of a friendly ship in the
lane, surrenders, sinkings and, after the battle, prizes. Several wordings
per event, battle time (mm:ss). Written to MelonLoader\Latest.log
("[Dispatch]" lines) and to one text file per battle in
UserData\IronAdmiral\Dispatches. The technical "[Battle]" lines stay
(switch BattleAiLog) - problem reports need them.
Switch: BattleDispatches (F10 panel, "Battle AI").
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.45; awaiting confirmation in game.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
96. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.46 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.74) — AGEING CONTACT REPORTS, NIGHT GIVES AWAY THE SHIPS THAT FIRE — 2026-10-21
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: an inexactly known force was drawn with a position error, but always
around its current position - the map "knew" where it was now.
Historically a staff had a report hours or days old: last seen here,
heading X. In night battles identification was decided by gun flashes,
fires and the quality of optics (Japanese lookouts with night binoculars
often beat American radar - the model is the game Task Force 1942 and its
manual).
WHAT:
- strategic map: the marker of an inexactly known force stays where it
  was last reported; each month the force can be sighted again - more
  often with better intelligence; the enemy group window tells the age of
  the report and the course seen (or that it was stationary).
  Switch FogStaleContacts;
- battle at night or in poor visibility: a ship that fired in the last
  seconds and a burning ship are identified from further away; Japanese
  lookouts identify from further away at night. Switch FogNightFactors.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.46; awaiting confirmation in game.

97. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.47 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.75) — 920 DESIGNS FROM "ULTIMATE ADMIRAL'S SHIPPACK" — 2026-10-21
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: the AI and the player have better ready-made designs when the shared
designs hold a design for almost every hull and period. "Ultimate
Admiral's Shippack" by Badkapp (MarineWerft, Nexus mod 45, version 4) has
937 such designs for the 10 vanilla great powers (1890-1950). The author's
permissions allow use of the assets without asking, provided the author is
credited; not in sold mods.
WHAT: every design was checked against Iron Admiral data (DIP The Great
Game 1.3.5): the hull and all parts must exist, the hull must be enabled
and available to the design's nation. 920 designs pass and are in the
package (Mods\IronAdmiralDesigns\Shippack, with CREDITS.txt). Left out
17: 2 - hull ca_1_italian_exp_japan_3mast does not exist, 6 - missing
parts (e.g. fuso_funnel_standard_1), 6 - hull disabled in DIP GG (bc_1,
ca_5_gen_treaty), 4 - hull not available to the nation. At game start the
designs are copied into the shared designs; existing files are never
overwritten. Switch: InstallShippackDesigns (F10 panel).

DIP data may differ from what the author intended.

98. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.48 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.76-1.11.77) — ARMOUR OBLIQUITY, SHIP TRADE, HISTORICAL CLASSES, NEW LICENCE — 2026-10-21
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. ARMOUR OBLIQUITY (Obliquity.cs)
WHY: the section 72 diagnostics (ArmorDiag, "[Armor]" log) showed in a
1918 battle that the game's penetration does not depend on the angle: the
penetration estimate (EstimatePenetration) for the same gun, range and
target was identical at 0/30/45/60/75/90 deg (e.g. 356 mm at 36.4 km:
15389 at every angle), and the penetration used for hits (GetPenetration)
with a 45-deg direction gave the same 221.6 mm against the belt.
Horizontal impact angles in that battle (15-deg bands, 0-90): 1601/1292/
1412/1938/1684/1438 - almost even, a third at 60 deg or more, each resolved
as a square-on hit. The angle only decided belt or deck (side-hit chance
84% at 0-15 deg fall, 7% at 75-90) and ricochet.
WHAT: the belt thickness a shell must defeat grows with the horizontal
angle between the shell's path and the belt (angle from the game's own
impact computation, same frame, against the target's axes):
- capped shells (from CappedShellYear = 1905): belt / cos(angle) up to 45 deg;
- above 45 deg the cap is knocked aside (Okun, navweaps, 46 cm Type 91,
  note 2); the excess angle counts as for an uncapped shell;
- uncapped shells (before 1905): belt / cos(angle)^1.3 (soft caps begin to
  tear off at about 15 deg and are almost useless above 20 - navweaps
  tech-055);
- upper limit x3 (about 70 deg); steeper hits are left to ricochet;
- decks unchanged (the game has separate deck penetration curves).
Factor (capped / uncapped): 15 deg x1.04/1.05; 30 x1.15/1.21; 45 x1.41/
1.57; 60 x2.22/2.46; 70+ x3/x3. Example: a 305 mm belt at 45 deg = about
431 mm, at 60 deg = about 677 mm.
Ricochet: no numeric critical angles for capped shells were found in the
sources; Okun's values for uncapped shells stay (45 / 80 deg / 100%,
section 72).
Switches: ArmorObliquity, ObliquityMaxFactor (3), ObliquityUncappedExponent
(1.3), CappedShellYear (1905). Log after a battle: "[Armor] obliquity" (belt
hits by angle, average factor) and five axis calibration lines.
2. SHIP TRADE (Trade.cs, F9 window)
- Used ships for sale instead of scrapping: the player lists a ship; states
  at peace with him (great powers and minors) send offers each month (up to
  3 at a time, valid 3 months; price from the ship's cost, age -4.5% a year,
  min 15%, hull condition, attitude 0.85-1.1 and the buyer's money);
  accepting hands over the ship with its design; the buyer's attitude
  improves, the buyer's enemies' attitude to the seller worsens. A listing
  expires after a year without offers. AI great powers sell ships of 20+
  years (BB, BC, CA) or 15+ (others): 8% chance a month, the oldest ship, a
  random buyer among those who can pay.
- Orders abroad without an alliance: from any great power at peace with the
  player; the builder's design or his own (copied to the builder); price =
  cost x 1.25-1.5 by attitude (ForeignOrderPremiumMin/Max), paid monthly
  over the build time to the builder; takes no slip at home; three unpaid
  months cancel the order; on completion, at war or with attitude below
  -60 the builder keeps the ship (requisition, like Agincourt and Erin in
  1914).
- State saved with the game (IronAdmiral_Economy\trade_*.txt).
Switches: ShipTrade, ShipTradeAiSells, ShipTradeKey (F9).
3. HISTORICAL CLASSES IN THE SHARED DESIGNS
- Historical design builder (HistDesigner.cs, author tool): for each class
  in the specification (data: data/historical) the game's generator builds
  the ship on the proper hull, limited to the main calibre, gun count and
  turret type; displacement, speed and armour are then set and the armour
  thinned until the weight fits; only designs the game accepts are saved.
- First batch: 41 battleship and battlecruiser classes
  (Mods\IronAdmiralDesigns\Historical, historical_ids.csv); more classes in
  the next versions.
- The AI picks a historical class of its own navy first (HistPriority.cs,
  before the TweaksAndFixes choice) when the design is from the last 12
  years and the technology match is at least 0.9.
Switches: HistoricalDesignsFirst, HistoricalDesignsYearsBack (12).
4. SETTINGS: a settings file saved as zeros was restored without writing it
to disk; now a healthy file is copied to MelonPreferences.cfg.lastgood and
restored from it.
5. LICENCE: from this version the author's own work is under the terms in
LICENSE (playing, private changes, videos - free; republishing, forks,
reuse in other mods - with the author's permission; no commercial use).
Versions up to 1.2.47 stay under Apache 2.0. Third-party parts keep their
own licences (NOTICE.md); contributions per CONTRIBUTING.md.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.48; obliquity and trade await
confirmation in game.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
99. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.49 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.78-1.11.79) — TRADE WINDOW AND OBLIQUITY FIXES, 48 HISTORICAL CLASSES — 2026-10-21
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. TRADE WINDOW (F9): in 1.2.48 the window showed only its frame and tabs,
no ship list, and the log got an error every frame ("Method unstripping
failed", "pushing more GUIClips than popping") - the GUILayout functions
are stripped from the game build. The window is now drawn with GUI.Box /
GUI.Label only, and clicks are read with Input.GetMouseButtonDown and
hit-tested against the rectangles of the last frame (as the F10 panel);
lists are split into pages.
2. OBLIQUITY: calibration in the first battle (1918, Hildebrand against a
squadron with Vergniaud) showed that the game calls
ImpactForwardToAngleSideForward(direction, side normal) sometimes in world
space (2nd argument = the ship's axis) and sometimes in the ship's local
space (2nd argument = (1,0,0), direction = shell velocity). 1.2.48 always
compared the direction with the ship's world axis, so for local-space calls
the angle could come out at about 85 deg instead of about 25 (average belt
factor in that battle x2.22 over 120 computations). The angle is now taken
between the two arguments of the call (always the same space), in the
horizontal plane; the calibration lines in the log show it next to the
game's own result.
3. HISTORICAL CLASSES: the package now holds 48 classes (battleships,
battlecruisers and the first cruisers). Builder (author tool): keeps the
first valid try that matches the historical main battery; single main
mounts for early cruisers and odd gun counts; file names without
characters Windows does not allow.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.49; the F9 window and the new angle
measurement await confirmation in game.

buttons ended up off-screen.

the screen (GUI.Box / GUI.Label, as the F9 and F10 windows), with the

(Kronprinz offered), no exceptions in the log; obliquity: belt hits by
horizontal angle 0-15/15-30/30-45/45-60/60-75/75-90 deg =
2181/1468/2215/1149/277/153, average belt thickness factor x1.29 over 7443
computations (1.2.48: x2.22).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: a custom battle (1890, Japan against China) showed in the log
"no such TechnologyData from save: submarine_exp_1", then "Couldn't load
MHMW Unyo", "Couldn't load MHMW Yamashiro" and "Shared design Battleship
#1: Failed!"; TweaksAndFixes fell back to the stock predefined designs
(31692 loaded in about 8 s) and then generated random ships. A check of the
package showed that all 920 Shippack designs (section 97, 1.2.47) list
technologies that DIP does not have - 67 names in five groups
(submarine_exp_*, submarine_hull_*, sub_surv_*, sub_surv_end_*,

none of them: neither as shared designs in the campaign nor in custom

designs and the other shipped designs are never touched.

Mods\IRON_ADMIRAL_VERSION.txt still named an older package - correct
behaviour when only the DLL is swapped by hand.

102. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.52 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.82) — CUSTOM BATTLES: HISTORICAL CLASSES FIRST, NO BROKEN PREDEFINED STEP — 2026-10-21
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
BACKGROUND (TweaksAndFixes source, Harmony/BattleManager.cs): for every
ship of a custom battle TAF tries (1) a shared design from the Designs
folder, (2) a predefined design, (3) the ship generator. Iron Admiral ships
no predefined-design file, so step 2 loads the game's built-in designs
(31692 in about 8 s); they refer to technologies DIP removed and none of
them loads ("no such TechnologyData from save: submarine_exp_1", "Failed to
load predef ... Trying again", about 20 tries per ship type).
1. "Pregenerated" off by default (DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.82, CustomBattle.cs):
on the main menu the add-on sets TAF's switch for predefined designs in
custom battles (UiM.skirmishSetupMod.usePredefs) to off, so a ship without
a fitting shared design goes straight to the generator. TAF does not save
this switch between game runs; the "Pregenerated" button in the custom
battle screen turns it back on for the running game. New setting (F10):
CustomBattlePregenerated (off). If the TAF field is not found, the add-on
logs it and changes nothing.
2. Historical classes first in custom battles: TAF asks for shared designs
through its own CampaignControllerM.GetSharedDesign, which the
historical-first choice of 1.11.77 (on the game's method) did not reach.
The same choice is now hooked there too, for custom battles only: a
historical class of the nation for the battle year (up to
HistoricalDesignsYearsBack years older) is taken before other shared
designs; designs already placed in the battle are not repeated.
3. Considered and not taken:
- DIP's pre-generated design files (DIP Extended 1.3.5 HF1, Nexus mod 7,
23 files, 545 MB). Checked against Iron Admiral's data: all hulls, parts,
components and technologies exist, and a test custom battle (USA vs
Spain, 1890) loaded them without errors. They are generator output with
historical names, not historical classes; from the British file: 1906
"Bellerophon" with 5 twin 14" turrets (historical: 10 x 12" in 5 twin
turrets), 1906 "Glory" with 5 twin 14" (historical: Canopus-class
pre-dreadnought, 4 x 12"), 1912 "Colossus" with 4 triple 13" (historical:
10 x 12" in 5 twin), 1912 "Warspite" with 6 twin 14" (historical Queen
Elizabeth class: 8 x 15" in 4 twin); 1368 of 8782 names numbered
("California 4271"). Not used (author's decision).
- A predefined-design file built from Iron Admiral's historical classes.
It worked where a class exists (test 1912: King George V for Britain,
Danton for France), but with any predefined file present TAF no longer
falls back to the built-in designs, and where the file has no design for
the nation, type and year TAF passes an empty result on without a check
(GetRandomShip returns null; BattleManager then calls FromStore and reads
its name), which would stop the battle loading. The historical classes
reach custom battles as shared designs instead (point 2).
4. Fix: the historical class "Dreadnought" carried the design name
"Vengeance"; the name field is corrected, the rest of the file unchanged.
5. Section 101 confirmed in game (custom battle Britain vs China 1890 on
1.2.51): shared designs BB MHMW Lordington and BB MHMW Wei taken at once,
0 "no such TechnologyData", 0 "Couldn't load", 0 exceptions.
6. In-game Help: new chapter "Custom battles: where the ships come from";
campaign guide: new chapter 12.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.52; points 1 and 2 await
confirmation in a custom battle.

104. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.54 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.89) - BATTLE AI AFTER THE FLEET BATTLE INSTRUCTIONS, DOCTRINES BY NATION AND PERIOD, DISPATCHES ABOVE THE SHIPS - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Background: a player's test battle in the unmodded game (1916, four German
battleships against an American battleship with a cruiser screen) showed the
AI battleship waiting at extreme range until its screen had been destroyed,
and the destroyers firing once and then hovering just outside torpedo range.
The rules below follow the US Navy War Instructions 1944 (chapters 8 and 12,
paragraph numbers in brackets) and historical accounts of the navies; the
sources are listed in Mods\battleDoctrines.csv.
1. Battle line first (1295, 1298, 12124): AI battleships and heavy cruisers
move up to effective gun range instead of waiting at extreme range; AI
flotillas hold their torpedoes until the battle lines are engaged, and the
line then engages the enemies nearest to the attacking flotillas. When the
enemy is clearly stronger the torpedoes go first (delay action, 12298): with
at least one torpedo craft per target the massed attack goes in even below
full massed strength (before, the whole force withdrew at once).
2. Torpedo attack cycle: flotillas press the attack until every tube is
empty. Ships with reload torpedoes turn away behind smoke, open the range,
wait at a safe distance, reload and return; ships without reloads turn on the
enemy light forces (12128-12129). A ship whose tubes do not reload within
1.5 x ReloadMaxSeconds counts as having no reloads.
3. Covering retreat: when the torpedo attack has failed and the enemy is
stronger, the most valuable ships (cost and build time) withdraw while the
cheaper ones attack the pursuers or lay smoke between them. The damage at
which a charge is broken off depends on the ship's value (cheap ships fight
longer, the most valuable break off earlier).
4. Cruiser roles (heavy cruisers always, light cruisers when the side has
destroyers): defend the battle line against enemy flotillas (12117, 1207),
support an own flotilla attack (12117), threaten the enemy line from the
flank opposite the torpedo attack (12126), keep out of the enemy line's
concentrated fire (12120); a "flying squadron" of fast cruisers strikes the
enemy flank where the doctrine has it (Yalu 1894).
5. Night (chapter 8): heavy ships do not close the range at night (829);
flotillas attack by surprise and break off when spotted early by a stronger
enemy (827); nations that avoid night action hold their flotillas back,
nations that seek it attack without waiting for the battle lines.
6. Fire distribution for main guns (12164-12166): at most the doctrine's
number of ships (default 3) on one target, one ship per target when
outnumbered, targets combining hitting power with weak protection first; the
current target is kept, and when too many ships are on one target the ships
with the lowest ids keep it.
7. Doctrines by nation and period: Mods\battleDoctrines.csv has a new format
(year range, lineFirst, night, heavyProtect, turnAwayAlways, flyingSquadron,
commandDelay, hitAndRun, maxPerTarget and the source in every row; 25 rows;
the old format is still read). Where the instructions contradict each other
the row of the nation and period decides - for example: Japan from 1922 and
France before 1905 send the flotillas first; Britain to 1929, Italy to 1940
and the USA to 1941 avoid night action while Germany to 1918, Japan from 1906,
France from 1919 and the USA from 1942 seek it; the Royal Navy to 1929 always
turns away from torpedoes; Austria-Hungary, the Kriegsmarine and France use
hit-and-run light forces; Britain to 1929 and China react more slowly
(centralised command). The numbers are a gameplay calibration, not values
from the sources. Rows marked "gap" have no researched source yet.
8. Fixes found in the battle test: the AI's own penetration estimate picked
up the armour angle of a shell impact handled in the same frame (belt counted
up to three times as thick), so AI battleships wrongly decided they could not
hurt the enemy and fled; four battleships could lock on one cruiser despite
the limit (the heaviest-calibre filter ran before the distribution); a
reloading flotilla ran far out of the battle instead of waiting at a safe
distance; a flotilla without reloads kept charging with empty tubes.
9. Dispatches above the ships: during battle a dispatch about a ship is shown
for nine seconds in a box above it - own ships and identified enemies only
(fog of war). Switch: BattleDispatchBubbles (F10). New dispatches for the
rules above (reloading, returning, cruiser roles, covering retreat, night).
10. Also in this DLL, written earlier and pending confirmation in a campaign:
F7 naval intelligence window (rival navies in service, building and designed,
known as well as the intelligence allows - 1.11.84); ship trade fixes (no
instalments while the buyer is at war with the builder, AI sales never charge
the player, fog-of-war placement counter - 1.11.84); historical design
builder improvements (author tool, 1.11.83).
11. All new rules can be switched off in F10 > Battle AI: CapitalSupportScreen,
TorpedoReloadCycle, CoverRetreat, ValueBasedAbort, CruiserRoles, NightRules,
FireDistribution. Author tool (off by default): AutoTestBattle runs an
unattended custom battle from the main menu.
TEST: unattended custom battle 1916, Germany 4 BB against USA BB, CA, 3 CL,
6 DD (shared designs Konig, MHMW North Carolina, Kansas/Dallas, Wilmington,
Fairfax/Rhind/Josephus Daniels), four runs with fixes in between. Observed in
the log: massed torpedo attack of the weaker side, battle line moving up and
staying in action after the penetration fix, heavy cruiser pinning the
opposite flank, light cruisers supporting the flotillas, destroyers turning
away behind smoke to reload and returning (reload took about 950 s of game
time), fire spread over several targets. Not yet seen in a battle: covering
retreat, night rules, flying squadron. Dispatches above the ships: see the
status line of the release notes.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.54.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

106. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.56 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.90) - 16 TOWERS LOAD AGAIN, DOCTRINES FOR MORE NAVIES - 2026-10-22

Towers I-VI, Dual Barbette Towers I-III, Mini Rangefinder Towers I-II, Rear
Tower III) the condition cell "need(...), needunlock(...)" was written
without quotes. The comma split the cell into an extra column, the game
logged "error for config line ... value outside header's columns" and did
not load these parts, so shared designs using them were rejected as well

Japan 1906-1921 (night torpedo attrition before and after the decisive day
action), France 1906-1918 (fleet kept at Corfu after 1915), Russia

the Soviet navy from 1918 (small-war school), Spain (1898 and the Civil
War), the Netherlands from 1930 (cruiser-led destroyer attacks, night
action), Scandinavia (coastal fleet in being) and Greece (Kountouriotis'
independent manoeuvre at Elli and Lemnos). The sources are named in each
row; the numbers remain a gameplay calibration. Still without a source:

107. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.57 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.92) - MAIN GUNS ENGAGE THE CLOSE ENEMY - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Main-gun target choice: a player report showed three armoured cruisers
firing at a light cruiser well over ten km away while an enemy armoured
cruiser at about 4 km went untouched by their main guns, and switching
between the two over twenty times. Cause: the fire distribution limit
counted the battleships already firing at the close cruiser, so the
cruisers were sent to the free target far away, and the target score
favoured the weakly armoured light cruiser with too small a distance
penalty. Now an enemy inside close range (the larger of 5 km and 30% of the
gun range) is always a candidate regardless of the distribution limit and
gets a bonus that grows as it closes, distance weighs more in the score, and
a ship keeps its current target unless another one is clearly better. A
target given by the player is still never overridden.
2. The continuous campaign map prototype (setting ContinuousMap) is
included but off by default.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.57.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
108. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.58 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.93) - NO HOPELESS TORPEDO SHOTS - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Torpedoes of AI ships: a player report showed an enemy armoured cruiser
firing torpedoes without any chance of a hit. The automatic torpedo fire of
the game (made more aggressive in params.csv: ammo_normal_torp_acc_threshold
0.1, ammo_torp_always_shoot_range 4500 m) does not check whether the torpedo
can reach a moving target. Now an AI ship holds its torpedoes while the shot
is hopeless: the target is beyond torpedo range, the target is a small ship
(not a capital ship and not clearly larger than the shooter) beyond close
range, the target outruns the torpedo, or the run to the intercept point
(from the target's measured course and speed) exceeds 85% of the torpedo
range. Inside 1.5 km the torpedoes are never held. Settings: TorpedoSense,
TorpedoSensePlayer (the same check for the player's ships, off by default),
TorpedoSenseCloseRange, TorpedoSenseTorpedoSpeed, TorpedoSenseRangeMargin.
Holds and releases are written to the battle log.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.58.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
109. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.59 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.94) - NO TORPEDOES INTO EMPTY SEA - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Torpedoes to the wrong side: a player report showed an enemy armoured
cruiser firing torpedoes to a side of the ship where there were no ships at
all. The game keeps separate aims for the weapons of each side; a torpedo aim
of one side could hold a target lying on the other side. Such an aim is now
dropped, so the game picks again. The side of each weapon group is not
assumed but confirmed in every battle from the gun aims of that group; a
group whose guns fire to both sides (centre-line mounts) is left alone, and
a torpedo target set by the player is never touched. Setting:
TorpedoSideCheck.
2. Torpedo launch audit: every torpedo is checked once after launch; when no
enemy within reach lies within 40 degrees of its course, the battle log
records the shooter, the direction of the launch and the torpedo aims of
both sides, so any remaining case can be traced.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.59.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
110. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.60 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.95) - SURRENDERED SHIPS: NO TORPEDOES, NO LOSS ON RETURN - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Torpedoes at surrendered ships: the game's own torpedo aim does not skip a
ship that has struck her colours (the gun target selection of the mod already
did). A torpedo aim on a surrendered ship is now dropped, and the hopeless
shot check holds the torpedoes for such a target. Found in a player battle:
an enemy armoured cruiser fired torpedoes towards a surrendered destroyer.
2. A surrendered ship returning to the side that won the battle could be
lost: the damage passed to the campaign was raised to the capture value but
not capped, so a ship that ended the battle with damage 1.00 was sunk right
after being "returned" (DD V-2 in a player campaign). The damage is now
capped at 0.95.
3. A player ship that has surrendered no longer runs the scout torpedo
charge logic.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.60.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
111. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.61 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.98) - TORPEDO FIXES, BATTLE PERFORMANCE - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. The torpedo side check of 1.2.59 (TorpedoSideCheck) is switched off. In a
large battle the side of each weapon group was "confirmed" from the gun aims
while all enemies lay on one beam, and valid torpedo aims of the other group
were dropped again and again (30 times in one battle). The setting stays as
an experimental option, off by default; existing settings are switched off
once. Torpedoes are still never aimed at surrendered ships.
2. Hopeless torpedo shot check: the measured speed of the target is capped at
its top speed. A position jump made a 21-knot cruiser "outrun the torpedo"
at 1.5 km.
3. Torpedo launch audit: a torpedo or ship destroyed in the same frame no
longer stops the check.
4. Battle performance: target selection computes the enemy data (visibility,
distance, damage, calibre, penetration) once per ship instead of once per gun
group; ships pick targets at spread-out moments instead of all in the same
frame; the per-ship battle state is cleared at the start of each battle. In a
20-ship test battle the mod took about 0.7 ms of a 30-45 ms frame.
5. The armour diagnostics (log only) are off by default and their hooks on
every shell impact are installed only when switched on; existing settings
are switched off once.
6. Performance log: every 30 s of battle a [Perf] line with the frame rate,
the slowest frame, ships, torpedoes and the time each part of the mod takes;
during turn processing the time of the mod's hooks. Setting: PerformanceLog.
7. The continuous campaign map (prototype, off by default) writes a list of
the map's renderers to the log when switched on.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.61.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
112. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.62 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.101) - PANELS FOLLOW THE UI SCALE, SHORTER END OF TURN, TORPEDO DEFENCE - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. The mod's panels (F7 intelligence, F8 treasury, F10 settings, trade,
battle report, dispatches, decisions, diagnostics) follow the game's UI
scale: they use the scale factor of the game's own UI canvas, which already
contains the player's UI scale and the resolution. On a 4K screen they were
drawn in raw pixels and were far too small. Clicks and scrolling work in the
scaled panels. Settings: ModUiScaleAuto, ModUiScale (extra multiplier,
default 1).
2. Ship generator failure memory (campaign): at the end of a turn the AI
designs new ships with the game's generator, and a failed design runs all 4
attempts (10-45 s each). A nation whose design on a hull failed now gets 1
attempt on that hull for the next 12 months; a success clears the entry.
Every design is written to the log with its result and time. Settings:
ShipGenMemory, ShipGenMemoryMonths, ShipGenMemoryTries.
3. Torpedoes in self-defence: a ship may answer a light ship that is closing
in (a torpedo attack on it) with torpedoes inside 60% of its torpedo range;
small targets that are far away or not closing are still not worth a
torpedo.
4. Hopeless torpedo shot check: a decision to hold or release stands for at
least 4 s and the range limit has a 5% margin (no switching every second
near the limit).
5. One-time setting changes now run on the first frame and are saved at once.
6. Performance log: during turn processing the [Perf] line shows the time
taken by the mod's hooks in the game's tonnage, cost and build checks (in
the test: a fraction of a percent of the turn).
7. Continuous campaign map (prototype, off by default): the game's black side
planes beyond the map edges are hidden while the map is on, so the copies
of the map can be seen.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.62.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
113. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.63 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.102) - SMALLER DISPATCH BUBBLES IN BATTLE - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. The dispatch bubbles above the ships in battle have their own scale,
0.55 of the panel scale. With the panel scaling of 1.2.62 they took the full
panel scale and were about twice as large as before on a 4K screen; the text
is now about the size it had before the scaling, and the bubbles are
narrower. Setting: BattleDispatchBubbleScale (1 = the size of the panels).
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.63.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
114. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.64 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.105) - SHIP GENERATOR MEMORY KEPT, MISSING DESIGNS OF OWN SHIPS - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Ship generator failure memory: the remembered nation/hull failures are
kept in UserData\IronAdmiral\shipgen_failures.txt. In 1.2.62 they were lost
at every game start, so a game restarted between turns never saved an
attempt.
2. Missing designs of own ships: a few seconds after a campaign is loaded,
every ship of the player whose design is not in the player's design list
(for example an older prize) gets a copy of its design, so it can be
refitted. Ships whose design is in the list but hidden from it or belongs
to another nation are written to the log. Setting: RepairMissingDesigns.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.64.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
115. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.65 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.106) - CONTINUOUS MAP SAFETY FIX - 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Continuous campaign map (prototype, off by default): the camera position
was wrapped with two loops; a far-away or invalid position (for example the
camera right after a battle) could make them run without end, which looks
like a frozen game. The position is now wrapped in one step, and positions
far outside the map are left to the game. A game froze after a battle with
the map switched on; this is the most likely cause.
2. The continuous map is set up again for every new campaign map (after a
battle or a load); before, the copies and hidden border planes of the old
map were kept.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.65.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
116. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.66 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.141) - BATTLE AI, TORPEDO AND PRIZE FIXES - 2026-10-23
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Own ship details in battle: after an unidentified enemy ship was selected
and deselected, the details panel of the player's own ships stayed hidden.
It is shown again.
2. Secondary guns: when no torpedo boat threatens the ship, the secondary
guns engage the nearest enemy in range; the damage already dealt decides
only between targets at almost the same distance.
3. F10 switch "Beta updates" (Information and updates): the update check
follows the beta line or the stable line; from a beta version it also
offers the way back to the latest stable version.
4. Captured ships: a prize whose design had been erased (listed in the
fleet, missing in the designer, no refit possible) gets a copy of its
design owned by the captor. The copy is an ordinary design, visible in the
designer. Checked when the campaign is loaded and again every month.
5. Battle AI, capital ships that cannot penetrate their target: they break
off only after the target has been identified and after two minutes of
battle time (HopelessEscapeDelay), and not at all while their own side has
at least 1.5 times the enemy's combat tonnage (HopelessFightOnRatio).
Before, a cruiser could turn away the moment a ship of its own class was
sighted, and an attacking force of eleven ships broke off from two. The
dispatch no longer speaks of hits that were never fired.
6. Torpedo check for the player's ships (TorpedoSensePlayer): the player's
own ships may use the full torpedo range; the 85% margin held shots that
were a few dozen metres beyond the limit. AI ships keep the margin.
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.66.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
117. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.69 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.249) - THE 1.2.67-1.2.69 BETA LINE PROMOTED TO STABLE - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Everything below was published first as the betas 1.2.67-beta.1 ... 1.2.69-beta.59
(23-25 October) and played in my own campaign; every mechanic has its switch
in the F10 panel. Sources for the historical values: DIP_CHANGES.md.
1. Ballistics from physics and technology. The muzzle velocity and the shell
weight follow the real guns of each calibre and generation (Mk 1-5, gaps
interpolated); the shell nose shape (blunt, pointed, streamlined) comes from
the shell technologies the navy has researched; the mount can elevate as far
as its generation allows (13.5 to 45 degrees); the range is the distance the
shell flies at that elevation with drag taken from real range tables. A shot
beyond that range plunges on a steep arc instead of the game's flat line.
Gun barrels rise to the elevation of the shot. Settings: RealisticBallistics,
HistoricalShells, RangeByMountElevation, BarrelElevation.
2. Hit chance by fire-control technology (FireControlTech*): the hit chance
and the dispersion follow the year of the researched fire-control and radar
technologies, never the calendar.
3. Damage: a high-explosive shell meeting plating thinner than a quarter of
its calibre blows through it and bursts inside (HeBlastThinPlating);
partial-penetration damage raised to the upper end of the sourced range
(HistoricalDamage); capped AP shells by the shell technology of the firing
ship (obliquity); hit types counted on the damage path and listed in the
battle summary of the log.
4. Division orders in battle: the orders panel at the top of the screen,
foldable; Screen, Scout, Torpedo, Free action, Hunt (Alt+1 to Alt+6); the
order in force shown above the division and on its card; torpedo evasion
timed by the turning circle after the torpedoes are sighted; a manual move
takes command back; range tags above identified enemies, measured from the
selected division; enemy torpedo launches reported once sighted.
5. Ship parts out of place: a design whose parts stand far above or below the
hull at the start of a battle has them put back at their design positions
(RepairUnmountedParts); parts left on the campaign map without their ship
are logged and hidden (MapGhostParts).
6. Occupied lands: the player decides province by province; the AI decides
every month for every province it holds; relations kept within -100..100;
occupation costs raised several times; decision windows wait while the game
shows its own message.
7. Lost territory: a great power works to win back the provinces it held at
the campaign start and its national cores - worse relations with the holder,
war when strong enough or when the holder is at war already, land
offensives into lost provinces, provinces returned at peace. A colony weighs
half of a core province, a jewel in the crown three quarters.
8. Technology diffusion and catch-up: technologies known to other navies are
researched faster, far-ahead ones slower; powers far behind receive known
technologies; catch-up technologies marked researched.
9. AI ship designer: current hulls only, finished designs kept and reused,
failure memory per hull; main calibre, main turret count and secondary
mount count limited by the displacement the generator actually chose; an
overweight attempt loses its light guns before the armour is cut; a small
weight asymmetry no longer throws an attempt away; own randParts.csv with
the tower rows before the fore barbette for the CA_Late2 and g4 battleship
part sets (two hulls had no main tower in 19 of 20 attempts); one main
turret fewer allowed instead of giving a modern hull up; start hulls of
1890 under the newest-hull rule; generator log kept at full detail.
10. Historical weights and parts: battleship machinery heavier and cruiser
machinery lighter per horsepower, fuel for range as on real ships, secondary
mounts and casemates weigh what the period's did (HistoricalWeights,
HistoricalCasemates); optional historical parts mode (HistoricalParts).
11. Shared designs: unknown technologies, parts and components skipped on
loading (SharedDesignsSkipUnknownTech) - no more "Couldn't load" for the
Shippack designs; custom battles may force the generator
(CustomBattleNoSharedDesigns, author's tool).
12. Ship trade: refits for the buyers of the player's ships - paid refit
requests after some years in foreign service, licence or own yards, Refits
tab in the trade window (ShipTradeRefits).
13. Campaign AI and reports: AI divisions keep their distance in battle
(AiDivisionSpacing); monthly fleet age report ([FleetAge]); prize crews from
the winner's crew pool; the MelonLoader console no longer freezes the game
when clicked.
14. Politics window: the exceptions thrown while the window draws its rows
after a restored nation returns are caught and logged, so every row is
drawn.
15. Refit of a ship already above the current displacement maximum allowed
at its own displacement; relation losses kept in the game's range; Iron
Admiral version shown in the main menu and on the map (1.2.68).
16. In-game Help: eight new chapters on these mechanics (main menu / Esc ->
Help -> Iron Admiral - new mechanics).
STATUS: published as Iron Admiral 1.2.69.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
118. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.70 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.250) - DAMAGE MODEL STEP 1: ARMOUR QUALITY; BATTLE FIXES FROM A PLAYER'S TEST FILM - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Published at the same time as 1.2.70-beta.1: until the damage model is finished
the beta and the stable line get every release together.
1. Armour quality measured against Krupp cemented armour (F10: ArmorQualityHistorical,
on). The penetration table is already given in Krupp cemented armour (a 12-inch
shell pierces 439 mm at 10 km), and the game multiplied every plate once more
by the strength of its armour technology: a 200 mm belt of 1916 met the shell as
461 mm and a 16-inch belt as 907 mm. Shells now meet the plate times its
historical quality: wrought iron 0.40, compound 0.50, nickel steel 0.60, Harvey
0.80 (10.2 in of Krupp = 12 in of Harvey), Krupp cemented 1.00, later types up
to 1.12. My test (1916, 3 dreadnoughts against 2 battleships and a cruiser):
before - 30 minutes of fire at 6-12 km, about 120 heavy hits, every ship below
5 % damage; after - the American 14-inch battleship sunk within 19 minutes, the
others at 14-17 %, a 12-inch gun at 11 km just short of a 14-inch belt, as at
Jutland.
2. Target orders given by the player hold (film, "Why are we changing
targets?"): a ship ordered to fire at an enemy keeps that target until it sinks
or surrenders, or until the ship is given back to automatic targeting. The mod's
target selection no longer takes such an order for its own pick.
3. A main battery split over twin and triple turrets is one main battery: the
second turret type was treated as secondary guns, picked the nearest cruiser and
switched between the cruiser and the battleship every few seconds.
4. An AI battleship that cannot pierce the enemy battle line stays in action
(F10: HopelessEngageLighter, on): it fires at the enemy ships it can hurt, and it
no longer leaves the battle when no enemy can pierce its own belt either (film:
an AI battleship with a 16-inch belt left after a few salvoes, 0 % hits, 97 %
of its ammunition unused).
5. The division orders panel, the order tags, the range tags and the dispatch
bubbles are hidden while the battle result window or a message box is open
("Battle won - Play again?").
6. Leaving a custom battle: another mod's hook on the custom battle setup screen
throws an exception every time (NullReferenceException in SkirmishSetupMod); it
is caught so the screen finishes building - the likely cause of the hang after
"Play again? - No". The way out of the battle is logged ([Battle] leaving the
battle).
7. The "Shoot Info" panel: the first time in a battle you point at an enemy, the
state of the game's panel goes to the log ([Battle] Shoot Info check) - please
send me Latest.log if the panel stays empty.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
119. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.71 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.251) - DAMAGE MODEL STEP 2: PENETRATION FROM PHYSICS (OKUN) - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Published at the same time as 1.2.71-beta.1 (both lines get every release until
the damage model is finished).
A battle log of 25 October showed one gun mount counted as three different guns:
the gun window gave 201.6 mm, 164 kg and 945 m/s, the shells flew as 178 mm,
114 kg and 945 m/s, and the penetration was taken for 178 mm. The muzzle velocity
did not fall with the heavier shell (1.8-2.6 times the energy of the historical
7-inch gun of that generation from the same propellant), and the belt
penetration used in battle was about twice what the shell's own striking
velocity and angle of fall allowed.
1. One physical gun (F10: PhysicalGun, on). The bore as built (the calibre after
the designer's diameter change), the shell weight of the gun window and one
muzzle velocity serve the gun window, the flight of the shells, the range and the
penetration alike. The reference is the historical shell of the calibre and gun
generation scaled to the bore; at the same charge a heavier shell leaves slower
(velocity ~ weight^-0.30: the US 8"/55 fired its 118 kg AP shell at 823 m/s and
its 152 kg super-heavy AP shell at 762 m/s with the same 85 lb charge). The
technology bonuses of barrel length, propellant and AP shell count as extra
charge energy; the velocity effect of the light/heavy shell choice is replaced
by this weight law.
2. Penetration from the physics of each shot (F10: PhysicalPenetration, on): the
striking velocity and angle of fall of the shell's physical arc decide what it
pierces. Belts, turret sides, barbettes and conning towers - face-hardened armour,
Okun's formula T = C x W^0.2 x V^1.21 with the fall angle as obliquity and the AP
shell technology as shell quality; decks and turret tops - homogeneous armour,
Thompson's F-formula with Okun's standard F. C is fitted to the US 8"/55 AP Mk 21
against Class A armour (navweaps.com): the model gives 240/189/144/123/101 mm of
belt at 9.9/14.1/19.0/22.3/26.2 km against the published 254/203/152/127/102 mm,
and 51/77/102 mm of deck at 16.8/21.8/25.2 km against 51/76/102 mm. The armour
quality of step 1 (against Krupp cemented armour) and the horizontal angle to the
belt stay on top. HE shells keep the game's HE-to-AP ratio on the physical base.
The AI's penetration estimates use the same physics.
3. The gun window shows the battle values: muzzle velocity from the charge and a
penetration table with the striking velocity and angle of fall of each range
("beyond the reach of the mount" where the mount generation cannot elevate far
enough).
4. One log line per hit (F10: HitLog, at most HitLogMax per battle): calibre,
weight, striking velocity, angle of fall, physical belt and deck penetration, the
plate the shell met, the game's penetration, result and damage, and a summary
by calibre and result.
5. The diagnostic lines "[Ballistics] sample" are renamed "arc fit on the game
clock": their times and gravity belong to the game's time-compressed clock, not
to the physical flight.
My test (1916, 4 German against 4 American battleships, 12-inch and 13-inch
guns): at 8-10 km a 12-inch AP shell strikes at 530-580 m/s and pierces 310-350
mm of belt instead of the 440-460 mm of the game's table; most 12-inch hits on
the American belts at 9-11 km ended as armour hits without penetration.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
120. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.72-BETA.1 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.253) - TORPEDO SETTINGS AND TARGET PRIORITIES; AP PASS-THROUGH DAMAGE; ESCORT FIRST, TRANSPORTS AFTER - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. The stable 1.2.72 follows after the target-priority and convoy
battle tests.
1. Torpedo run settings (F10, Battle AI: TorpedoSettings). Before it was fired,
a torpedo of the period was set for a short, fast run or a long, slow one; from
the First World War some navies added an extreme-range setting for firing into a
battle line. The settings a ship has come from her navy and her torpedo
propulsion technology (torpedoSettings.csv, from each navy's own torpedoes of
each generation). AI ships and the player's ships on Auto take the fastest
setting that still reaches the point where the torpedo meets the target, with a
safety allowance; the extreme-range setting is used only against a battle line.
A change resets the tubes. The player sets a division (Shift: one ship) on the
torpedo setting strip below the division orders. The torpedo diameter and the
propulsion type now multiply each other instead of adding up. The old "Fast"
propulsion component works like the standard one.
2. Torpedo target priorities for the player: a strip of ship classes (BB, BC, CA,
CL, DD, TB, TR) clicked in order of importance. The division's tubes aim at the
nearest enemy of the most important class in reach; a division under the Torpedo
attack order charges it. A ship not yet identified in battle is not a candidate
(fog of war). A target given by the player is kept. AI ships keep their own rules.
3. Damage of an AP shell that passes clean through a ship (F10, Battle:
OverPenDamage). The game left almost nothing of such a hit; it now does a share of
the damage of a penetration, taken from US Navy war damage reports (Boise, South
Dakota, Sterett, Salt Lake City): shells that passed through rarely did serious
damage, shells that burst inside often did. HE shells are left to the game.
4. Physical gun, step 2b: the shell weight follows the bore as built, the muzzle
velocity is the historical one of the calibre and gun generation multiplied by
the game's technology bonus, and the plate scaling has safe limits.
5. Battle AI - escort first, transports after (F10, Battle AI:
TransportsAfterEscort). While enemy warships are present, guns do not fire at
transports; only low-calibre guns may take a transport, and only when no enemy
warship is within their reach. Once the escort is sunk, has surrendered or has
been out of sight for a while, every gun may engage the transports. A target
ordered by the player is kept.
6. In-game help: chapter "Torpedo settings and targets in battle".
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
121. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.72-BETA.2 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.254) - GUN WINDOW SHOWS THE PHYSICAL SHELL WEIGHT AND THE SOURCE OF THE MUZZLE VELOCITY - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.72-beta.1.
1. Gun window: the muzzle velocity line names its source - the historical
velocity of the calibre and gun generation, the game's technology bonus and the
shell weight it is computed for (the game's own value follows for comparison).
2. Gun window: on a gun bored out above its base calibre the shell weight line
shows the shell of the bore as built (the shell flight and the penetration
already used it since 1.2.72-beta.1); the game's own value follows for
comparison. Example: a 142.2 mm gun on a 5-inch mount - 48.6 kg instead of
34.6 kg.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
122. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.72-BETA.3 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.256) - SCOUTS HEAD FOR REPORTED SMOKE, TORPEDO TARGET PRIORITIES FALL THROUGH - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Published at the same time as the stable 1.2.72. Contains everything of
1.2.72-beta.2.
1. Scout order: when the lookouts report smoke on the horizon (an enemy ship
sighted but not identified yet), a scouting division heads for the nearest smoke
within its search range and closes until the ship can be made out. Once she is
identified the scout goes back to its usual work: shadowing heavy ships at the
edge of their gun range, falling back from a stronger light force, staying
within reach of the main body. Applies to the mod's Scout order and to the
game's own Scout order. F10: ScoutTowardsSmoke (on).
2. Torpedo target priorities: a listed class the enemy does not have falls
through to the next class down, also during a Torpedo attack charge.
3. The torpedo setting and torpedo target strips under the division orders are
compact single rows with smaller buttons.
My test (1916 and 1905, two scouting divisions each, x5): after the lookouts'
reports the scouts headed for the smoke from 8-16 km and the ships were made
out; no new errors.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
123. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.72 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.256) - TORPEDO SETTINGS AND TARGETS, AP PASS-THROUGH DAMAGE, ESCORT FIRST, SCOUTS TOWARD SMOKE - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Published at the same time as 1.2.72-beta.3 (both lines get every release until
the damage model is finished). Brings to the stable line everything of
1.2.72-beta.1 to 1.2.72-beta.3 (sections 120-122):
1. Torpedo run settings by navy and torpedo technology (short-fast, long-slow,
extreme range against a battle line), chosen in battle, and torpedo target
priorities for the player's divisions.
2. Damage of AP shells passing clean through a ship, from war damage reports.
3. Physical gun, step 2b: the shell weight of the bore as built and the
historical muzzle velocity with the game's technology bonus; the gun window
shows both and names the source of the velocity.
4. Battle AI: escort first, transports after.
5. Scouts head for smoke reported by the lookouts and close until the ship can
be made out.
6. In-game help: chapter on torpedo settings and targets in battle.

124. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73-BETA.1 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.257) - AP PASS-THROUGH DAMAGE BY THE PLACE THE SHELL WENT THROUGH - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only.
1. Damage of an AP shell passing clean through a ship (F10, Battle:
OverPenDamage, OverPenBySection). It is now a share of the damage the same shell
would do as a penetration in the same place: upper works 5 %, turret or barbette
10 %, hull ends 15 %, citadel and machinery or magazine sections 20 %, and 5 %
more at or below the waterline (at most 25 %). Taken from US Navy war damage
reports: shells passing through the upper works holed fire-control directors,
radars, ducts and bulkheads; through the hull at the waterline they flooded
compartments; a shell stopped in the machinery cut the steam lines. Before, heavy
shells passing through did almost nothing.
2. Battle log: each hit names the part and hull section it struck; after the
battle a summary of damage by place and result.
3. Gun window: the shell weight line shows the weight the shells fly with in
battle (the game's own figure follows for comparison).
4. Torpedo target priorities: a class the enemy does not have passes to the next
class down (BB, BC, CA, CL, DD, TB, TR), also for the Torpedo attack order.
The torpedo setting and torpedo target strips take one low row each.
5. Battle log: the shell shape (nose) of each ship names the technology source
it was read from.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
125. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73-BETA.2 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.259) - GUN ORDERS FOR THE PLAYER'S DIVISIONS; DISPATCHES TELL WHERE SHELLS STRUCK - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.73-beta.1.
1. Gun orders (F10, Battle AI: GunOrders). Two strips below the torpedo strips,
one for the main battery and one for the secondary battery. Ship classes (BB,
BC, CA, CL, DD, TB, TR) are clicked in order of importance; the guns take the
nearest enemy of the most important class in range, and a class the enemy does
not have passes to the next class down. "Threat" (the default) aims the main
battery at the enemy whose guns threaten the ship most and the secondary battery
at torpedo craft closing on a firing position. "Split" lets the main battery
fire at one ship to starboard and another to port, with a lower hit chance
while it does (GunSplitAccuracy, 0.7). Shift = this ship only. A target the
player gives himself is kept, and transports are still left until the escort
is gone. AI ships keep their own rules.
2. Battle dispatches tell where notable shell hits struck and what they did:
the place (upper works, a turret, the hull forward or aft, amidships, the
machinery or magazine spaces), whether the shell burst inside, went clean
through or was stopped by the armour, fires, flooding and damaged equipment
(F10, Battle: BattleHitDispatches; at most one per ship every 25 s).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
126. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73-BETA.3 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.260) - SHELL SHAPE CHECK FOR THE PLAYER'S SHIPS IN THE BATTLE LOG - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.73-beta.2.
1. Battle log: the shell shape (nose) of every player's ship is written with the
technology sources it was read from, also when the campaign's own designs have
already filled the log lines (the player's 1921 battleships fired the blunt
1890s shell, while the enemy had the 1907 nose).

127. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73-BETA.4 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.263) - BATTLE STRIPS STAY OPEN, ORDERED TORPEDO ATTACK GOES IN, SHELL SHAPE FOR NAVIES WITHOUT THE NOSE TECHNOLOGY - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.73-beta.3.
1. The battle strips (division orders, torpedo setting, torpedo targets, gun
orders) stay open after a click. The click also reached the game, which took it
for a click on the sea and dropped the ship selection. The ship selected when a
strip was clicked now stays the strips' ship until another ship is selected or
the player clicks outside the strips.
2. The player's Torpedo attack order goes in at once at the torpedo target
priority (the next class down when the listed one is missing) or the nearest
capital ship. Before, it waited by the AI's rules until a capital ship came
within the AI's start distance.
3. Shell shape (nose): when a navy's technology list holds neither nose
technology at all (seen in a 1921 campaign: the player's navy could never
research the 1907 nose and fired the blunt 1890s shell), the year of the battle
decides: the 1907 nose from 1910, the 1927 shells from 1930.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
128. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73-BETA.5 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.264) - BELT PENETRATION RECALIBRATED FOR ALL CALIBRES - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.73-beta.4.
1. Belt penetration now depends on the calibre as well as on the shell weight and
the striking velocity (a DeMarre-type law), fitted to the side-armour
penetration of nine AP guns from 5 to 16 inch (navweaps.com). Before, the law was
fitted to one 8-inch gun only: 5-6 inch guns penetrated 1.4-2.6 times too much,
12-16 inch guns 12-26 % too little. Examples: a 6-inch Mk 4 (59.8 kg) at 5.6 km
now 199 mm (was 248); a 15-inch (1,004 kg) at 10 km 512 mm (was 404); a 3-inch
(11.3 kg) at 1 km 102 mm (was 157). Propellant and barrel technologies act
through the muzzle velocity, for every calibre alike. Setting (F10, Battle):
PhysicalPenetrationBeltC.
2. Shell shape (nose): when a navy's technology list holds no nose technology,
the AP shell fitted to the ship decides first (a shell of 1907 or later has the
improved nose); the year of the battle only when the shell is unknown.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
129. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73-BETA.6 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.266) - SMALLER BATTLE PANELS AND GUN WINDOW TEXT - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.73-beta.5.
1. Division order buttons are smaller; the torpedo and gun strips under them
moved up accordingly.
2. Battle strips: torpedo setting buttons read Auto / Fast / Long / Extreme and
fit their buttons; the help text after the strip buttons is gone (the scope stays).
3. Gun window: shorter lines (muzzle velocity source, shell weight, penetration
rows in whole millimetres with striking velocity and angle of fall) and the whole
description in smaller text. Setting (F10, Battle): GunInfoTextSize (1 = the
game's size).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
130. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73-BETA.7 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.268) - TORPEDO ATTACK ORDER NO LONGER RAMS; SHELL TYPE STATISTICS - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.73-beta.6.
1. Torpedo attack order (player divisions): inside torpedo launch range the
division stops closing and holds off at the launch distance, running across the
target's bearing. Before, it kept steering for the target after launch range and
cruisers rammed enemy battleships.
2. Torpedo target priority: a change of target is kept (the log repeated it every
second).
3. Battle log: the battle summary lists the shells fired by nation, calibre and
type (AP / HE), as created and as flying, and logs a shell whose type changed
after it was fired.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
131. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73-BETA.8 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.269) - HIT LOG FOR BOTH SIDES - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.73-beta.7.
1. Battle log: every hit on the player's ships is logged (own limit, so the
player's own fire no longer uses up the hit log), with the type the shell
carries in flight (AP / HE).
2. The armour of each ship (every zone, mm) and its tonnage are logged at the
ship's first hit, for the player's and the AI's ships alike.
3. The hit summary is split by direction (AI on the player, player on the AI) for
the same calibre, shell type and result, so the damage both sides receive can
be compared.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
132. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73-BETA.9 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.270) - TORPEDO TURN-AWAY OPENS THE RANGE - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.73-beta.8.
1. Turning away from torpedoes: of the two courses parallel to the torpedo
tracks the division now takes the one that opens the range from the nearest
enemy ship within 2.5 km. Before, a cruiser combing the tracks ran up to 600 m
from an enemy battleship.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
133. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.73 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.270) - PENETRATION FOR ALL CALIBRES, PASS-THROUGH DAMAGE BY LOCATION, GUN ORDERS, TORPEDO ATTACK WITHOUT RAMMING - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Published from 1.2.73-beta.9 (both lines get every release until the damage
model is finished). Brings to the stable line everything of 1.2.73-beta.1 to
1.2.73-beta.9 (sections 124-132):
1. Damage model: belt penetration recalibrated for all calibres against the
published side-armour penetration of AP guns from 5 to 16 inch; damage of AP
shells passing clean through set by the place the shell went through (upper
works, turret, hull ends, citadel, machinery).
2. Gun orders for the player's divisions: main and secondary battery target
priorities by class.
3. Torpedo attack order: goes in at once at the torpedo target priority, holds
off at launch distance instead of ramming, and turns away on the course that
opens the range from the nearest enemy.
4. Battle strips stay open after a click; smaller division order buttons and
shorter gun window text.
5. Battle dispatches tell where notable hits struck; the battle log records hits
for both sides, the shells fired by type, and the shell shape (nose) of the
player's ships.

133. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74-BETA.2 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.271) - SHELL NOSE FROM THE SHELL FITTED AND THE SHELL TREE - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.73-beta.9 and of stable 1.2.73. 1.2.74-beta.1 was published a few minutes earlier with the same text but still with DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.270 - this build carries the change.
1. Shell shape (air drag of the shell in flight): the AP shell fitted to the ship
decides first - AP, APC and SAP fly as blunt shells, APBC, SAPBC, Improved APBC
and SAPC as ballistic-capped (streamlined) shells. Without a fitted AP shell the
navy's researched shell technologies decide (CPBC, SAPBC, APBC, Improved APBC,
SAPC = ballistic cap; Advanced naval shells = the 1927+ shape). Before, the rule
looked only for two technologies that some navies do not have in their lists,
so their ships always fired blunt shells.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
134. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74-BETA.3 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.272) - PHYSICAL PENETRATION REPLACES THE GAME'S VALUE; HISTORICAL SHELL WEIGHT - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.74-beta.2.
1. Penetration (step 3 of the damage model): the game's penetration value itself
is now the physical one - belt and turrets from the DeMarre-type law with the
striking velocity and the angle of fall, decks from Thompson's F-formula - for
the arriving shell (its own flight), for the AI's estimates and in the gun
window. The armour plate is no longer scaled to it: a plate keeps only its
quality (iron ... Krupp cemented) and the angle of the hit. HE shells keep the
game's HE/AP ratio on top of the physical AP value. Setting (F10, Battle):
PhysicalPenetrationReplace. When the game refuses the patch of this function,
the mod says so in the log and keeps the earlier plate scaling.
2. Shell weight: the physical AP shell is the historical AP shell of the calibre
and gun generation at the bore as built (e.g. 12 in generation 4: 448 kg, 15 in
generation 3: 837 kg), times the ship's shell-weight choice damped to the
historical spread (light / heavy / super heavy about -13 / +13 / +20 %). The
game's own shell weights (12 in 617-695 kg, 15 in 1004-1444 kg) made small
guns out-penetrate large ones. The gun window names the source ("hist." or
"bore"). Settings: PhysicalShellHistoricalWeight, PhysicalShellWeightChoiceScale.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
135. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74-BETA.4 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.273) - HOTFIX: CRASH AT BATTLE START IN BETA.3 - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.74-beta.3.
1. 1.2.74-beta.3 crashed the game at the start of every battle: the replacement
of the game's penetration function (section 134, point 1) hooked a function
with argument types the game cannot be hooked on. The hook is removed and the
setting PhysicalPenetrationReplace is off; the plate scaling of the earlier
method is back in force (same hit results as before beta.3). The physical
penetration will replace the game's value by another route.
2. The historical shell weight (section 134, point 2) stays.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
136. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74-BETA.5 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.274) - PHYSICAL PENETRATION THROUGH THE PENETRATION TABLE - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.74-beta.4.
1. Penetration (step 3, second route): the game reads a gun's penetration from a
row of its penetration table (penetration.csv, by range) and applies its own
modifiers. For every gun and ship the mod now hands the game a private row
holding the physical penetration at every range column (belt: DeMarre-type
law with striking velocity and angle of fall; deck: Thompson's F-formula),
divided by the game's measured modifier, so the game's own reading - at the
hit, in the AI's estimates and in the gun window - is the physical value. HE
keeps the game's HE/AP ratio. The plate is no longer scaled; it keeps its
quality (iron ... Krupp) and the angle of the hit. The hook of beta.3 that
crashed the game is not used. Log at battle start: "physical penetration
replaces the game's value" or the fallback to the earlier plate scaling.
Setting (F10, Battle): PhysicalPenetrationReplace.
2. HE shell weight: the historical AP shell times the game's HE/AP weight ratio
of the gun (beta.4 showed HE heavier than AP).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
137. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74-BETA.6 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.275) - PENETRATION ROWS FIXED AND ON BY DEFAULT - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.74-beta.5.
1. The private penetration rows of beta.5 (section 136) made the game's
penetration reading fail (empty gun window, hits without a penetration
value) because a fresh row lacked the internal range tables the game reads.
The row is now a copy of the game's own row with its own range tables filled
with the physical values. The setting is renamed PhysicalPenetrationRows and
is on by default (the old PhysicalPenetrationReplace had been saved as off by
the beta.4 hotfix).
2. HE shell weight: the HE/AP weight ratio is read from the gun data when the
game reports the same weight for both shell types.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
138. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74-BETA.8 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.276) - DAMAGE DIAGNOSTICS FOR THE DAMAGE MODEL - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.74-beta.6 (1.2.74-beta.7, published minutes earlier, held point 1 only).
Battle log only, no change in play.
1. The game's explosion-damage computation (arguments and result) is logged for
the first 60 hits of a battle together with the shell being resolved (calibre,
weight, striking velocity, physical penetration, gun base damage), and the
gun's base damage is added to every hit line. With the physical penetration
and the real plate (beta.6) the damage of a full penetration grew about three
times (12 in into a destroyer 3,560), because the game scales damage by the
margin of penetration over the plate; these records are the basis for a damage
model from the burst charge and the place of the hit.
2. Hits without penetration: every hit line now carries the game's damage
modifier for its result, fire, flooding and the modules hit; the battle
summary adds a table of the non-penetrating hits by shell type, calibre band,
location and result (hits, mean damage, fires, floodings, hits damaging
modules) - the basis for the model of HE and AP shells bursting on armour
(superstructures, modules, fires, crew instead of a scrap of hull damage).
Setting (F10, Battle): DamageDiagLog.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.74-beta.8.
1. Battle log only: the game's explosion-damage function is now searched in the
whole game assembly (beta.8 looked in a few classes and found nothing, so no
diagnostic line was written). No change in play.

140. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74-BETA.10 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.279) - HISTORICAL DAMAGE MODEL; DELIBERATE AIMING AT CLOSE RANGE - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.74-beta.9.
1. Damage model (steps 3b/3c): the damage of a full penetration is the shell's
base damage (charge and calibre with the ship's technologies, the gun window's
"Damage AP" / "Damage HE") times the place of the burst - citadel 1.0,
machinery or magazine section 1.2, hull ends 0.6, turret or barbette 0.8, upper
works 0.5, +0.1 at or below the waterline. It no longer grows with the margin
of penetration over the plate (which gave up to eleven times the base on
unarmoured targets: 17,050 for one 12 in HE shell into a transport). Pass-throughs
keep their fraction of that by place. An HE shell bursting on a turret or the
upper works does the damage of a penetration there; on the belt or the hull ends
it stays small, as in the historical record. AP bursts on armour unchanged.
Settings (F10, Battle): DamageModelHistorical and the place factors.
2. Deliberate aiming at close range (#42): with a flat trajectory gunners choose
the point of aim - armour-piercing shells at the waterline and the belt, high
explosive at the upper works. The chance that a hit lands where it was aimed
grows as the range shortens (none beyond 6 km) and with the crew's training;
the player's and the AI's ships alike. Gun orders panel: a third strip "Aim"
(Auto = AP waterline / HE upper works, Waterline, Upper works; Shift = this
ship). The orders are heard in the dispatches ("Lay her on the waterline!
Armour-piercing, every gun!"). Settings: AimPoint, AimPointMaxRange,
AimPointChance.
3. Battle log: "[Damage] historical model ..." for the first 40 replaced hits,
"[Aim] ..." for the first 30 hits moved to the point of aim.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
141. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74-BETA.11 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.281) - THE CREWS' VOICES IN THE DISPATCHES; "NONE" HITS DIAGNOSED - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.74-beta.10.
1. Dispatches: the voices of the crews - blood, fear, terror, prayers and
curses, in the language of the war films of the period. Hit reports have
separate wording for grave hits (heavy damage, fire, flooding, heavy shells)
and tell what a burst did in the place it struck (magazine flash, scalded
stokers, a turret crew lost, a wrecked bridge); sinkings, panic, aborted
attacks, ramming, torpedo sightings and torpedo runs got darker variants.
Every dispatch now has at least three variants.
2. Battle log: a hit the game resolves as "none" (registered, but judged
against no armour and dealing no damage - three quarters of the enemy's hits
in the author's battle of 19:36) now records the part and the hull section
the game chose for it, and the battle summary tallies these hits by what
they struck. The first 40 such hits are logged.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
142. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.281) - PHYSICAL PENETRATION IN BATTLE, HISTORICAL DAMAGE MODEL, HISTORICAL SHELL WEIGHT, CREWS' VOICES IN THE DISPATCHES - 2026-10-25
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Published from 1.2.74-beta.11. The last release that both lines get together:
from now on the stable line takes only bug fixes and the features that have
spent three days in the beta without reported problems.
Brings to the stable line everything of 1.2.74-beta.2 to 1.2.74-beta.11
(sections 133-141):
1. Damage model, step 3: the penetration the game uses in battle is the physical
penetration of each gun (striking velocity, angle of fall, shell weight and
shape), written into the game's own penetration table.
2. Damage model, steps 3b/3c: the damage of a full penetration follows the
shell's bursting charge and the place it burst, instead of the margin of
penetration over the plate.
3. Shell weight: the AP shell is the historical shell of the calibre and gun
generation; the HE shell keeps the game's HE/AP weight ratio. The shell shape
(nose) comes from the AP shell fitted to the ship and the shell tree.
4. Deliberate aiming at close range: with a flat trajectory the gunners aim at
the waterline and the turrets.
5. Dispatches carry the crews' voices; the battle log records the damage model
(explosion damage, hits without penetration, "none" hits).

145. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.3 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.283) - BATTLE DISPATCHES IN THE NAVIES' OWN LANGUAGES - 2026-10-25
