147. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.5 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.284) - PENETRATION ON SCREEN AND IN THE AI'S JUDGEMENT FROM THE SAME PHYSICS AS THE HIT - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.4.
1. Gun window in every language: the window with the shell velocities, shell
weights and the belt/deck penetration table now shows the physical values
(the same ones the hits use) in all ten languages of the game. Until now
only the English game got them; in French, German, Russian and the other
languages the window kept the game's own table, which can be several times
lower than what the shell really does. Your own labels stay as they are.
2. AI judgement "can I penetrate": the battle AI decides whether its main
guns can pierce the enemy belt with the same physics as the hit (striking
velocity and angle of fall at that distance, shell weight and AP quality),
not with the game's reading. AI ships no longer turn away from fights they
would win against a modest belt because of a far too low estimate.
3. Main battery: the AI judges with the largest gun on the ship, also when
a main turret failed the mount check - a battleship is no longer rated by
its secondary guns.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
148. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.6 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.285) - DIVISION ORDER "FINISH OFF" - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.5.
1. New division order "Finish off" (seventh button of the orders panel,
Alt+7): the division goes for enemy ships that are already badly damaged
and still afloat - the worst damaged and nearest first. Against ships its
guns cannot sink (capital ships, a bigger calibre) a division with
torpedoes closes to torpedo reach, otherwise it fights at a short gun
range. It keeps clear of the reach of every undamaged enemy that can hurt
it, and when no crippled enemy is in sight it waits by the main body.
Transports remain the business of "Hunt".
2. The usual limits apply: a badly damaged division breaks off and falls
back behind the main body, torpedoes are evaded, and a move or retreat
order given by hand takes command back.
3. F10 / settings: DivisionFinishDamage - from which estimated damage an
enemy ship counts as crippled for this order.
4. In-game help: the battle orders chapter describes the new order.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
149. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.7 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.286) - MISSING RELATIONS BETWEEN GREAT POWERS RESTORED - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.6.
1. Fix: in some campaigns a pair of great powers had no relation record at
all (seen after a great power came back or after loading an older save).
The politics window then left rows of these nations without actions, and
diplomatic actions and the AI's diplomacy between them were impossible.
The campaign is now checked after loading and after every turn, and a
missing pair gets a neutral relation (no war, no alliance) - existing
relations are never changed.
2. F10 / settings: RelationRepair (on by default).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
150. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74.1 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.287) - STABLE FIX: PENETRATION ON SCREEN AND IN THE AI'S JUDGEMENT FROM THE SAME PHYSICS AS THE HIT - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Stable line, bug fix release. The same fixes as 1.2.75-beta.5 (tested in battle on 26 October).
1. Gun window in every language: the shell velocities, shell weights and
the belt/deck penetration table show the physical values the hits use in
all ten languages of the game. Until now only the English game got them;
in the other languages the window kept the game's own table, which can be
several times lower than what the shell really does.
2. AI judgement "can I penetrate": the battle AI decides whether its main
guns can pierce the enemy belt with the same physics as the hit, not with
the game's reading, so AI ships no longer turn away from fights they would
win against a modest belt.
3. Main battery: the AI judges with the largest gun on the ship, also when
a main turret failed the mount check.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
151. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.8 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.288) - TECHNOLOGY SPREAD: A FAR-AHEAD POWER IS SLOWED AGAIN - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.7.
1. Fix: the technology spread rule that slows research of a technology
far ahead of its time compared the technology with the newest one known
by all great powers - the researching power included. A power that had
pulled far ahead of the others therefore never counted as ahead, its
research was never slowed, and the gap to the other navies kept growing
(late campaigns: AI fleets on hulls decades older than the leader's).
The comparison now uses only the other great powers, as intended.
2. Log: once a quarter a line "[Tech] standing" lists every great power's
technology level against the others and its newest technology, so the
gap can be followed over the campaign.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
152. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.9 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.289) - SCREEN RESOLUTION GUARD FOR TWEAKSANDFIXES - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.8.
1. When the game's own display settings fail to load at start, the chosen
resolution pointed outside the list of resolutions. TweaksAndFixes then
raised an error in every frame (a log of tens of megabytes in a few
minutes) and its own interface additions stopped updating for the whole
session. The mod now points the setting at the current screen resolution
in that case - nothing on screen changes, and the errors stop.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
153. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.10 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.290) - PRIZES: SHIPS THAT SURRENDERED IN THE BATTLE - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.9.
1. A ship that struck her colours during a campaign battle was not taken
as a prize: after the battle the mod reported her as "not in this battle"
and left her to the game, which counts her as lost. The game moves a
surrendered ship to the battle's list of sunk ships before the mod's
check, and the check looked only at the list of ships still afloat. The
sunk list is now searched too, so a ship that surrendered in this battle
goes to the winner (or back to her own side if it won) as described in
the prize rules. Ships captured in earlier years that only carry an old
surrender flag are still left alone.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
154. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.11 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.291) - COALITION WARS WITH A BALANCE OF POWER - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.10.
1. A player whose navy had become the strongest could find himself at war
with eight nations at once: every hostile great power kept joining the
coalition every month, even when the coalition was already far stronger,
and small navies joined as full members. Now every great power outside
such a war takes one decision a month, with the reason in the log:
- join: the hostile powers, more readily the more hostile they are and
  the worse the target is doing in its wars, and much less readily once
  the coalition already outweighs the target;
- take the target's side: when the coalition is more than twice as
  strong as the target, a power allied to the target, or one that fears
  the coalition's leader more than the target, may declare war on the
  leader (Britain against a continental hegemon; France and Britain for
  the Ottomans in 1854);
- wait: small navies (below a quarter of the target's fleet) and powers
  with mixed relations stay out; when the target is clearly losing they
  may join the winning side late (Italy 1915, Romania 1916);
- stay neutral: at war elsewhere, bankrupt, or at peace with the target
  for less than 12 months (saved with the campaign).
The start of a coalition is unchanged. The log shows once a month every
nation fighting two or more wars and which rule of the mod started each.
F10, Diplomacy: "CoalitionBalanceOfPower" (off: the previous rule).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
155. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.12 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.292) - ALLIES JOINING A WAR: NO MORE ERROR IN THE ATTITUDE CHANGE - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.11.
1. When a war started and the allies of both sides were drawn in, the
game's attitude change sometimes raised an error. The rest of that step
was then skipped, so further allies could stay out of the war. A change
for a pair of nations without relations is now skipped. An error inside
the change is written to the log with the pair of nations, and the game
goes on with the next ally.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
156. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.13 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.293) - NO MORE MOUNT ERRORS IN BATTLE - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.12.
1. On some hulls a mounting point was left without the ship part it
belongs to. Every check of whether a gun or tower fits there raised an
error with a full trace, hundreds to thousands of times per battle. That
filled the log and cost time in every frame of the check. Such a point
now simply counts as not fitting, and the game's check is not entered.
Points that belong to a part are not affected.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
157. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.14 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.294) - TORPEDO SHOT CHECK IN THE GAME'S UNITS, TRANSPORTS DO NOT COVER A RETREAT - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.13.
1. Ships held their torpedoes at close range against a target moving
away. The check compared a fixed torpedo speed with the target's speed in
the game's own units, which are about three times larger, so the run to
the intercept point came out at tens of kilometres at 1.5 km. The check
now uses the torpedo's real speed with the same computation as the choice
of the torpedo setting.
2. Transports and other unarmed ships no longer "cover the retreat"
between the warships and the pursuers.
3. Clearer battle log: a massed torpedo attack that goes in short of the
full strength is shown as a delaying attack; the reason given for a
withdrawal says why leaving is safe; a charge that goes in at once (by
order, massed or after reloading) no longer shows a meaningless start
distance.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
158. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.15 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.297) - FLOATING PARTS LOWERED, NEWEST TORPEDOES FOR AI DESIGNS, UPDATE CHECK OF THE LOADED DLL - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.14.
1. Some ships, mostly AI designs on other nations' hulls, showed turrets
and superstructures floating above the deck. In battle such a part is
now measured against the hull under it and lowered onto the deck when it
floats more than 1.5 m above it. Only the look changes: the guns fire as
before. The log gives the gap for every such part.
2. Designs made by the ship generator (AI and auto-design) get the
largest torpedo diameter the nation has researched instead of a random
older one (F10, Campaign AI: "AiBestTorpedoDiameter").
3. The update check compares the DLL that is actually running with the
installed package. When the two differ (for example after replacing only
the DLL), it says so and offers the package again.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
159. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.16 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.301) - TORPEDO ATTACK DISTANCE, HUNT AGAINST TRANSPORTS, FLOATING PARTS - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.15.
1. Torpedo attack order: cruisers went in to 1-2 km of a battleship. They
now fire from about 85 % of the torpedo range, start holding off a little
earlier because the target closes too, and with the tubes empty turn away
and open the range until the tubes are reloaded.
2. Torpedoes are no longer held against a battleship, battlecruiser or
heavy cruiser inside three quarters of the torpedo range: a spread at a
target that large covers its turns.
3. Hunt: a cruiser no longer swings between closing in and turning away
on the edge of a covering battleship's reach. It keeps a margin, waits
30 s after turning away and moves to a safe point while the prey is
covered. Transports are approached to about 2.5 km and fired at with HE;
the earlier shell choice comes back when the prey or the order changes.
4. Floating parts: hulls have no surface the check could measure, so the
deck height now comes from the nearest mounting point of the hull itself.
5. The battle log lists for every ship the AP shell fitted and which AP
shells the ship may use (with the game's reason when not), to find why
ships carry the shells of the 1890s.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
160. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.17 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.302) - TORPEDO ATTACK FROM BOTH SIDES AND CROSS ATTACK - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.16.
1. Torpedo attack order: once the tubes on one side have fired, the
division turns so that the loaded side bears on the target and fires it.
Only with both sides empty does it turn away to reload.
2. When two or more divisions attack the same target with torpedoes, they
approach 45 degrees either side of its bow, so the tracks cross at about a
right angle: turning bow-on to one spread puts the target's side to the
other. Ships of one division manoeuvre together, so a cross attack needs
at least two divisions.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
161. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.18 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.303) - DIVISION SPLITS FOR THE CROSS TORPEDO ATTACK - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.17.
1. A division of two or more ships given the torpedo attack order now
splits by itself: the leader keeps the first half, the rest form a second
division with the same order, and the two approach the target from either
side of its bow so the torpedo tracks cross at about a right angle. Once
the detached part has fired its torpedoes, its ships join their division
again, which withdraws and reloads as one.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
162. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.19 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.303) - DIVISION SPLITS FOR THE CROSS TORPEDO ATTACK (CORRECTED PACKAGE) - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.18.
1. 1.2.75-beta.18 was published with the DLL of beta.17 (DIP_GG_Fixes
1.11.302), so the change described in section 161 was not in it. This
package carries it (DIP_GG_Fixes 1.11.303): a division of two or more
ships under the torpedo attack order splits by itself for a cross attack
and joins again after the detached part's salvo.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
163. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.20 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.305) - LIGHT CRAFT TORPEDO LARGE SHIPS, COALITION FIX - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.19.
1. Torpedo attack: against a battleship, battlecruiser or cruiser the
torpedoes are no longer held up to 90% of the torpedo range unless the
target is clearly running away (its course opening the range at more
than half its speed). Before, light cruisers and torpedo boats closed to
4.5-4.7 km of a battleship and did not fire.
2. Coalition wars (#40): two nations fighting a common enemy no longer
go to war with each other or take opposite sides in another war.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
164. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.21 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.306) - SHELL RESEARCH AS IN VANILLA, SLOWER TECHNOLOGY CATCH-UP - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.20.
1. Shell technologies (projectile_shell, the AP and HE shell marks):
research difficulty 160 -> 44, the vanilla value (the last one, 1930:
480 -> 132). DIP's difficulty left the navies of the 1920s firing
blunt-nosed shells of 1898, so striking velocity and penetration at
long range came out at about half of the historical values. The other
technologies keep DIP's difficulty.
2. Technology catch-up: a great power rated "Very Behind" receives one
technology every second turn (was every turn), "Behind" every fourth
turn (was every second).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
165. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.22 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.308) - DAMAGED LIGHT CRAFT STILL TORPEDO, BROKEN AI DESIGNS RETIRED - 2026-10-26
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.21.
1. Torpedo attack: a damaged AI division that cannot get away (too slow
to escape) and still has loaded tubes no longer gives up the attack when
a battleship or cruiser is inside torpedo range - it presses on and
fires. Before, such a light cruiser only fought on with its guns.
2. Broken AI designs (#32): an AI design whose ship comes into battle
with a part lying far from the hull (turrets or superstructure put back
by the mod) is marked broken; the AI builds no more ships from it and
designs a new one. Ships already built stay in service, the player's
designs are never marked, and the marks are kept in the save. Switch:
BrokenDesignsObsolete (F10, Campaign AI).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
166. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.23 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.309) - AI DESTROYERS CARRY A FULL TORPEDO OUTFIT - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.22.
1. AI destroyer designs (#47, second part): a generated destroyer that
carries fewer torpedo tubes than its technology and size allow gets
larger launchers on the same mounts - two of the largest launchers it may
use for a full-size destroyer, one for a small one. Side launchers are
changed in pairs so the ship stays balanced; a mount that cannot take a
larger launcher keeps its own. The extra weight is paid for by the
design's usual weight reduction. Only generated designs (AI and
auto-design); the player's own designs keep the player's choice. Logged
as [ShipGen] ... destroyer with N torpedo tube(s), norm M. Switches:
AiMinTorpedoTubes and AiDestroyerTubesTonnage (F10, Campaign AI).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
167. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.24 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.310) - DESIGN RECORDER - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.23.
1. Design recorder (#64): every step of designing or refitting a ship in
the constructor is written to UserData\IronAdmiral\design_log.csv - hull,
speed, tonnage, range, beam, draught, bulkheads, crew quarters, each
component, the armour of each zone and the parts (towers, funnels, guns,
barbettes, torpedo tubes) with their place fore / midships / aft, and the
weight and tonnage reserve after each step. A slider drag is one step
with its final value. The file stays on your computer. Switch:
DesignRecorder (F10, Interface).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
168. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.25 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.312) - IMPACT ANGLE COUNTED ONCE, DESIGN RECORDER FIXES - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.24.
1. Penetration (#58, #60): in a hit the game itself thickens the plate by
the impact angles (plate / cosine of the horizontal angle x cosine of the
vertical angle). The mod's belt obliquity factor and the reduction of the
physical belt penetration by the angle of fall came on top of it, so the
angle was counted twice: shells were stopped by plates thinner than their
penetration (a 152 mm shell with 63 mm penetration stopped by a "30 mm"
plate) and small shells met plates of 460-690 mm on the hull ends. Both
are off in hits now (the angle is counted once, by the game); the belt
penetration shown and used is the value at normal impact. Switches:
ObliquityOnTopOfGame (off), PhysicalPenetrationNormalIncidence (on).
2. Hit log (#59): each hit line shows the armour zone the game checked,
the impact angles and the plate as sloped by the game.
3. Design recorder (#64): the current design name on every line, a
"design saved" line when a design is saved, fore / midships / aft by
thirds of the ship's length.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
169. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74.2 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.313) - STABLE FIX: SURRENDERED SHIPS TAKEN AS PRIZES AGAIN, RESOLUTION GUARD - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Stable line. Two fixes from the beta line (1.2.75-beta.10 and beta.9),
both confirmed in beta battles and campaigns.
1. Prizes (#35): when a ship strikes her colours the game moves her to
the battle's list of sunk ships at once, and the prize check looked only
among the ships still afloat - she was skipped as "not in this battle"
and the prize was lost. The check now searches every list of the battle,
the sunk list included, so a surrendered ship is taken as a prize again.
2. Resolution guard (#51): when the game's settings fail at start, the
resolution index is set to the current screen, so another mod's
per-frame interface update no longer throws an error in every frame.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
170. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.74.3 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.314) - STABLE FIX: SURRENDERED SHIPS TAKEN AS PRIZES AGAIN (CORRECTED PACKAGE) - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Stable line. Contains everything of 1.2.74.2.
1. 1.2.74.2 was published without the prize fix described in section
169 (the resolution guard was in it). This package carries it: when a
ship strikes her colours the game moves her to the battle's list of sunk
ships at once, and the prize check now searches that list too, so a
surrendered ship is taken as a prize again (#35).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
171. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.26 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.315) - SIDE PENETRATION FOR SIDE PLATES - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.25.
1. Penetration (#59): the game chooses the side or the deck penetration
of a hit by a random roll on the impact angle, independent of the plate
it then checks. In the test of beta.25 a third of the hits on belt
plates (bow and stern belts) read the deck penetration - a 381 mm shell
falling at 7 degrees had 84 mm instead of 469 mm - and was stopped by
thin end plating. A shell that meets a vertical plate (belts, turret
sides, barbettes, conning tower) now always uses the side penetration.
Switch: PhysicalPenetrationByZone (on).
2. Hit log: the plate estimate added in beta.25 is removed (the game's
own slope of the plate has further factors); the zone and the impact
angles stay.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
172. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.27 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.316) - SIDE-PLATE CHANGE OF BETA.26 WITHDRAWN - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.26.
1. The change of section 171 (side penetration for side plates) made the
results worse in the test battle: most hits on belts read far less
penetration instead of more (normal penetrations fell from 171 to 53 in
the same setup). It is switched off (experimental switch
PhysicalPenetrationByZoneTest, off); penetration works as in beta.25.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
173. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.28 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.317) - DECK HITS MEET THE DECK - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.27.
1. Penetration (#59): the game decides by a roll on the impact angle
whether a shell strikes the side or the deck, and uses the side or the
deck penetration accordingly. For deck hits on the bow and stern it still
checked the belt plate, so a shell with its small deck penetration was
stopped by thin end belts (a 381 mm shell at 7 degrees of fall: 85 mm
against 39 mm). A deck hit now meets the deck plate of that part of the
ship (deck, bow deck, stern deck). Switch: DeckHitsMeetDeck (F10, Battle).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
174. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.29 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.319) - AI CROSS TORPEDO ATTACK AND TWO-SIDE SALVO - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.28.
1. AI torpedo tactics (#56): AI divisions attacking the same target with
torpedoes approach 45 degrees either side of its bow, so the tracks cross
and the target cannot comb both salvos; a division whose tubes on one
side are empty turns to fire the loaded side before going off to reload
- the same tactics as the player's torpedo attack order. Switch:
AiCrossTorpedo (F10, Battle AI).
2. Hit log: each hit line shows whether the game used AP or HE for the
hit (the shells fly as "Auto" and the kind is decided in the hit).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
175. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.30 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.320) - IMPACT ANGLE FROM THE SHELL'S FALL - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.29.
1. Penetration (#58, #59): the game uses one "side-up" impact angle both
to roll whether a shell strikes the side or the deck and to thicken the
plate it meets (plate / cosine of the angle, up to 85 degrees). For a
shell falling at 5-15 degrees the game's angle came out at 75-89
degrees, so flat shells were mostly made deck hits, and when they did
strike a side plate it counted 4-11 times thicker (a 152 mm AP shell with
110 mm penetration stopped by a 39 mm end belt hit almost square). This
angle is now the shell's angle of fall from its physical flight: a flat
shell meets a side plate almost square and mostly strikes the side, a
plunging one is more likely to strike the deck. Switch:
ImpactAngleFromFall (F10, Battle).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
176. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.31 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.321) - IMPACT ANGLE FROM THE SHELL'S FALL, CORRECTED - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.30.
1. The change of section 175 did not act: the game rolls a hit when the
gun fires, before the shell exists, so the shell's own flight was not at
hand (applied 0 times in the test). The angle of fall is now taken from
the impact direction the game itself passes to its angle function, at
the firing and at the impact alike.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
177. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.32 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.322) - LIGHT SHIPS TAKE DAMAGE AGAIN - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.31.
1. Torpedo boats, destroyers and light cruisers took almost no damage
(forum reports; in a test a torpedo boat took 72 hits from 2 to 12 in
and ended at 6 % damage). The game gives small ships far more structure
per ton than battleships and made up for it by multiplying hits on thin
plates (up to x11); the historical damage model had removed that
multiplier. Every hit on a light ship is now multiplied by its structure
per ton over that of the capital ships in the battle (x1 to x12), so a
shell destroys the same amount of tonnage in any ship: a hit that costs
a 30,000 t battleship 5 % costs a 1,500 t destroyer the whole ship. Switches LightShipDamage, LightShipDamageMax and
LightShipTypes (F10, Battle). (#67)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
178. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.33 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.323) - DAMAGE IN PROPORTION TO TONNAGE FOR EVERY CLASS - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.32.
1. The rule of section 177 now applies to every ship, whatever its class:
each hit is multiplied by the ship's structure per ton over that of the
battleships and battlecruisers in the battle (from x0.5 to x12), so a
shell destroys the same amount of tonnage in any ship - torpedo boat,
destroyer, cruiser or battleship (5 % of a 30,000 t battleship is the
whole of a 1,500 t destroyer). The switches are renamed
DamageByTonnage, DamageByTonnageMin, DamageByTonnageMax and
DamageByTonnageTypes (all classes by default; F10, Battle). The battle
log names each ship's multiplier and sums the damage by ship type. (#67)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
179. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.34 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.324) - DAMAGE BY TONNAGE: CAP X60 - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.33.
1. The game gives a 635 t torpedo boat more structure (12,594) than a
25,796 t battleship (11,787) - 43 times as much per ton. The x12 cap of
section 178 left torpedo boats at 1-15 % damage after up to 13 hits.
The largest multiplier (DamageByTonnageMax) is now x60: in two test
battles 3 of 8 torpedo boats sank after 10-21 hits and 4 were wrecks
(46-96 %). (#67)
2. New switches StructureSinkPercent and DestroyedSectionDamagePenalty
(F10, Battle) set the game's parameters for sinking from structure
damage (structure_sink: vanilla 40, DIP GG 100) and for hits on an
already destroyed section (0.15). Default -1 keeps the game values;
they are for the destroyer and cruiser tests. (#67)

180. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.35 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.325) - TORPEDOES NOT MULTIPLIED BY TONNAGE - 2026-10-27
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.34.
1. The damage-by-tonnage multiplier no longer applies to torpedo hits.
In a test one torpedo sank a 14,786 t armoured cruiser: the game's
torpedo damage (11,359, about a battleship's whole structure) x1.88 was
more than the cruiser's whole structure. Torpedo damage stays as the
torpedo technologies give it. The battle summary lists the hits by the
game's damage type. (#67)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
181. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.36 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.326) - SHIPS SINK AT 40 % STRUCTURE DAMAGE - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.35.
1. A ship now sinks when 40 % of its structure is destroyed (the vanilla
value; DIP GG has 100 %). With 100 % a 2,577 t destroyer stayed afloat
after 136 hits. Test battles with the damage-by-tonnage rule and 40 %
(27-28.10) against the historical record:
- torpedo boats (635 t): all sunk after 3-10 hits (history 5-10);
- destroyers (2,000-2,600 t): sunk after 19-36 hits, one with 11 hits
  survived (Hoel 2,100 t sank after about 40; Sterett survived 11 x 5 in);
- light cruisers (4,285 t): sunk after 14 (5 heavy), 40 and 51 hits;
- armoured cruisers: one 15,000 t ship sunk after 146 hits (Bluecher:
  70-100 heavy hits and torpedoes), a 10,700 t ship a wreck at 66 % after
  61 hits (Exeter: 7 x 11 in);
- battleships: sunk after 23-33 heavy hits (Luetzow: 24, Seydlitz
  survived 21 and a torpedo); about 60 medium hits leave a battleship
  almost undamaged (South Dakota: 27); one torpedo 5-6 %.
StructureSinkPercent (F10, Battle): -1 = 40 (default), 100 = DIP GG. (#67)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
182. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.37 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.327) - 7 IN GUNS NO LONGER SLOWER THAN 8 IN - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.36.
1. The historical rate of fire had the WWI 7 in guns (Mk 3) at 3.3 rounds
a minute against 5.0 for 8 in - a 7.9 in gun (the 7 in gun enlarged)
reloaded far slower than an 8 in gun (forum report). Below 11 in a
calibre now fires at least as fast as every heavier calibre of the same
generation: Mk 1 7 in 2 -> 2.4, Mk 2 9 in 2 -> 2.5, Mk 3 7 in 3.3 -> 5
rounds a minute. Heavy guns keep their measured rates. The game's own
reload penalty for an enlarged bore stays. (#68)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
183. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75-BETA.38 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.328) - DAMAGE BY TONNAGE ALSO WITHOUT BATTLESHIPS - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. Contains everything of 1.2.75-beta.37.
1. In a battle without battleships or battlecruisers (cruisers and light
forces only) and with no value saved from an earlier battle, the damage-
by-tonnage rule had no reference and multiplied nothing. Such battles
now use 0.40 structure per ton, the value of the battleships in the
tests of 27-28.10. (#67)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
184. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.75 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.328) - SHIPS SINK BY THEIR SIZE, IMPACT ANGLE FROM THE SHELL'S FALL, TORPEDO TACTICS, DIVISION ORDER "FINISH OFF", COALITION WARS - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Published from 1.2.75-beta.38 (author's decision 28.10). Brings to the
stable line everything of 1.2.75-beta.2 to 1.2.75-beta.38 (sections 144-183
without the stable fixes 150, 169 and 170, which the stable line already has):
1. Damage by tonnage: a shell destroys the same amount of tonnage in any ship.
The game gives every ship almost the same structure (a 635 t torpedo boat as
much as a 25,800 t battleship); each hit is now multiplied by the ship's
structure per ton over that of the battleships (x0.5 to x60; torpedoes not
multiplied), and a ship sinks at 40 % structure damage as in vanilla (DIP GG
100 %). In test battles torpedo boats sink after 3-10 hits, destroyers after
12-45, battleships after 14-33 heavy hits, while medium guns barely scratch
a battleship - as in the historical record (#67).
2. Impact angle: the angle a shell meets the side is taken from its fall, the
slope of the plate is counted once, deck hits meet the deck plate (#58, #59,
#60). Heavy AP shells that should pierce a belt now do (91 % against 73 %).
3. Torpedo tactics of the AI: torpedo boats and cruisers attack large ships up
to 90 % of the torpedo range, damaged light craft press the attack (#36);
divisions attacking the same target come in 45 degrees either side of its
bow so the tracks cross, and a division whose tubes on one side are empty
turns to fire the other side (#56, seen in the test of 28.10); AI destroyers
carry a full torpedo outfit (#47).
4. Division order "Finish off" (Alt+7): light divisions go for crippled enemies.
5. Campaign: coalition wars keep a balance of power; missing relations between
great powers restored; technology spread slows a far-ahead power, catch-up
of a backward power is slower (very behind every 2 turns, behind every 4);
shell research as in vanilla; broken AI designs are retired (#32).
6. Gunnery: the gun window and the AI's can-I-penetrate judgement use the same
physics as the hit; 7 in guns no longer fire slower than 8 in (#68).
7. Battle dispatches in the navies' own languages; 33 more historical classes;
design recorder (every step of designing a ship written to a local file, #64).
8. Fixes: no more mount errors in battle, floating parts lowered onto the hull,
allies joining a war, screen resolution guard, update check of the loaded DLL.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
185. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.1 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.328) - BETA LINE CONTINUES FROM STABLE 1.2.75 - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beta line only. The same code as stable 1.2.75 (section 184); the beta line
now counts from it, so a beta installation no longer shows an older number
than the stable release.

186. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.2 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.329) - SETTINGS PANEL TABS, NUMBERS, HELP CHAPTERS, DEFAULTS ON NEW VERSION - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Settings panel (F10): 13 tabs in rows of five - Battle AI, Gunnery &
damage, Fog of war, Crews & prizes, Campaign AI, Ship design, Economy,
Diplomacy, Display, Logs, Info & updates, Tools. Every switch now sits in
its own tab: 71 switches that landed in "Other" are sorted into the tabs,
and four switches added in 1.11.317-1.11.328 (DamageByTonnage,
DeckHitsMeetDeck, ImpactAngleFromFall, DesignRecorder) were in groups
without a tab and could not be seen at all - they are now in Gunnery &
damage and Ship design. The author's test tools and one-time migration
flags are no longer shown.

Numbers: every switch has a small grey number before its name (#1 to
#160), the same as in the Help chapters, so a post can say "switch #65".
Rows are listed in number order.

In-game Help (Iron Admiral): a new chapter "Settings panel (F10)" describes
the window, tabs, numbers, pages and the defaults button, and the chapters
"Settings #a-b: <tab>" list every switch with its default, what it turns
on ([ON]) and what the game does without it ([OFF]).

New version = defaults: the first time a new version of the mod starts,
every switch you changed goes back to its default once, so new and
reworked mechanics get tried (a switch turned off once was never seen
again). Info & updates and Tools keep your choice. The panel footer and
Latest.log list the switches that were reset; the cfg entry
ResetSwitchesOnNewVersion = false turns this off.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
187. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.3 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.330) - DIVISION ORDERS PANEL FOLDED, AI FLOTILLAS WITHOUT TORPEDOES KEEP CLEAR - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Division orders panel (#69, player report): the panel at the top of the
battle screen starts folded to a small tab, so it no longer covers the
view of the enemy. F5 (or a click on the tab / the arrow) folds and
unfolds it; the state is kept for the next battles. The key can be changed
in the cfg (DivisionOrdersPanelKey). The Alt+1..7 order keys work either
way.

AI flotillas without torpedoes (#70, player report: AI destroyers with no
torpedoes left and damaged kept chasing the player's battleships and were
sunk by their secondaries): an AI torpedo boat, destroyer or light cruiser
division with no torpedoes left, with no reloads, or too damaged for a
charge, keeps out of the reach of the enemy capital ships (it turns away
and lays smoke when close) instead of being left to the game's AI. It
still fights enemy light forces with its guns, but no longer goes for a
light ship sailing next to an enemy capital ship.

Settings panel (F10): rows are taller, so descriptions of three lines no
longer run into the next row.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
188. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.4 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.331) - AI TORPEDO CRAFT AIM AT CAPITAL SHIPS - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
AI torpedo craft (#71, player report: AI destroyers in a good firing
position sometimes did not launch): the game aims the tubes of an AI ship
at a target of its own choice, often the nearest destroyer; the rule
against hopeless torpedo shots then held the torpedoes as "a small target"
while a battleship was within reach. AI torpedo boats, destroyers and
light cruisers now aim their tubes at the nearest enemy battleship,
battlecruiser or heavy cruiser in torpedo reach; with none in reach the
game's choice stays. New switch #161 AiTorpedoCapitalsFirst (Battle AI
tab, on by default).

Settings panel (F10): switch numbers no longer change between versions. A
switch added later gets the next free number and is listed at the end of
its tab (here #161).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
189. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.5 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.332) - PRIZE CREW, DESIGNER AND BUILDER ERROR FIXES - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Prizes (#61): a captured ship keeps the prize crew put aboard. The game's
DamageShip, called after the capture, took crew in proportion to the
damage (a captured battleship went from 70% to 0% crew and LowCrew); the
crew is now put back and a LowCrew prize goes to Repairing like the
others.

Designer outside a campaign (#62): the shipbuilding capacity limit is not
asked from the game when no campaign is loaded (it failed on the missing
province data and wrote about 180 errors per builder or designer
session); without a campaign there is no limit.

TweaksAndFixes generator (#63): an index error in TweaksAndFixes'
ResetTorpGrade during ship generation is caught and noted once instead of
an error stack in the log.

Broken mounts (#65): a fit check against a destroyed mount or part answers
"does not fit" instead of throwing (the historical builder met about
85,000 such errors in 15 minutes on French pre-dreadnought hulls and the
game exited). The historical builder gives up a class after 15 minutes or
2,000 broken mount checks and moves on.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
190. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.6 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.333) - CAPTURED TECHNOLOGY, SHIPYARD LOG, MAP GHOST CHECK - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Captured technology (#50, player report: a captured, more advanced ship
was refitted, copied, and new ships were built with guns, armour and fire
control the nation had not researched): the parts of a prize that the
captor has not researched are noted when the prize's design is copied to
the captor. The prize keeps them (refits of that ship are unchanged), but
a new design or a copy of the prize design with such a part is not valid
until the part is researched - the designer marks the part in red with
the reason "captured technology - not researched by your nation". New
switch #162 CapturedTechNotCopied (Ship design tab, on by default).

Shipyard expansion log (#48, #49, player reports: the expansion plan
showed a different gain than the game applied; does the AI expand its
shipyards on the same terms as the player): one [Shipyard] line in the
log when a great power, player or AI, starts or finishes an expansion -
months, monthly cost and its share of the naval budget, capacity and the
largest ship before and after.

Campaign map ghost parts (#52): a part of a design object drawn on the
world map is now written to the log with its position and scale (not
hidden yet) - a log with such a line and a screenshot of a floating
turret identifies the source.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
191. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.7 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.334) - HOUR OF CAMPAIGN BATTLES - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Hour of campaign battles (#57): the side whose force is faster - the
speed it can keep together, i.e. its slowest ship, at least 3 knots
faster - and better informed about the other (contact report quality:
era optics and signalling, recon technology, intelligence budget, own
scouts) chooses when to fight. Historically the faster side set the terms
and night was sought or avoided by doctrine (Jutland, the Japanese night
doctrine, US War Instructions 1944 ch. 8).
- You choose in a small window over the battle screen: dawn (a full day
  of light, the heavier line counts most), day, dusk (the battle runs on
  into the night) or night (destroyers and torpedoes count more, heavy
  guns less), or leave it to chance.
- The AI chooses by its doctrine and the balance of forces: a doctrine
  seeking night action, or a weaker battle line with torpedo craft ->
  night; a stronger battle line -> dawn; a doctrine avoiding night -> day.
  The window tells you what the enemy chose and why.
- With a large reconnaissance edge a dusk or night battle starts as an
  ambush: the chooser's ships open at about 10 km from the other force
  (Savo Island 1942, Coronel 1914).
- Neither side able to choose: the game's hour stays.
New switch #163 BattleHourChoice (Campaign AI tab, on by default); cfg
numbers BattleHourSpeedKn, BattleHourReconMargin, BattleHourAmbushMargin,
BattleHourAmbushKm.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
192. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.8 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.335) - TORPEDO CHECK FOR YOUR SHIPS ON BY DEFAULT - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Switch #38 TorpedoSensePlayer is now on by default: your ships under
automatic torpedo fire hold hopeless shots as the AI does - a target
beyond torpedo range, one that outruns the torpedo, or a small ship far
off and not closing. Inside 1.5 km and on a large target within reach the
torpedoes are always fired; shots you order yourself are never held. With
the new version the switch is set to on once for everyone (Battle AI tab;
switch it off there if you prefer the game's own automatic fire).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
193. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.9 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.336) - NO LANDLOCKED REUNIFICATION, TORPEDO SIDE CHECK REDESIGNED - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Reunification (#7: after loading a save Austria-Hungary was back with 2
provinces, no ports and no ships): the national cores of Austria-Hungary
are the two inland provinces Austria and Hungary, so a reunification gave
a great power without a coast. A dissolved great power whose national
cores have no port no longer reunites; the successor states stay
(log: "no reunification as a naval great power").

Torpedo side check (#13, switch TorpedoSideCheck, still off by default):
the side of each launcher group now comes from the ship's own launchers -
when every torpedo launcher sits off the centre line, the right-side group
fires to starboard and the left-side group to port, and an aim of a group
at a target on the other side is dropped. Ships with a centre-line launcher
are left alone. The old way took the side from the gun aims and
"confirmed" a side whenever all enemies lay on one beam. The launcher
layout of each ship is written to the log; the switch stays off until a
test confirms the new check.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
194. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.10 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.337) - AI TORPEDO AIM FIX, BUILDER CALIBRE APPROXIMATION - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
AI torpedo aims (#71, test 28.10): the aim of AI torpedo craft at the
nearest capital ship (1.11.331) was set again every second - the game
clears an AI ship's torpedo aim while it has no target of its own, and
each new aim restarted the aiming from zero, so the tubes could not come
to bear. An AI ship is now retargeted only away from a smaller ship the
game chose, and keeps its aiming progress.

Historical builder (#14): when the game offers no gun of the class's
historical calibre (or one up to 0.9 in smaller, the designer's bore
limit), the builder uses the largest gun up to 2 in smaller with its bore
increased and notes the approximation (Hoche 1891: 13.4 in built as
12.9 in from the 12 in gun France had that year), instead of giving up
the class.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
195. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.11 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.338) - SHIP SOLD AT SEA HANDED OVER IN PORT - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ship trade (#16): a ship sold while she is at sea is no longer handed over
in the middle of the ocean. The sale is agreed, and the ship is handed
over - and the price paid - when she is back in port. The sale lapses if
the ship is lost, if she is not back in port within a year, or if buyer
and seller go to war. Log: "[Trade] sale agreed ... will be handed over
in port".
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
196. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.12 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.339) - PRIZE DESIGNS SHARED BY CLASS, TORPEDO DIVISIONS KEEP TOGETHER - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Prize designs (#72, player report: 3 captured light cruisers of one class
came with 3 identical designs, each linked to one ship, so every refit had
to be designed 3 times; 10 captured torpedo boats came as 1/1/8 designs):
a captured ship is linked to the copy of her class the captor already has
(same design name, hull and tonnage), and identical copies no ship uses
are removed. Designs left over from earlier captures stay in the save.

Torpedo divisions (#71, player report and test: when the leading ship of
an AI division took light damage and lost a little speed, the division
was split in the middle of its torpedo run - the faster ships went on as a
new division and started over): a division that is attacking with
torpedoes, turning away to reload or coming back to the attack keeps
together. Slow ships are separated before or after the attack.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
197. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.13 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.341) - CONTINUOUS MAP: ROUTE LINES BEYOND THE EDGE, AI SMOKE ONLY WHEN NEEDED - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Continuous world map (#11, switch ContinuousMap, Campaign AI tab, still
off by default - beta feature): the route lines and destination markers
of fleets are now also drawn on the copies of the map beyond its edge, so
a fleet ordered near the date line shows its route on the side you are
looking at. Still open: choosing the lap on a click, the battle window,
and a full campaign test with the switch on.

AI smoke screens (#22, player suggestion; new switch #164
AiSmokeOnlyWhenNeeded, Battle AI tab, on by default): the game's own AI
laid smoke as soon as it could, often far outside the range of any enemy
gun, so the smoke was gone when it was needed and hid the AI's own
targets. The AI now lays smoke only when a visible enemy within 15 km has
the ship in its gun range. Smoke laid by the mod's battle AI (turning
away after a torpedo attack, covering a retreat) and by you is not
affected. The log shows each decision in lines starting with [Smoke].
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
198. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.14 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.344) - AI TORPEDO ATTACKS FROM AHEAD AND ASTERN, SMOKE IN THE APPROACH - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
AI torpedo attacks (#71). In a test, AI destroyers and cruisers that
charged two battleships from abeam at 9 km lost 5 of 6 ships in about
4 minutes and scored 0 and 1 torpedo hits.
- From ahead and astern (new switch #165 AiTorpedoPincer, Battle AI tab,
  on by default): an AI flotilla faster than the enemy line works ahead
  of it and attacks the leading ship from fine on its bow - a narrow
  target, the fastest closing, the shortest time under fire. A slower
  flotilla takes the last ship from its quarter. A flotilla of 4 or more
  ships splits in two when the charge starts: one half attacks from
  ahead, the other checks the line from astern.
- Smoke in the approach (new switch #166 AiApproachSmoke, Battle AI tab,
  on by default): a flotilla that is hit during its run-in lays smoke at
  once, every 30 s at most while it keeps being hit.
- An AI ship that is hit, or whose division is losing ships, fires at a
  battleship or cruiser with the full torpedo range instead of waiting
  under fire for a shorter run.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
199. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.15 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.345) - AI WITHDRAWS WHEN OUT OF AMMUNITION - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
AI ammunition (#22, from a player discussion; new switch #167
AiWithdrawLowAmmo, Battle AI tab, on by default): an AI division whose
guns have less than 15 % of their ammunition left on average, and no
loaded torpedo tubes, now withdraws from the battle instead of staying
in it with empty magazines. If it cannot outrun its pursuers it fights
on with what is left.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
200. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.16 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.346) - AI OUT OF AMMUNITION = NO MAIN-BATTERY SHELLS - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
AI ammunition (#22, switch #167 AiWithdrawLowAmmo): "out of ammunition"
now means no shells left at all for the main battery (the guns of the
largest calibre). An AI division withdraws only when every one of its
ships has 0 such shells and it has no loaded torpedo tubes. Beta.15 used
"under 15 % of all gun ammunition", which sent ships home too early.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
201. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.17 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.353) - DISPATCH PANEL, PRIZE SCUTTLING, ONE DESIGN PER CAPTURED CLASS - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Dispatch panel in battle (new switch #168 BattleDispatchPanel, Display
tab, on by default): the reports of hits, damage, torpedo attacks,
identification and withdrawals stay on the screen in a panel on the
left, newest first, with the battle clock. It sits in the free space
between the game's Shoot Info and Report panels and shows as many
reports as fit there; F6 folds it. Before, a report was shown only for
9 seconds above its ship, often off the screen.

Surrendered wrecks and scuttling (new switch #169 PrizeScuttle, Crews &
prizes tab, on by default): a ship that struck her colours with 90 % or
more damage no longer reaches port as a prize - her crew scuttles her,
or she founders under tow. Below that, her crew may still scuttle her
rather than hand her over; the better trained the crew, the more likely
(up to 60 %). The battle report says so.

Captured ships of one class share one design (#72): a captured class was
copied once per ship (four captured Matsushima cruisers gave four
identical designs, two captured Hibernia battleships two). The copies
are now one design, and the existing duplicates in a campaign are merged
once when it is loaded.

Division orders panel (#69): with an enemy ship or nothing selected the
panel now shows your last selected division, or your first one, instead
of disappearing.

AI torpedo attacks (#71): in a two-way attack the torpedo tubes of the
AI ships are laid on the ship the division is attacking - before, the
game kept them on another ship and the flotilla held its torpedoes.

Battle summary (#73): the log lists, for each ship hit, the damage by
calibre and result, to check how much heavy and medium hits take from a
battleship.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
202. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.18 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.354) - DAYLIGHT TORPEDO ATTACKS FROM CLOSER, BATTLE HOUR FIX - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
AI massed torpedo attacks by day (#71): in a campaign test the AI threw
its flotillas at the enemy line from 32-39 km in daylight - 15
torpedoes, no hit; in the next battle 51 torpedoes, one hit. Historically
daylight attacks hit with about 3 % of their torpedoes, and launches
from beyond 8 km about as rarely. By day a massed attack now waits until
the target is within twice the flotilla's own start distance (at least
twice its torpedo range); at night it still goes in at once.

Attack from ahead (#71): a flotilla now takes the leading ship from
ahead with a 5 % speed edge (before 15 %), and also when it is already
ahead of the leading ship's beam and not more than 10 % slower - the
target comes to it.

Battle hour (#57): the choice of the hour read 0 knots for both fleets
on the pre-battle screen, so nobody could ever choose. It now uses the
ships' design top speed.

Turn end (game): when the game resolved a land battle it asked for the
shell weight of a gun without a ship, and the game and TweaksAndFixes
threw about 120 errors in a row. Those calls now return 0 quietly.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
203. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.19 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.355) - MERGE OF CAPTURED-CLASS DESIGNS AFTER LOADING - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Captured classes (#72): the one-time merge of identical designs of a
captured class (beta.17) ran while the campaign was still loading and
failed, so the duplicates stayed. It now waits until the campaign has
loaded and tries again if it fails.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
204. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.20 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.356) - DISPATCH PANEL ON THE SCREEN - 2026-10-28
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Dispatch panel (switch #168): on some screens the game's Shoot Info
element is as large as the whole screen and the Report element has no
size, so the panel was placed below the bottom edge (only its tab
"Dispatches", if anything, was visible). Elements of that kind are now
ignored, and the panel takes the gap between Shoot Info and Report on
the left of the screen (53-75 % of the height).

205. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.21 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.357) - SMALLER TEXT IN THE DESIGNER PART PANEL - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ship designer: on large screens the text in the part information panel
was too big and did not fit. New setting DesignerPartTextScale (Display
tab, default 0.7) scales only the text of that panel; 1.0 leaves it as
the game draws it. Nothing else in the designer or the battle changes.

206. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.22 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.358) - TWO-WAY TORPEDO ATTACK BY TWO FLOTILLAS - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
AI torpedo attack from two directions (switch #165): until now only a
flotilla of 4 or more ships split to attack from two sides. Flotillas
of 3 never split, so two of them both went for the leading ship from
ahead. Now, when one flotilla already takes the leading ship of a line
from ahead and nobody takes the last one, the next flotilla attacking
the same line takes the last ship from the quarter.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
207. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.23 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.361) - TORPEDO ATTACK ORDER, OCCUPATION COSTS SHOWN, SLOW UPDATES - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Torpedo attack order (Alt+4): your division now approaches like the AI
flotillas - a faster division takes the leading enemy ship fine on her
bow, a slower one the last ship from the quarter, a second division on
the same line takes the last ship, and smoke goes up when hits start
during the run-in. The split in two for an attack from two sides can be
switched for each division with the new panel button "Torpedo: split in
two / one group"; the default is the new switch #170
DivisionTorpedoSplit (on).
Occupation (#78): the decision window shows the standing garrison cost
already taken this month for all occupied core provinces, and says that
Pay costs more on top of it; the costs themselves are unchanged.
Automatic update (#82): the download may take up to 15 minutes (was 120
seconds), for slow connections.
Military conflicts (#77): every calculation of the chance to succeed is
written to the log with its inputs (for checking the game's formula).
Other mods: CarrierPrototype is recognised; the log lists game methods
that Iron Admiral and another mod both change.

208. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.24 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.363) - ORDERS HIDDEN WHILE MOVING THE CAMERA, NO OUTDATED DESIGNS IN CUSTOM BATTLES - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Division orders: the orders panel and the order tags above your divisions
hide while you work the camera (right or middle mouse button, or a camera
key held) and come back a moment after you let go.
Custom battles (#80): a shared design more than 15 years older than the
battle year is no longer used - the ship generator designs a ship for the
year instead (setting CustomBattleDesignMaxAge, 0 = any age). Before, a
1916 battle could field a pre-dreadnought and 1890s cruisers.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
209. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.25 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.365) - SUPERPOSED GUNS RELOAD SLOWER, TRANSPORTS AS FAST AS THEIR YEAR - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Superposed guns (#81, switch #171 StackedGunsPenalty): a gun with another
gun mounted almost directly above it (as on the US Kearsarge and Virginia
classes) reloads 25 % slower for every gun above it - the blast of the
upper guns disturbs its crew. Ordinary superfiring turrets, set fore or
aft of the lower one, are not affected.
Transports (#79, switch #172 TransportSpeedByYear): in battle a
transport is as fast as cargo ships of its year - up to 1899 9 kn,
1900s 10, 1910s 11, 1920s 12, from 1930 14 kn, some up to 1 kn slower -
instead of 11-14 kn in every era.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
210. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.26 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.366) - MIXED CONVOYS - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Convoys (#79): the game arms transports by national technology, so a
convoy was armed or unarmed as a whole. Now about a third of a convoy's
armed transports keep their guns (at least one per side, like an
auxiliary cruiser); the others sail as unarmed merchant ships and do not
fire (setting TransportArmedShare, 1 = all armed as before).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
211. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.27 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.367) - CUSTOM BATTLES: NO PRE-DREADNOUGHTS IN 1916 - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Custom battles (#80): a test of the 1916 setup still gave the US side the
historical class Mississippi (BB-23) of 1908 - a pre-dreadnought, the
newest US battleship class in the set. In custom battles a historical
class is now chosen only when it is at most 6 years older than the
battle (setting CustomBattleHistoricalYearsBack; the campaign keeps
HistoricalDesignsYearsBack), and other shared designs at most 8 years
(CustomBattleDesignMaxAge, was 15); older ones are left to the ship
generator, which designs a ship for the year.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
212. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.28 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.369) - FLOODING THROUGH THE BOW, AI SIZES UP ITS ENEMIES - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Damage (#73): an AP shell passing clean through the bow or stern at the
waterline floods more - the two holes let the sea in (as Bismarck's bow at
the Denmark Strait). A bow hole floods faster the faster the ship goes
ahead and slower when she stops or goes astern; a stern hole the other
way round (setting OverPenWaterlineEndsMult, 0 = off).
Campaign AI (#84, step 1, switch #173 EnemyProfileLog): once a month
every AI great power writes to the log its likely enemies (at war or bad
relations), their largest guns, battle-line speed, cruisers and torpedo
craft, and the design targets and build mix it would take. Log only for
now; designs fitted against the enemy follow.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
213. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.29 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.371) - THANKS ON THE MAIN MENU, NO 1891 HULLS IN 1916 CUSTOM BATTLES - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Thanks (switch #174 ThanksOnMainMenu): the players who test, report and
record for Iron Admiral are named on the main menu and in the log at
start (Mods\IronAdmiral_THANKS.txt, updated with the forum reviews).
Custom battles (#80): a shared design is judged by the older of its
design year and its hull's technology year - a 1916 battle no longer
gets a light cruiser on the 1891 hull.

214. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.30 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.372) - AIR STRIKE PROTOTYPE (TEST SWITCH) - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes, prototype (#85, switch #175 AirStrikeTest, off by default):
in daylight, waves of torpedo aircraft of the AI side come in from
beyond the horizon and attack the largest ship of the player's side
from both beams. Their torpedoes are real game torpedoes. Light guns of
2-3 in on ships within 4 km can shoot the aircraft down - weak fire in
the 1910s, strong in the 1940s; guns over 3 in fire splash barrages
into the sea ahead of aircraft running in low. No strikes at night. A first step
towards carriers that fight from beyond the horizon.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
215. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.31 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.375) - AI FITS ITS BUILDING TO ITS ENEMIES, QUIET MERCHANT TRANSPORTS, AIR STRIKE TUNING - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Campaign AI (#84, switch #176 EnemyFitBuildMix): an AI great power fits
its building to its likely enemies - more torpedo craft against a
slower or poorly screened battle line or a battleship race it would
lose, more light cruisers against many enemy torpedo craft. Enemies and
targets change slowly: bad relations count after 6 months in a row (war
at once), an enemy is dropped after 12 calm months, and the targets
change at most once a year and only on a real shift. No design is
deleted because enemies change.
Convoys (#79): a transport sailing as an unarmed merchant ship fires
no torpedoes either.
Air strikes, test switch #175 (#85): anti-aircraft fire tuned down to
historical losses (the first test lost 4 of 6 aircraft before they
dropped); waves keep coming after the attacking surface ships are gone;
every drop and each wave's result are logged.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes, test switch #175 (#85): the aircraft fly and aim at the
battle's own scale (the battle runs ships and torpedoes faster than real
time) - they keep up with a moving ship, come in abeam of where it will
be and aim their torpedoes at the true intercept point; before, they
dropped 1.6 km out at 47 deg off the bow and missed. New setting
AirStrikeScreenshots: a series of screenshots of every strike.

The aircraft are now biplanes built after the drawings: a Sopwith Cuckoo

wires, a turning propeller, roundels and the torpedo under the fuselage.

222. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.38 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.384) - OUR CARRIER IN CUSTOM BATTLES 1917-1925 - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Custom battles (#85, switch #177 CustomBattleCarrier, on): in a custom
battle of 1917-1925 - the years of the Sopwith Camel, which flew from
HMS Furious against Tondern in July 1918 - the player has a carrier
beyond the horizon. By day its torpedo aircraft attack the enemy's
largest ship in waves from both beams; the enemy's light guns and the
splash barrage of its heavier guns can bring them down. Pick your own
designs of the period in the custom battle and watch the strike.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
223. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.39 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.386) - AERIAL WARHEADS, CAMERA ON AIRCRAFT AND TORPEDOES - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): a torpedo dropped by an aircraft hits with an aerial
warhead - x0.5 of the ship torpedo's damage up to 1929, x0.6 in the
1930s, x0.75 from 1940 (the first custom battle sank a battleship with
five hits of the first wave). During a strike three buttons at the
bottom of the screen move the camera: behind an aircraft, behind a
torpedo just above the water (its wake ahead), or back to the free
camera. The aircraft size can be set next to switch #177 in F10.
Every wave in the air has an identification label (flight, number
and type of aircraft); its pilots' radio talk shows in a bubble under
it at the run-in, the drop, hits and losses, and goes to the dispatch
log with the carrier's and lookouts' reports.

Custom battles (#85): the setup screen has a "Torpedo air strike (our
carrier)" panel - switch our carrier's strike on or off and set the
number of waves (1-8) and of aircraft in a wave (1-24).

Air strikes (#85): our carrier's waves fly also when none of the
player's ships carries torpedo tubes (they come from the carrier); the
torpedo data are then taken from any tube in the battle. Before, the
wave was not sent.
