226. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.42 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.389) - STRIKES COME OVER THEIR OWN FLEET, ROCKING THEIR WINGS - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): every wave starts 8 km behind its own fleet's
farthest ship, seen from the target, so it always comes in over its own
ships - and the aircraft rock their wings over each of them, the
airmen's greeting to the sailors below.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
227. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.43 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.390) - SUPERPOSED GUNS: THE DRAWBACKS FROM THE SOURCES - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Superposed guns (#81): the penalty now follows what the sources record
of the US Kearsarge class, the only ships built with an 8 in turret on
top of the 13 in turret:
- the upper gun cannot train on its own and follows the lower guns'
  aim: its hit chance is 15 % lower (StackedGunsAimPenalty);
- the upper guns' ammunition hoists run through the lower gunhouse:
  the lower gun still reloads 25 % slower per gun above it;
- one hit could silence both: when the lower gun is ruined, the gun
  above it falls silent too (StackedGunsSilence).
The earlier "blast disturbs the crew" reason is dropped - no source
confirms it.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
228. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.44 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.391) - AIR STRIKES: LIVELY FLIGHT, LOOSER FORMATION, FLEET BARRAGE - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85):
- the aircraft no longer fly as if on rails: they bank into turns, bob
  and drift in gusty air and wobble a little in roll, pitch and yaw -
  less so, and quicker, low over the sea (AirStrikeTurbulence, 0 = off);
- looser formation: the aircraft of a section fly 250 m apart, staggered
  back and up, and each drops at its own point along the target - a fan
  of torpedoes instead of one bunch (AirStrikeSpacing);
- the whole defending fleet in range shows its fire: tracers and dark
  shell bursts of the light guns around the aircraft, and white splash
  columns of the heavy guns in the sea ahead of aircraft running in low
  (AirStrikeBarrage). The chance of shooting an aircraft down is
  unchanged.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
229. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.45 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.392) - FIX: CRASH AT THE START OF A BATTLE - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Fix: beta.43 and beta.44 could crash the game as a battle opened (an
error inside the .NET runtime). The cause was the new aim penalty of
superposed guns (#81), hooked into the game's hit chance calculation.
It now goes through the per-gun-type accuracy multiplier the mod
already adjusts: a gun type mounted only on top of other guns (the
Kearsarge case - 8 in on top, 13 in below) has 15 % lower hit chance.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
230. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.46 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.393) - AIR STRIKES: THE SHORT 184 WITH MOVING CONTROLS - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): the torpedo aircraft is now the Short 184 seaplane -
the type that made the first sinking by an air-dropped torpedo
(Dardanelles, August 1915), flown from the seaplane carrier HMS
Ben-my-Chree. Model "Short Admiralty Type 184" by mgg8, CC0, simplified
for the game. Its ailerons, elevator and rudder move with the flight -
the rate of roll, climb and dive, turns and gusts - and the propeller
spins. Custom battles with our carrier: 1915-1925 (was 1917-1925).
The Sopwith Camel stays available (AirStrikeModel = sopwith_camel).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
231. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.47 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.394) - YOUR DESIGNS: SAVED FROM CUSTOM BATTLES, CHOSEN FIRST - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Your designs:
- every ship you finish in the custom battle constructor is saved to
  the shared designs for its nation and year, as with the save button
  (switch #179 CustomBattleSaveDesigns);
- designs you save are noted as yours (UserData\IronAdmiral\
  player_designs.csv). When a nation picks a shared design - in the
  campaign and in custom battles - your designs of its type come first,
  newest first, up to 12 years old (technology permitting in the
  campaign), then the historical classes, then the rest (switch #178
  PlayerDesignsFirst). A nation that builds from a ready design skips
  the ship generator.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
232. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.48 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.395) - SUPERPOSED GUNS: NO MORE FALSE MATCHES - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Superposed guns (#81): casemates in two tiers and light guns (under
4 in) on turret roofs were taken for Kearsarge-style superposed turrets
and penalised. Only turret guns count now, and the upper gun must be of
4 in or more. The log line names each pair, its offset and height.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
233. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.49 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.396) - DESIGNER: SMALLER TEXT IN THE PART WINDOW - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Designer: at a large UI scale (4K) the part window on the right (Mark,
weight, cost, crew, ranges, damage, reload) was drawn in huge letters.
The fix of 1.11.357 changed the font size, but the game's text carries
its own size tags, so nothing changed. The text itself is scaled now:
DesignerPartTextScale, default 70 %.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
234. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.50 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.397) - A SERVICE RECORD FOR EVERY DESIGN - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every design (ship class) keeps a battle record across games: battles,
victories, draws, defeats and ships of the class sunk, from campaign
and custom battles. Each ship that fought adds to its class. The
designer shows the record of the design on the slipway under the top
bar. File: UserData\IronAdmiral\design_stats.csv. Switch #180
DesignStats.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
235. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.51 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.398) - NATIONS PREFER THE DESIGNS WITH THE BEST RECORD - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
When a nation picks a shared design (campaign and custom battles) the
battle records now count:
- order: your own designs, then proven designs (at least 3 battles and
  a score of 0.55 or more), then the historical classes, then the rest;
- within each group the best record goes first, then the newest;
- score = (battles won + half the draws + 1) / (battles + 2), less half
  the share of the class's ships sunk per battle; no record = 0.5.
Switch #181 DesignStatsPreferBest; DesignStatsProvenBattles and
DesignStatsProvenScore set the bar. The log names the record of every
design chosen.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
236. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.52 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.399) - AIR STRIKES: THE TORPEDO AIRCRAFT OF THE YEAR - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): the aircraft now follow the year of the battle
(AirStrikeModel = auto): Short 184 up to 1925, Fairey Swordfish from
1926, Albacore and Swordfish wave by wave in 1940-1942, Swordfish
after. Each type flies at its own speed (Short 184 34 m/s, Swordfish
50, Albacore 55). The carrier is named after the type (Ben-my-Chree,
Courageous, Ark Royal, Formidable). Custom battles with our carrier:
1915-1945. New models, all CC0 by mgg8, simplified, with moving
ailerons, elevators and rudders: Swordfish (its own torpedo, released
at the drop), Albacore, and for shore-based strikes to come Beaufort,
Beaufighter TF.X and Hampden TB.I (two propellers turning). The Short
184 was converted again without the holes in its wings.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
237. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.53 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.400) - CUSTOM BATTLES: HISTORICAL CLASSES NO LONGER THROWN OUT FOR THEIR HULL'S YEAR - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Custom battles: a historical class (or your own design) was rejected
when its hull type was older than the age limit - Japan's Tosa (1920)
for a 1920 battle, because her hull dates from 1910 - and the ship
generator then spent many minutes on failing attempts. The hull's year
now counts only for generated designs; historical classes and your
designs keep their own year.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
238. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.54 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.401) - AIR STRIKES FLOWN AS THEY WERE - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85), tactics and speeds after the sources:
- the waves come in high (2,000 m from 1935 - Taranto: in "above cloud
  cover at 7,500 feet"; 1,200 m 1925-34 and 450 m for the Short 184,
  estimates), glide down from 8 km out, level off 1.5 km before the
  drop and make a straight, level run at wave-top height;
- the drop is made at no more than 100 knots, from 15 m (10 m from
  1935 - Williamson at Taranto: "just 30 feet"), 700 m from the target
  from 1935 ("1,000 yards or less"; Taranto 400-700 yards), 900 m
  before;
- the two halves of a wave attack on both bows, about 45 degrees off
  - the anvil attack, which leaves the ship no turn that does not
  show her side to one of them (AirStrikeAttackAngle, 80 = abeam);
- speeds: Short 184 34 m/s (top 77 kn), Swordfish 45 m/s (pilots
  "rarely topped 100" mph), Albacore 52 m/s; faster in the glide.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
239. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.55 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.402) - AIR STRIKES: THE BARRAGE WITH THE GAME'S OWN EFFECTS - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): the fleet's barrage no longer uses solid shapes -
black spheres for shell bursts and orange boxes for tracers, which fell
into the sea. It now uses the game's own effects: muzzle flash and
smoke at the defending ships' light guns, the game's shell burst in the
air around the aircraft and the game's shell splash in the sea ahead
of aircraft running in low. The aircraft setting has a new name,
AirStrikeAircraft (auto = the type of the battle year), so an older
fixed choice (the Sopwith Camel) no longer overrides it.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
240. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.56 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.403) - CUSTOM BATTLES: NO HULLS FROM AFTER THE BATTLE YEAR FOR THE SHIP GENERATOR - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Custom battles: the ship generator could take a hull unlocked only
later than the battle year - Japan got the 1927 Modern Battleship I
(Yamato-style) hull in a 1920 battle - and then failed attempt after
attempt, about a minute each, because no main tower or funnel of 1920
fits it. Hulls from after the battle year are now left out for the
generator; the log names each one.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
241. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.57 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.404) - AIR STRIKES: TORPEDO ALONG THE AIRCRAFT'S HEADING, VISIBLE TRACERS - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85):
- an aerial torpedo runs the way the aircraft flies: for the last 1.5 km
  the aircraft flies straight at the point where its torpedo will meet
  the ship and drops at the release range when lined up (the torpedoes
  went off 1.9-4.1 km out before, on a course of their own);
- the light guns of the defending ships now show tracers - thin glowing
  streaks from the guns towards the aircraft, ending in the air; the
  game's hit effects are looked up by name for the shell bursts (the log
  lists which were found).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
242. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.58 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.405) - NEW TECHNOLOGY TYPE: NAVAL AVIATION - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Research (#85): a new technology type, Naval Aviation, in the Tactics
tab - 32 technologies from 1912 to 1944: reconnaissance (coastal
seaplane stations, airships, flying boats, long-range patrol, airborne
radar), aircraft on ships (seaplanes, catapult, gunnery spotting),
aerial torpedoes (from the experimental torpedo of 1914 to the drag ring
of 1944), carriers and aircraft (seaplane carrier, flying-off deck, full
flight deck, arresting gear, torpedo aircraft types, the anvil attack,
shore-based torpedo aviation, dive bombing) and the defence against
aircraft (AA guns, splash barrage, AA fire control, air search radar,
light automatic guns, proximity fuze, combat air patrol). The
reconnaissance technologies add to the game's own reconnaissance; the
other effects are being wired to the air strikes and will follow. In a
campaign started earlier the new technologies appear unresearched.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
243. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.59 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.406) - HELP: AIR STRIKES, NAVAL AVIATION, YOUR DESIGNS - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
In-game Help (Iron Admiral - new mechanics): two new chapters - "Air
strikes and naval aviation" (our carrier in custom battles, the aircraft
of the year, how they attack, the defence, aerial warheads, cameras,
the Naval Aviation research) and "Your designs and their service
record" (designs saved from custom battles, your designs first, the
service record, the best record preferred). The settings chapters follow
from chapter 25. Switches #178-181 are in the Ship design tab. The
feature overview lists both. The installer now also copies
techTypes.csv (needed by the Naval Aviation technologies). Log: one
"shell nose" line per ship.

244. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.60 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.407) - AIR RECONNAISSANCE FROM YOUR PORTS - 2026-10-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Fog of war (#85, step 1): the Naval Aviation reconnaissance technologies
send air patrols from a nation's ports. An enemy force within their
range of one of its ports is found more easily - up to +0.30 intelligence
quality, falling off with distance: seaplane stations (1912) about 100
km, airships (1915) 400 km, flying boats (1918) 500 km, long-range
patrol (1930) 900 km, airborne radar (1941) 1000 km. Bad weather grounds
the patrols - in each 800 km sea area 30 % of the months, 50 % in winter,
15 % with radar. The ranges are estimates. The intelligence log line
names the air patrol. Switch #182 NavalAviationRecon.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
245. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.61 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.408) - CAMPAIGN AIR STRIKES FOLLOW RESEARCH - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): in campaign battles an air strike follows the Naval
Aviation research instead of the year. The attacking nation needs a
seaplane carrier and an aerial torpedo; its carrier technologies set the
number of waves and aircraft per wave, the aircraft types set the
aircraft and the attack profile, the aerial torpedo sets the warhead.
The defending ships' anti-aircraft technologies set how well their light
guns shoot, and the splash barrage of heavy guns needs the splash barrage
technology. Custom battles still use the battle year. For now strikes
appear in the campaign only with the air strike test switch.
Fog of war (#85): air reports mistake ship types by patrol type -
airships and seaplanes often, flying boats less, long-range patrol and
radar rarely - unless ports or own forces give a better picture.
Help chapter "Air strikes and naval aviation" updated.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
246. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.62 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.409) - CARRIER IN ALL CUSTOM BATTLES 1915-1945 - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): settings kept from an older beta limited our carrier
in custom battles to 1917-1925, so battles of other years had no air
strike. The old default years are moved to 1915-1945 once.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
247. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.63 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.410) - SPOTTING AIRCRAFT - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Gunnery (#85, step 2): with the Naval Aviation ship catapult technology,
battleships and cruisers launch a spotting aircraft in daylight that
reports the fall of shot - a better hit chance beyond 12 km, in full
from 18 km, and more with the air spotting technology. Custom battles
use the year (catapult from 1922, spotting from 1925). The values are
estimates. Switch #183 NavalAviationSpotting. Help chapter "Air strikes
and naval aviation" updated.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
248. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.64 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.411) - ANTI-AIRCRAFT FIRE FOR EVERY SHIP - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): a ship without guns of 2-3 in did not fire at the
aircraft at all (a 1941 test: 15 torpedo aircraft, none lost). From 1930
secondary guns up to 5.5 in also fire as dual-purpose AA, and from 1937
every ship carries light automatic AA mountings the game does not show
(one per 4000 t, up to 12). In campaign battles the AA technologies
decide this (fire director, 20-40 mm guns). The carrier name now fits
the year from the first message of the battle.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
249. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.65 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.412) - SECONDARY TOWER FOR GENERATED BATTLESHIPS - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ship generator: in 1938 custom battles every attempt on Italian and
German battleship hulls came out without a secondary tower, although
the hull takes several, and the battle never started. After one such
attempt the later attempts of the design get an available secondary
tower on a free mount before the check. Switch #184
ShipGenSecondaryTower.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
250. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.66 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.413) - SECONDARY TOWER PLACED EARLIER - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ship generator: the secondary tower of beta.65 is now put in as the parts
are placed, before the weight reduction, and only on the centre line (a
tower on a side mount unbalanced the ship). When no free mount takes it,
a lighter gun on a centre-line mount gives way. 1938 German battleships:
before this, 13 of 16 attempts found no free mount.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
251. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.67 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.414) - SECONDARY TOWER ON THE DECK - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ship generator: on hulls whose towers stand freely on the deck (not on
mounts - a 1938 test on the Nelson hull found none of 266 mounts taking
a tower), the missing secondary tower is now placed on the centre line
of the deck, aft of the main tower first, where the room allows.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
252. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.68 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.415) - SECONDARY TOWER ON THE DECK (FIX) - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ship generator: the deck search for a missing secondary tower measured
from the wrong origin and never found a place (1938 German battleship,
8 attempts). It now goes along the ship's axis from the main tower, and
the log names the reasons when no place is found.

253. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.69 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.416) - SECONDARY TOWER: ROOM AFT OF THE MAIN TOWER - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ship generator: the game refuses a secondary tower in front of the main
tower, and aft of it every place collided with other parts. The search
now goes aft only, up to 120 m, and lighter guns (not of the main
calibre) in the way give way - at most twice per attempt.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
254. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.70 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.417) - SPOTTING AIRCRAFT YOU CAN SEE - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Battle (#85): the spotting aircraft are now flown. By day each battleship
and cruiser with the catapult fires its aircraft off before the main
battery opens - the trolley run off the side, nose up, a climbing turn -
and it circles on its own side of the target, out of reach of the
target's anti-aircraft fire. At dusk it alights in the wake of its ship
and is hoisted in. The long-range spotting bonus counts only while the
aircraft is up. Switch #185 NavalAviationSpotterPlanes.
Uprisings (#77): the conflict log now writes the raw values the game's
chance formula uses, to settle how the defender's tonnage counts.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
255. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.71 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.418) - TORPEDO AIRCRAFT HOP OVER THEIR TARGET - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): after the drop the torpedo aircraft no longer turn
away at once - they press on low and hop over the ship at about mast
height, then climb away. Air strike screenshots (switch
AirStrikeScreenshots) now also come from the aircraft's own camera: at
the heaviest anti-aircraft fire of the run, 2 and 6 seconds after the
drop (the torpedo's track), over the ship and just after a hit.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
256. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.72 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.419) - FLIGHT PHYSICS OF THE TORPEDO AIRCRAFT - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): the torpedo aircraft fly by a simple aerodynamic model
built on the real aircraft's data - weight, wing area and span, engine
power, top speed and the torpedo's weight (Short 184, Swordfish,
Albacore, Beaufort, Hampden, Beaufighter, Camel). Thrust, parasite and
induced drag and the weight on the climb decide the speed and how
steeply each type can climb; the pilot works the throttle (cruise on the
approach, no faster than about 100 knots on the torpedo run). When the
torpedo leaves the aircraft it is suddenly hundreds of kilograms lighter:
it jumps up, the nose rises and it speeds up.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
257. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.73 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.420) - HOW TO ADD A REPORT ON GITHUB - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Problem reports: the F10 Tools tab, the README inside the report zip and
the help chapter "Treasury panel, reports and settings" now explain step
by step how to add the report (or Latest.log) to an issue on GitHub.
A new button "Open GitHub issues" opens the issues page.

261. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.77 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.424) - LEVEL RUN-IN BEFORE THE DROP - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): an aircraft still gliding down too fast close to the
release point (one drop at 219 km/h in a test) now stops its descent and
flies level with the throttle closed, so the drag takes the speed off
before the torpedo goes. Every torpedo drop is logged with its speed.

262. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.78 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.425) - AIRCRAFT RECONNAISSANCE - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes and spotting aircraft (#85): aircraft in the air reconnoitre.
An enemy ship near a spotting aircraft (up to the visibility, at most
20 km) or a torpedo aircraft (about 10 km) is seen by that side, in
daylight; a shot-down aircraft or one that has lost its ship sees
nothing. New switch #186 NavalAviationAirRecon (on).
The first wave could still drop at up to 260 km/h coming down steeply:
too fast, the glide is now held shallow and level near the sea. The
screenshot of a spotting aircraft leaving the catapult is taken from
over the water - the camera stood inside the superstructure.

263. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.79 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.426) - AIR STRIKES IN THE CAMPAIGN - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Naval aviation (#85): air strikes come to campaign battles. In a daylight
battle with battleships or cruisers each side whose nation has the
seaplane carrier (av_carrier_1) and the aerial torpedo (at_1) researched
sends torpedo aircraft from a carrier beyond the horizon against the
largest enemy ship - both sides, each by its own technologies (aircraft
type and year, waves, aircraft per wave, warhead, the defender's AA).
Battles of light forces and convoys have no air strikes.
The carrier costs a monthly upkeep from the first carrier technology:
3% of the fleet maintenance, +2 points for each further carrier
technology (3/5/7/9%), shown in the expenses breakdown
(NavalAviationUpkeepPercent, NavalAviationUpkeepStep).
New switch #187 NavalAviationCampaignStrikes (on). Test switch
AirStrikeCampaignRulesTest: the campaign rules in a custom battle.

264. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.80 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.429) - SHORE-BASED AIRCRAFT, WEATHER, BATTLE HOUR - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Naval aviation (#85):
- Shore-based torpedo aircraft (new switch #188 NavalAviationShoreStrikes,
  on): a side with the aerial torpedo (at_1) and shore aviation
  (av_recon_1) strikes a daylight battle within reach of its nearest port -
  120/250/400/600/750 km by the shore aviation technologies; the chance is
  70% next to the port, falling to 0 at the edge of the reach. Aircraft:
  Short 184 to 1929, Swordfish 1930-38, Beaufort 1939-41, Hampden 1942,
  Beaufighter TF from 1943; 2 waves of 2-6 aircraft. Also against light
  forces and convoys.
- The carrier is not with every fleet: 15/30/50/65% by carrier technology,
  +20% of that for each capital ship beyond the first (at most 90%).
- Naval aviation upkeep scale from the historical budget shares (Navy
  Estimates, US Navy BuAer share): 1.5/3.5/7/11.5% of the fleet maintenance
  (NavalAviationUpkeepScale).
- Weather: each wave flies only if the weather allows it for its era -
  1915-20 seaplanes to Beaufort 3 and no rain or storm; 1920s to Beaufort 5,
  visibility 5 km; 1930s to Beaufort 6, 4 km; 1940s to Beaufort 9, 3 km.
  Catapult spotting aircraft go up only when they can be recovered by crane
  (to Beaufort 4).
- The spotting aircraft's catapult screenshot is taken just after it
  leaves the catapult.
Battle hour (#57): the faster force (3 kn) chooses the hour when its
reconnaissance is not worse than the enemy's (it had to be 0.15 better -
it almost never happened); a better-informed force still gets the ambush.
Ship generator: switch #184 ShipGenSecondaryTower off until #91 is solved.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
265. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.81 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.431) - BATTLE WEATHER BY CLIMATE, LOST TERRITORY WARS - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Battle weather (new switch #189 BattleClimateWeather, on): the weather and
the wind of a campaign battle come from the climate of its latitude, sea
and season instead of the game's draw - North Sea gales and overcast in
winter, a clear Mediterranean summer, the Southern Ocean, the SW monsoon,
the tropical cyclone seasons (US Navy Marine Climatic Atlas, NOAA pilot
charts, wave and wind climatologies). The naval aviation weather rules
follow from it.
Lost territory (#94): a nation joins a war its rival is already fighting
only with a way to win the territory back - a land road to the province
or, at sea, its fleet with the rival's other enemies at least 1.2 times
the rival's; already at war elsewhere, only by land (reported by hurepoix:
a much weaker nation in recession joined anyway).
Air strikes (#85): fast twin-engined torpedo aircraft (Beaufort and
later) release at up to about 150 kn - the shore-based Beauforts held
their torpedoes and one wave dropped none.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Air strikes (#85): the shore-based Beauforts came down from 2000 m in a
long shallow glide and still dropped at 350 km/h; an aircraft too fast on
the run-in now also lowers its flaps (about 2 m/s off its speed every
second) down to the release speed.

Air strikes in the campaign (#95): the waves and the aircraft per wave
come from the carrier level and the national strike tactics of the period
(Taranto 12+9, Bismarck 9 and 15, Pearl Harbor 40, Coral Sea 15-22, US
torpedo squadrons of 12-15 one after another): seaplane carrier 1 wave of
2; early carrier 1 of 6; fleet carrier 2 of 12 (Japan 24); several
carriers 3 of 15 (USA, Britain), 2 of 40 for Japan's combined strikes;
France at most the early carrier. Shore-based strikes by the shore
aviation level (SM.79 flights of 5, Beaufort squadrons 6-9, Force Z 17+26
in 5 attacks, PQ 18 42): 1 wave of 2, 2 of 4-5, 2 of 9, 3 of 20, 4 of 30
(Germany and Japan 40). Every aircraft is drawn.

270. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.86 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.438) - EVENT ANSWERS NOT MULTIPLIED, JPG SCREENSHOTS, REPORT FOLDER - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Diplomacy (#98, reported by A Simple Tako): refusing the alliance event
(-50 relation) took a relation of 99 straight to war - the mod's
negative attitude multiplier (x4) made it -200. The attitude multipliers
are meant for the small month-to-month swings; a one-off change of 20 or
more (event answers, one-off actions) now keeps its own size
(DiplomacyUnscaledFrom, 20; 0 = all multiplied as before).

Screenshots: every screenshot of the mod also gets a JPG copy
(UserData\IronAdmiral\screens\jpg, about 0.1-2 MB, under GitHub's 10 MB
limit for pictures) - drag it straight into a GitHub comment and it shows
as a picture (switch #192 ScreenshotsJpg, on).

Problem report (F10, Tools): now a folder on the desktop that opens by
itself - Latest.log as a plain file, the newest JPG screenshots (last two
days, up to 6) and the zip with everything else. Each file can be dragged
into a GitHub comment as it is. The how-to lists GitHub's limits:
pictures and videos 10 MB, other files 25 MB.
Wiki: new pages "Naval aviation" (with screenshots) and
"Reporting problems".

271. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.87 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.440) - RELATIVE TECHNOLOGY LEVEL, PANELS HIDE WITH THE CAMERA - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
World tab: the technology level of a nation was measured against the year,
and with the mod's technology years every nation showed as "Advanced". It
is now relative to the other active major nations - researched
technologies against their average (Very advanced / Advanced / Average /
Behind / Very behind) with the place in the ranking, e.g.
"Advanced (2/8)" (switch #193 TechLevelRelative, on).

Battle: the fire settings rows (torpedo setting, torpedo targets, main
guns, secondary, aim) now hide with the order buttons while the camera is
moved (right or middle mouse button, WASD, Q/E, arrows) and come back
0.4 s after it is let go.

Experimental, off by default (#96): battles of aircraft alone - a player's
task force at sea within reach of an enemy's torpedo aviation can be
attacked from the air, a playable battle with only the air waves
(switches #194 AirOnlyBattles, #195 AirOnlyBattleChance,
#196 AirOnlyBattleTest).

272. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.88 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.441) - TURRETS ARMOURED BY DEFAULT - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ship designer: a newly added gun calibre used to start with no turret
armour, which was easy to forget. It now gets a 25 mm roof and walls as
thick as the gun's calibre at once (within the limits the designer allows
for that gun). Casemate guns, loaded and copied designs and values the
player has set are left alone; AI designs are not changed
(switches #197 TurretArmorDefault, #198 TurretArmorDefaultRoofMm).

273. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.89 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.442) - TORPEDO AVOIDANCE ON, LEADER KEPT, SPACING SLIDER - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Battle, the player's divisions:
- start with torpedo avoidance on (the game starts them with it off) -
  a click on the button still turns it off (switch #199
  PlayerAvoidTorpedoes);
- start with the automatic change of the division leader off - the leader
  stays the ship you put at the head (switch #200
  PlayerKeepDivisionLeader);
- new "Spacing" row under the gun orders: a slider for the distance
  between the ships of the selected division, x0.5 (closed up) to x3
  (wide open) of the game's distance; Shift + slider = all your divisions.
  The default for new divisions is switch #201 FormationSpacing (1.0 =
  the game's own).

274. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.90 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.443) - ARMOUR FOR SMALL GUNS - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ship designer: small guns (2-4 in) could not get any turret armour - the
game's limit was 0 and nothing could be typed. Where the game allows none,
the limit is now a light gun house or shield: walls up to 38 mm (1.5 in),
roof up to 25 mm, never more than the calibre (switches #202
SmallGunShieldArmor, #203 SmallGunShieldSideMm, #204 SmallGunShieldTopMm).
The default turret armour of beta.88 no longer goes above the designer's
limit.

275. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.91 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.444) - NEW WAR STARTS AT ZERO, UNREST EXPLAINED - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
War score (reported by hurepoix): a new war between two nations started
with the victory points left over from their previous war (237 000 VP
after one month of a new war, an early peace worth 850 millions). The
points left from the previous war are now taken off when a new war
starts; points won in the new war count as usual (switch #205
WarScoreReset). A war already running in a loaded save is not changed.

Unrest (reported by hurepoix): the monthly economy report now lists the
unrest the mod adds and why - inflation, and the occupation of foreign
core provinces (grant autonomy or give them back to lower it).

276. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.92 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.445) - SHARE SCREENSHOTS AND DESIGNS, AUTHOR'S DESIGNS - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
F10 > Tools > "Share screenshots and designs": a folder on the desktop with
your newest screenshots (JPG, last 7 days, up to 10) and a zip of your own
shared designs (not those that come with the mod, not the generated ones),
and the GitHub page for them opens (pinned issue #101). Nothing is sent by
the mod - look at the files, delete what you do not want to share and drag
the rest into a comment. The best screenshots go to the wiki gallery, the
designs into the mod with your name in CREDITS.txt.

Shared designs: 95 of the author's own designs come with the mod
(IronAdmiralDesigns/Author), installed like the others - existing files are
never overwritten. Designs accepted from the players will come in
IronAdmiralDesigns/Community.

277. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.93 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.446) - FIXES: MAIN BATTERY TARGETS, BOW FLOODING, AERIAL TORPEDOES - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
- Smart targeting (#89): the main battery is the ship's largest gun
  calibre, as in the gun orders - the game's own main group could point at
  a secondary gun, and 432 mm turrets were then switched to a closing
  destroyer with the secondaries.
- Damage (#93): the way through the water for bow and stern flooding was
  scaled by the time acceleration (64-110 kn at x5); the same hit now
  floods the same at x1 and x5.
- Aerial torpedoes (#90, #96): a torpedo whose data come from a ship of
  the target's own side hit with no damage; such a hit now counts.
- Climate (#100): no error when the custom battle result window opens.

278. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.94 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.451) - DISTANCE TO UNIDENTIFIED CONTACTS, SMALL GUN ARMOUR, FASTER DESIGN LOADING - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Fog of war: the label of an unidentified or probable contact now shows its
distance, e.g. "Unidentified ship (large), 33 km" (to 0.5 km below 10 km).

Ship designer: the armour of small guns (2-4 in) could still not be set in
beta.90-93 - the value is now written when the game keeps it at 0 (walls
up to 38 mm, roof up to 25 mm, never more than the calibre).

Leaving the designer in the campaign: the export of your designs to the
shared designs serialised every design once per great power before
checking whether its file already existed - now once per design (part of
the long wait after saving a design; the time is logged when over 1 s).
The check of the shared designs against the game data (at every load of
the design list - game start and every saved design) skips designs found
clean before (remembered in UserData\IronAdmiral\designs_clean.txt).
Shared designs saved for a nation that may never use their hull are moved
out of the design folder to Designs_unbuildable_<date> (never deleted) and
not installed again (switch #206 MoveUnbuildableDesigns).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Climate (#102): every campaign battle was placed in the Arctic - the map
position was read the wrong way. The latitude now comes from the map's
projection and the longitude from the right coordinate (a North Sea
battle: 58 N 3 E), so the weather follows the real sea and season.

small guns, torpedo avoidance on and leader change off for the player's
divisions, the new war starting at zero, moving unbuildable designs out,

283. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.99 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.456) - SHIPS SUNK, WITH NAMES - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every ship sunk in a battle (campaign or custom) is recorded in
UserData\IronAdmiral\sinkings.csv: date, the ship with her class, type,
tonnage and nation, and the ship that sank her - the one that did her the
most damage in the battle - with her class and the weapon (gunfire of the
calibre, torpedo, aerial torpedo). In the designer the "Design ranking"
table shows the kills of every design, and "Ships sunk" lists the latest
20 sinkings.

284. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.100 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.458) - SHIP SERVICE RECORDS, SPACING SLIDER, SINGLE SHIPS EVADE TORPEDOES - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Statistics per ship: every ship keeps a service record (UserData\IronAdmiral\
ship_stats.csv) - battles, won / drawn / lost, damage dealt, sunk or not -
and her kills with their tonnage (sinkings.csv now records the sinking
ship too). In the designer's ranking panel the new "Ships" list shows the
best ships: kills, tonnage sunk, damage dealt.

Battle: the "Spacing" slider had no effect - it now changes the distance
the game keeps between the ships of the division (logged when used).

Torpedo avoidance never worked for a division of one ship - the game
refuses it there. Such ships of the player now use the mod's own evasion
(the one of the AI and the division orders): a sighted track that would
hit is combed in time, then the ship takes up her course again.
Climate: the Arctic begins at the Arctic Circle (the Norwegian Sea off
Trondheim is no longer Arctic).

World tab: the technology label also shows the number of researched
technologies, e.g. "Very advanced (2/13, 418 tech.)"; the whole ranking
is in the log.

285. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.101 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.459) - PENETRATION REFERENCE FOR THE WIKI - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every battle writes the physical penetration of the guns in it - side and
deck armour, 1 to 30 km, the same values the battle and the designer's gun
window use - to UserData\IronAdmiral\penetration_reference.csv. The new
wiki page "Penetration table" is made from it and updated after the tests
(switch #210 PenetrationReference).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
286. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.102 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.460) - PENETRATION MEASURED IN BATTLE - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Besides the theoretical values per gun, every shell that hits armour adds
to a running average (UserData\IronAdmiral\penetration_hits.csv) by
calibre, gun mark, decade and range band: hits, average penetration at the
hit with its real striking velocity, angle of fall and plate angle,
average plate met, share of plates pierced. The wiki's penetration table
shows both, averaged over every recorded battle.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
287. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.103 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.461) - PENETRATION TABLE BY SHELL - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The penetration reference keeps every gun with its shell apart - shell
weight, shell quality (construction: plain AP, capped, improved capped),
drag (nose shape) and muzzle velocity (propellant and barrel technologies) -
and the hits measured in battle are grouped by shell weight and quality as
well as calibre, decade and range.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
288. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.104 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.463) - SPECTACULAR SINKINGS - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Battleships, battlecruisers and heavy cruisers sink the way they did:
- torpedoed on one side (two or more hits more on one beam), they list to
  that side and roll over, keel up (Barham, Royal Oak);
- after a magazine hit (or by chance) they blow up - flash fires in the
  gun houses one after another, main turrets thrown off, a bow-first
  plunge (Hood, Queen Mary);
- otherwise they break their back (explosions amidships and a steep
  plunge) or settle burning;
- always: fires spreading along the hull and secondary explosions.
A real break of the hull in two pieces needs a split ship model, which the
game does not have; the plunge and the explosions amidships stand for it.
Switch #211 SpectacularSinking.

Diagnostics (off): #212 SlowCallProbe times the game's design, save and
refit methods and logs every call over 300 ms - to find what makes saving
a design in the campaign take 20-25 s.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
289. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.105 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.464) - REFIT WITHOUT A QUESTION - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
- Refit: the Refit button in the dockyard enters refit mode at once,
  without TweaksAndFixes' "Are you sure you want to enter Refit Mode?"
  (entering refit changes nothing until the refit is saved). Other
  confirmations (delete, scrap, automatic refit) stay. Switch #213
  SkipRefitConfirm (on; takes effect after a restart of the game).
- Settings panel (F10): switches #194-#213 were shown under "Other"
  without numbers; they are back in their tabs with their numbers. With
  this version they are put back to their defaults once, like the others.
- Diagnostics: #212 SlowCallProbe (off) now starts the moment it is
  switched on in F10 - before, it was read before the saved settings and
  never started.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
290. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.106 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.465) - SLOW SAVE PROBE ON - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
- Diagnostics: #212 SlowCallProbe is now on by default. It times the
  game's design, save, refit and ship list methods and writes every call
  over 300 ms to Latest.log - to find what makes saving a design, loading
  the campaign and entering refit take 20 s or more. If saving a design or
  a refit takes long for you, please attach Latest.log to a problem report
  (F10 > Info & updates > Report a problem).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
291. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.107 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.466) - FIX: CRASH AT START IN BETA.106 - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
- Beta.106 crashed the game a few seconds after start: the slow call
  probe (#212), switched on by default there, timed 152 game methods,
  among them methods run every frame. It is off again and, when switched
  on, times only the methods that save designs and start refits. Its
  setting has a new name (SlowCallProbeV2), so a value saved by beta.106
  does not switch it back on. Please update if you have beta.106.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
292. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.108 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.467) - NAVAL AVIATION BUDGET - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Naval aviation now has a budget of its own (switch #214
NavalAviationBudget, on):
- a slider under the treasury panel (F8) sets it from 0 to 200% of the
  historical upkeep of the naval air arm (100% = the cost so far; it
  appears once you have a carrier or shore aviation technology);
- the aircraft strength follows the budget by at most 10 points a month
  (#215) - aircraft and crews take time to buy and train, and a starved
  air arm wears out slowly;
- the strength sets the size of the strikes: aircraft per wave x the
  strength, fewer waves below 100%, a lower chance of shore strikes and
  weaker air reconnaissance; below 10% there are no aircraft to send;
- every aircraft shot down in a campaign battle is lost from the strength
  (#216);
- the AI spends by doctrine (#217): Japan 150%, USA 130%, Britain 100%,
  Italy 90%, France 80%, Germany 70%, the rest 80%.
The budget and the strength are saved with the campaign's economy state.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
293. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.109 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.470) - AVIATION SLIDER IN THE FINANCES WINDOW - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
- The naval aviation budget (#214) is now a fourth slider in the game's
  Finances window, next to Tech Budget: 0-200% in steps of 10, with its
  monthly cost and the aircraft strength; the expense list has a
  "Naval Aviation" line. The F8 treasury panel shows the budget only.
- Designer: the gun pop-up penetration table showed garbled characters
  instead of the degree sign of the angle of fall - fixed.
- Other mods check: ChonkE's CV Barebone Modification is recognised (a
  second air strike system and armour generation rules that replace
  TweaksAndFixes'); not tested together. Real aircraft carriers as
  floating air bases are planned in issue #104, building on ChonkE's
  work (MIT, credited in NOTICE and README).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
294. IRON ADMIRAL 1.2.76-BETA.110 (DIP_GG_FIXES 1.11.471) - FIX: DESIGNER PART MENU - 2026-10-30
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
- Designer (campaign, new designs and refits): after about half a minute
  the bottom menu stopped responding - neither a part group nor a part
  could be chosen. The check for ship parts left on the campaign map
  (#MapGhostParts) ran in the designer too and hid the part previews of
  that menu. It now leaves the designer alone and never touches the
  previews; previews it hid before are shown again.
